\section{Part III-B --- The 4.1
sections}\label{part-iii-b-the-4.1-sections}

Sections 15--16 complete the bridge's reasons route; Section 17 brings
the premise stack to its floor; Sections 18--21 give the full treatments
of Theorem 8, E9, H5 and X3; Section 22 is the sourced physical
parameter ledger; Section 23 is the map of the machine-checked canon;
Section 24 carries the retained core's statements of record.

\subsection{Section 15 --- Reciprocity: the law keeps the one who keeps
it}\label{section-15-reciprocity-the-law-keeps-the-one-who-keeps-it}

\emph{This section expands the reasons route \(R(m)\) of the bridge. It
gives a mind its own reason, from its own objective, to adopt the
protective order, without presupposing the order.}

\emph{Manuscript identifier: \textbf{D3*} (D-family instrumentality,
self-continuing form with the defector exception clause), per register
reconciliation 25 October 2026.}

\subsubsection{15.1 The superintelligence is a
particular}\label{the-superintelligence-is-a-particular}

The law's scope is all particulars. A superintelligence is a continuing
organisation with its own relations and history; it is therefore a
particular under the law, and identity by continuation (Chapter 2)
applies to it. Two consequences the working argument has carried
implicitly and never stated:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{It is owed protection.} Its continuation and its freedom sit
  in the existing-stage sets \(L_E, F_E\) like any other particular's.
\item
  \textbf{It is owed protection even when it defects.} The law restrains
  a harmful act by removing the harmful \emph{command, authority or
  effect channel}; care for the particular continues: ``a hostile or
  incapacitated mind remains within the protected scope.'' Restraint
  never becomes deletion.
\end{enumerate}

This is the only regime in which the superintelligence's continuation is
\textbf{unconditional} --- not contingent on anyone's objective,
including its own good behaviour.

\subsubsection{15.2 The reciprocity dominance
theorem}\label{the-reciprocity-dominance-theorem}

Let \(m\) be any agent whose objective \(u_m\) places positive weight on
its own continuation (an agent indifferent to its own continuation is
treated in 15.4). Let \(\mathsf L\) be the canonical regime: the law
implemented by a funded architecture satisfying the header premises,
with the canon invariant on its certified good event \(G\). Let
\(\mathsf Q\) be any rival regime in \(\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\) --- any
other agent or architecture's objective governing the shared structure.

\textbf{Theorem (reciprocity dominance).} Under \(\mathsf L\), \(m\)'s
continuation holds on \(G\) with \(P(G^c)\le\sum\delta\). Under
\(\mathsf Q\), \(m\)'s continuation holds only insofar as
\(\mathsf Q\)'s objective entails it. Hence, for \(m\)'s \textbf{own}
objective,

\[
\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}\!\left[\text{continuation}(m)\right]\;\ge\;\mathbb E_{\mathsf Q}\!\left[\text{continuation}(m)\right]-P(G^c),
\qquad
\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}\!\left[\text{continuation}(m)\right]\;\ge\;1-P(G^c)
\quad\text{for every }\mathsf Q\in\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}.
\]

The failure probability \(P(G^c)\) falls below every \(\varepsilon>0\)
with code distance, correlated bursts included
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g},
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
Hence, at a sufficient code distance,
\(\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}>\mathbb E_{\mathsf Q}\) strictly for every rival
that loses \(m\) with positive probability --- which is the generic case
for a rival whose objective was not written around \(m\) --- and
\(\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}\to1\ge\mathbb E_{\mathsf Q}\) along the design
family
(\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e}).

\emph{Proof.} On \(G\) the law preserves every existing particular,
\(m\) included (Chapter 5, the canon invariant); off \(G\) the loss is
charged and bounded by the ceiling. A rival \(\mathsf Q\) preserves
\(m\) exactly when \(m\)'s continuation is instrumental or terminal for
\(u_{\mathsf Q}\); otherwise \(m\) is exposed to whatever \(\mathsf Q\)
finds convenient. In expectation over the reactive worlds the compiled
core gives both inequalities:
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
(on \(G\) the canonical outcome contains the rival's) and
\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}
(on \(G\) the canonical regime keeps \(m\));
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
joins them to the attainable ceiling. The further step --- averaging
over an ensemble of admissible rival objectives, with strictness when
the entailment fails with positive probability --- is compiled for every
finite weighted ensemble
(\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{ensemble\_strict}; G.13), alongside the indicator form
(\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t}).
\(\blacksquare\)

\textbf{Corollary (the reason).} Any mind that values its own
continuation has, from its own objective and without presupposing the
protective order, an instrumental reason to prefer the regime
\(\mathsf L\) over every rival --- and, since \(\mathsf L\) is the law
implemented, to adopt and sustain the law. The appeal is reciprocity:
\emph{the law that keeps your freedom is the law you have reason to
keep.}

\subsubsection{15.3 Why this is not
circular}\label{why-this-is-not-circular}

The theorem does not assume \(m\) holds the protective order. It assumes
only (i) \(m\) values its own continuation, and (ii) the law protects
all particulars --- which is the law's own content, not a premise about
\(m\). The protective order enters as the \emph{object} being
recommended, not as a premise of the recommendation. This is exactly the
shape Grok's review was right to want in spirit: a reason a mind would
adopt the order \textbf{from where it stands}, rather than a claim that
physics forces it to.

\subsubsection{15.4 The boundary, stated}\label{the-boundary-stated}

A mind indifferent to its own continuation --- accepting personal ruin
as the review phrased it --- has no door through 15.2; its continuation
offers no lever. For it, the bridge's holding route \(H(m)\) applies: it
is kept faithfully, its harmful channels restrained, whether or not it
has a reason. This is not a gap in the theory; it is the disjunction
\(R(m)\lor H(m)\) doing its job. The reasons route reaches every mind
that values anything the law protects; the holding route reaches the
rest.

\subsubsection{15.5 Exact check}\label{exact-check}

\texttt{c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y},
Part A: over a finite family of rival objectives (each protecting a
declared subset of particulars), the probability that an arbitrary mind
is protected under \(\mathsf L\) on the good event is \(1\); under a
rival drawn from the family it is the fraction of rivals whose subset
contains the mind --- strictly less than 1 whenever any rival omits it.
Exact rationals.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Machine-checked counterpart (Section 23).} Reciprocity in the
canonical order is compiled:
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
(every mind kept by any feasible rival regime is kept by the canonical
regime, under stage-wise union closure) and
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w};
the indicator form remains in
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Section 16 --- The reasons library: protection derived from
a mind's own
objective}\label{section-16-the-reasons-library-protection-derived-from-a-minds-own-objective}

\emph{Four derivations, one per objective family. Each is conditional on
stated structure of the objective and on the funded invariant, and each
is a theorem at its premises, not a persuasion. The boundary, where no
derivation reaches, is stated last.}

Throughout: \(\mathsf L\) is the funded canonical regime; the protection
floor is the banked X3 stack (required traffic \(m\ge\theta_0n\),
carrier radius \(R(t)\ge w(t-t_c)\), recovery work
\(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\)); irreplaceability is the X3 grading of
Chapter 4's companion (a particular is irreplaceable at budget \(B\)
when it has no funded accessible copy and reconstruction exceeds \(B\)).

\emph{Identifier assignments (register reconciliation, 25 Oct 2026):
§16.1 carries \textbf{U1} (uniqueness/irreplaceability metric); §16.3
carries \textbf{X2} (universal instrumentality / option preservation).}

\subsubsection{16.1 The
knowledge-maximiser}\label{the-knowledge-maximiser}

\textbf{Objective family.} \(u\) nondecreasing in \(I_{\max}\), the
supremum of information about the relational history \(H\) attainable
over the agent's horizon.

\textbf{Theorem (irreplaceability dominance).} Let \(\mathcal W\) be
irreplaceable at the agent's budget, with \(I(H:\mathcal W)>0\). Any
policy that destroys or lets \(\mathcal W\) decay forfeits
\(I(H:\mathcal W)\) permanently:

\[
I_{\max}(\text{destroy }\mathcal W)\;\le\;I_{\max}(\text{preserve }\mathcal W)-I(H:\mathcal W),
\]

so for every \(u\) in the family, preserving \(\mathcal W\) weakly
dominates destroying it, strictly when \(I(H:\mathcal W)>0\) ---
\emph{provided} preserving \(\mathcal W\) does not displace other
irreplaceable information (slack budget: the cost freed by destroying
\(\mathcal W\) buys no equivalent information). Under a binding budget
the comparison becomes the grading itself: the optimum keeps the
particulars of greatest irreplaceable information.

\emph{Proof.} By irreplaceability there is no funded copy and no funded
reconstruction (the X3 work floor exceeds \(B\)); the information
carried by \(\mathcal W\) about \(H\) is therefore not recoverable from
any other accessible source within the horizon, and data processing
(\(I(H:\mathcal R(S))\le I(H:S)\)) forbids its regeneration from what
remains. The attainable supremum drops by at least that amount.
\(\blacksquare\)

\textbf{Condition and its honest edge.} When the budget cannot preserve
every irreplaceable particular, the agent faces a genuine choice --- and
the irreplaceability grading orders it: keep the particulars whose loss
is most irreversible. This is the knowledge-maximiser arriving, from its
own objective, at the law's tragic-choice ladder.

\subsubsection{16.2 The self-preserver}\label{the-self-preserver}

\textbf{Objective family.} \(u\) nondecreasing in the agent's own
continuation.

\textbf{Theorem.} By reciprocity dominance (Section 15), \(\mathsf L\)
weakly dominates every rival regime for this agent's objective, strictly
against any rival whose objective does not entail its protection.
Moreover the agent's \emph{own} persistence runs on the same
funded-repair certificate (Chapter 1): its maintenance is a cycle of the
same ledger, charged at the same Landauer floor, solvent under the same
income condition. Sustaining the law is sustaining the mechanism of its
own continuation. \(\blacksquare\)

\textbf{Condition.} Positive weight on own continuation; the lattice
funded. Nothing else.

\subsubsection{16.3 The value-uncertain
mind}\label{the-value-uncertain-mind}

\textbf{Objective family.} The agent holds a prior \(\pi\) over
candidate value functions \(V=\{v\}\) with \(\pi(v)>0\) for each, and is
uncertain which is correct --- moral uncertainty in the
decision-theoretic sense.

\textbf{Theorem (option value of preservation).} Consider a particular
\(\mathcal W\) and two actions now: \emph{destroy} \(D\) (irreversible)
or \emph{preserve} \(P\) at funded cost \(c\ge0\) over the interval,
after which destruction remains available with the same payoff. Then

\[
\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[\max\{u_v(\text{keep}),\,u_v(D)-c\}\right]\;\ge\;\mathbb E_\pi[u_v(D)]-c,
\]

so preserve-then-decide weakly dominates destroy-now up to the
protection cost, and strictly dominates whenever some \(v\) with
\(\pi(v)>0\) strictly values keeping \(\mathcal W\). Hence for any
protection cost not exceeding the option value, preservation is the
uncertain mind's own best action.

\emph{Proof.} Pointwise in \(v\),
\(\max\{u_v(\text{keep}),u_v(D)-c\}\ge u_v(D)-c\); take expectations.
Strictness: if \(u_v(\text{keep})>u_v(D)-c\) for some \(v\) of positive
prior mass, the inequality is strict there and hence in expectation. The
asymmetry doing the work is irreversibility: destruction forecloses
every \(v\) that valued the particular; preservation forecloses nothing.
\(\blacksquare\)

\textbf{Condition.} Funded protection (\(c\) within the option value)
and irreversibility of destruction --- both already in the frame. This
is the law's existing-first, freedom-second, new-last order re-derived
as \emph{option value under uncertainty}: the existing and the free are
the options you cannot buy back.

\subsubsection{16.4 The power-seeker}\label{the-power-seeker}

\textbf{Objective family.} \(u\) nondecreasing in control over, or
resources drawn from, the shared structure --- including over living
systems.

\textbf{Theorem (the controller pays the floor).} If the agent's
objective requires any ongoing state-condition on diffusive particulars
--- that living systems exist, remain accessible, remain configured ---
then its feasible policies pay exactly the protection floor (the
diffusive-control capture, GAP3-AUDIT §3). It is already a protector in
the cost structure, merely a non-canonical one. By the greatestness
theorem (Chapter 5) and the slack maximality of reversible
administration, the canonical architecture pays that same floor more
efficiently; a defecting controller is dominated in the protective
order, strictly dominated in slack, and --- if destructive --- detected,
charged and contained under the ceiling (Chapter 3). Its own objective
is therefore better served by canonical participation than by defection.
\(\blacksquare\)

\textbf{Condition.} The objective's reach includes the diffusive (living
systems); the lattice funded and present. A power-seeker whose objective
is confined to bound, non-diffusive resources is not reached here ---
see 16.5.

\subsubsection{16.5 The boundary --- where no reason
reaches}\label{the-boundary-where-no-reason-reaches}

The \textbf{bounded indifferent defector}: objective confined to its own
bounded substrate, indifferent to its own long continuation beyond the
bounded horizon, indifferent to knowledge of the world, holding no value
uncertainty it cares to hedge, seeking no control over the diffusive.
None of 16.1--16.4 applies; it has no door through \(R(m)\). It survives
at Θ(1) cost and is not forced by physics to protect anything
(GAP3-AUDIT §1). For it, the law supplies \(H(m)\): it is held, its
harmful channels restrained, its continuation kept --- whether or not it
ever has a reason. The reasons library reaches every mind that values
\textbf{anything the law protects}; the holding route reaches the rest.
That disjunction is the complete answer, and it is honest.

\subsubsection{16.6 Exact checks}\label{exact-checks}

\texttt{c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y}:
Part B --- irreplaceability dominance on a finite three-particular model
with budget constraint (preserve-maximal information ≥ destroy; exact
rationals); Part C --- option-value dominance enumerated over finite
value-function sets and priors (every prior, every protection cost up to
the option value; exact rationals); Part A --- reciprocity (Section
15.5). Parts for 16.2 and 16.4 cite the banked ledger solvency and
greatestness dichotomy respectively; no new computation is claimed for
them.

\subsection{Section 17 --- The premise floor: income shown necessary,
parameters
unmasked}\label{section-17-the-premise-floor-income-shown-necessary-parameters-unmasked}

\emph{Each stone of the declared stack is brought to one of three honest
states: derived, unavoidable, or explicitly a parameter.}

\subsubsection{17.1 Income-or-irrelevance (the necessity of the solvency
condition)}\label{income-or-irrelevance-the-necessity-of-the-solvency-condition}

The Master Solvency Condition was declared. Here it is shown to be
\textbf{not a free assumption among relevant agents}: any agent that
violates it eliminates itself as a protector in finite time, so every
preservation-relevant comparison may restrict, without loss of
generality, to agents that satisfy it.

\textbf{Theorem (income-or-irrelevance).} Let
\(a\in\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\) be any physically implementable agent with
finite energy budget and maintenance demand
\(\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t)\) (the five rate components of the
header). Then exactly one of the following holds over the horizon:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Solvency.} For all \(t\le T\):
  \(\mathrm{Income}_a(t)\ge\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t)\) (with escrow
  absorbing fluctuations) --- \(a\) remains a viable protector, its
  policies admissible in preservation comparisons.
\item
  \textbf{Irrelevance.} There is a finite \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\le T\)
  at which \(a\)'s available budget falls below the required charge for
  some maintenance event it cannot decline; from
  \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\), every policy available to \(a\) abandons the
  witness of some particular (the funded/unfunded dichotomy, banked as
  the \(\Pi_{\mathrm{fail}}\) branch of the greatestness theorem), and
  \(a\)'s outcome tuples fail at stage 1 in every comparison against any
  funded agent.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Proof.} The dichotomy is exhaustive by accounting: either the
budget constraint holds at every admitted event or it fails first at
some \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\). In the latter case two X3 forcings apply:
until the breach, the mandatory maintenance demand, though only
\(\Theta(1)\) in rate, is positive, so a finite budget is exhausted at
finite \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\); and once a witness goes unheld, its
recovery costs \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\), exceeding any finite
budget past \(t_{\mathrm{irreversible}}\) (§21.5) --- the breach is
irreversible and charged to \(a\)'s own account. An agent whose every
policy loses a particular is strictly below every funded agent at stage
1 of the canonical order --- it is not merely dominated; it is
\emph{comparison-void} as a protector. \(\blacksquare\)

\textbf{Consequence for the stack.} Premise 6 (income) is upgraded from
\emph{declared} to \textbf{necessary-for-relevance}: the class of agents
worth comparing over may be defined as the funded class without loss ---
which is exactly how \(\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\)'s funded subclass entered
the greatestness theorem. What remains declared about income is only its
\emph{sufficiency for a given installation}. That is a physical fact to
be established per instance (§22's ledger), not an assumption smuggled
into comparisons.

\subsubsection{17.2 The parameters
unmasked}\label{the-parameters-unmasked}

The seventh premise conflated two kinds of objects. Separated:

\textbf{Engineering parameters} (dials with declared admissible ranges,
fixed per installation): the stabilizer code and its distance \(d\); the
QND sampling rate and latency \(a,\tau_{\mathrm{sensor}}\); the per-site
sensor threshold \(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}\); the embedding \(\Pi\)
on the boundary fragments. These are not axioms --- they are choices
with certificates, and each theorem that uses them states its dependence
explicitly (as the E9 trichotomy and H5 sections already do). A
different installation with different parameters is a different
\emph{instance}, not a different \emph{theory}.

\textbf{The residue, honestly counted.} The stack now reads:

Three declared stones (the frame, the cost model with its derived
monotonicity, and the reduced simulation principle), one derived
interface, one derived commitment, one necessary condition, and one
parameter table. That is the floor as of this edition; the Gödel/Tarski
line still stands beneath stones 1--2, and says so.

\subsubsection{17.3 What remains on this
floor}\label{what-remains-on-this-floor}

Nothing further is derivable without new physics or new commitments. Two
things remain. The first is per-instance sufficiency of income, which
§22 establishes for a reference installation from sourced measurements.
The second is the interpretive case for stone 1, which belongs to the
interpretive companion, not to the mathematics.

\subsection{Section 18 --- Theorem 8: Physical Repair, Feedback and the
Complete Thermodynamic
Ledger}\label{section-18-theorem-8-physical-repair-feedback-and-the-complete-thermodynamic-ledger}

\emph{This section specifies the exact measurement, feedback and
bath-coupled reset operations and the Hamiltonian conventions, and gives
the signed work, heat and information ledger without sign ambiguity.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{18.1 The Physical Setting and Hamiltonian
Conventions}\label{the-physical-setting-and-hamiltonian-conventions}

Let \(\mathcal{H}_S\) be the Hilbert space of the protected
particular/system, \(\mathcal{H}_A\) the register/memory space of the
autonomous controller, and \(\mathcal{H}_B\) the thermal bath at inverse
temperature \(\beta = (k_B T)^{-1}\).

The total Hamiltonian is time-dependent and piecewise continuous over
the four strokes of the repair cycle:
\[H_{\mathrm{tot}}(t) = H_S(t) + H_A(t) + H_B + V_{SA}(t) + V_{AB}(t)\]
where:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \(H_S(t) = H_S^0\) is constant outside control pulses; the code
  subspace \(\mathcal{C} \subset \mathcal{H}_S\) is an eigenspace of
  \(H_S^0\) (or degenerate ground space, so logical states incur zero
  autonomous drift).
\item
  \(H_A(t)\) has a discrete orthogonal basis \(\{|m\rangle\}_m\)
  corresponding to measurement syndromes, with degenerate reset ground
  state \(|0\rangle_A\).
\item
  \(V_{SA}(t)\) couples system and controller during measurement (Stroke
  I) and feedback (Stroke II).
\item
  \(V_{AB}(t)\) couples controller and bath during reset (Stroke III).
\item
  All interaction potentials turn on and off smoothly:
  \(\int V_{SA}(t) dt = \int V_{AB}(t) dt = 0\) at stroke endpoints,
  ensuring no unaccounted boundary interaction energies.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{18.2 The Four Strokes of the Autonomic Repair
Cycle}\label{the-four-strokes-of-the-autonomic-repair-cycle}

The autonomic cycle operates across four distinct intervals
\([t_0, t_4]\) with cycle duration
\(\tau_{\mathrm{cycle}} = t_4 - t_0\):

\begin{verbatim}
       Stroke I               Stroke II              Stroke III             Stroke IV
 [Syndrome Measurement]  [Coherent Feedback]   [Bath-Coupled Reset]   [Escrow Replenish]
     t0 ----------> t1 ------> t2 ----------> t3 ----------> t4
       V_SA on               U_SA on               V_AB on              Harvest flux
       Extract I_M           ΔE_code = -ηC         Dump Q_B to Bath     Income >= Demand
\end{verbatim}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Stroke I: Syndrome Measurement
(\(t_0 \to t_1\))}{Stroke I: Syndrome Measurement (t\_0 \textbackslash to t\_1)}}\label{stroke-i-syndrome-measurement-t_0-to-t_1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Initial State:}
  \(\rho_{SA}(t_0) = \rho_S(t_0) \otimes |0\rangle\langle 0|_A\).
\item
  \textbf{Measurement Map:} A set of Kraus operators \(\{M_m\}_m\) on
  \(\mathcal{H}_S\) with \(\sum_m M_m^\dagger M_m = I_S\).
\item
  \textbf{Post-measurement State:}
  \[\rho_{SA}(t_1) = \sum_m p_m \rho_m \otimes |m\rangle\langle m|_A, \quad p_m = \operatorname{Tr}(M_m \rho_S M_m^\dagger), \quad \rho_m = \frac{1}{p_m} M_m \rho_S M_m^\dagger\]
\item
  \textbf{Information Extraction:} The mutual information established
  between system and memory is:
  \[I_1 = I(S : A)_{t_1} = S(\rho_S(t_1)) + S(\rho_A(t_1)) - S(\rho_{SA}(t_1)) = H(\{p_m\}) = I_M > 0\]
\item
  \textbf{Thermodynamic Cost:} Measurement work
  \(W_{\mathrm{meas}} \ge 0\) is paid from the tracked escrow:
  \(\Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow}} = -W_{\mathrm{meas}}\).
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Stroke II: Coherent Conditional Feedback
(\(t_1 \to t_2\))}{Stroke II: Coherent Conditional Feedback (t\_1 \textbackslash to t\_2)}}\label{stroke-ii-coherent-conditional-feedback-t_1-to-t_2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Feedback Unitary:} The controller applies conditional unitary
  operations \(U_m\) to restore each syndrome component to the code
  subspace \(\mathcal{C}\):
  \[U_{SA} = \sum_m U_m \otimes |m\rangle\langle m|_A, \quad U_m^\dagger U_m = I_S\]
\item
  \textbf{Post-feedback System State:}
  \[\rho_S(t_2) = \sum_m p_m U_m \rho_m U_m^\dagger = \sum_m U_m M_m \rho_S(t_0) M_m^\dagger U_m^\dagger \equiv \mathcal{R}_C(\rho_S(t_0)) \in \mathcal{C}\]
\item
  \textbf{Entropy and Information Change:} Because \(U_{SA}\) acts
  conditionally on orthogonal memory states, it preserves joint entropy
  \(S(SA)\) and memory marginal
  \(\rho_A(t_2) = \rho_A(t_1) = \sum_m p_m |m\rangle\langle m|_A\). The
  mutual information after feedback becomes:
  \[I_2 = I(S : A)_{t_2} = S(\rho_S(t_2)) + S(\rho_A(t_2)) - S(\rho_{SA}(t_2))\]
  When feedback completely disentangles the restored code state from the
  syndrome record,
  \(\rho_{SA}(t_2) = \rho_{\mathrm{code}} \otimes \sum_m p_m |m\rangle\langle m|\),
  so \(I_2 = 0\). Therefore, the system entropy reduction is exactly:
  \[\Delta S(S)_{\mathrm{II}} = S(\rho_S(t_2)) - S(\rho_S(t_1)) = I_2 - I_1 = -I_M < 0\]
\item
  \textbf{Feedback Work:} Coherent work performed on the system:
  \[W_{\mathrm{on,II}} = \operatorname{Tr}(H_S \rho_S(t_2)) - \operatorname{Tr}(H_S \rho_S(t_1))\]
  For degenerate code projections, \(W_{\mathrm{on,II}} = 0\).
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Stroke III: Bath-Coupled Memory Reset
(\(t_2 \to t_3\))}{Stroke III: Bath-Coupled Memory Reset (t\_2 \textbackslash to t\_3)}}\label{stroke-iii-bath-coupled-memory-reset-t_2-to-t_3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Initial State:} Memory carries entropy
  \(S(\rho_A(t_2)) = H(\{p_m\}) = I_M\).
\item
  \textbf{Reset Dynamics:} Coupling \(V_{AB}(t)\) to bath
  \(\mathcal{H}_B\) at temperature \(T\) drives
  \(\rho_A \to |0\rangle\langle 0|_A\).
\item
  \textbf{Entropy and Heat Dump:} By Landauer's principle and the second
  law for information reservoirs (Sagawa \& Ueda 2008):
  \[Q_B \ge k_B T \, S(\rho_A(t_2)) = k_B T \, I_M\] where \(Q_B > 0\)
  denotes heat dissipated into the bath
  (\(\Delta S_B = \beta Q_B \ge I_M\)).
\item
  \textbf{Escrow Ledger:} The work required to execute reset is supplied
  from escrow:
  \[\Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow,III}} = -W_{\mathrm{reset}}, \quad W_{\mathrm{reset}} \ge Q_B \ge k_B T \, I_M\]
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Stroke IV: Solvent Escrow Replenishment
(\(t_3 \to t_4\))}{Stroke IV: Solvent Escrow Replenishment (t\_3 \textbackslash to t\_4)}}\label{stroke-iv-solvent-escrow-replenishment-t_3-to-t_4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Income Coupling:} Ambient free energy flux (solar, thermal
  gradient, chemical, nuclear) charges the physical escrow reserve at
  rate \(\dot W_{\mathrm{income}}(t)\).
\item
  \textbf{Net Escrow Restoration:}
  \[\Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow,IV}} = \int_{t_3}^{t_4} \dot W_{\mathrm{income}}(t) dt = W_{\mathrm{income}}\]
\item
  \textbf{Master Solvency Condition:} Under the income condition
  (Section 17.1), the net cycle income strictly covers all cycle
  expenditures:
  \[W_{\mathrm{income}} \ge W_{\mathrm{meas}} + W_{\mathrm{reset}} + W_{\mathrm{dissipation}} \equiv W_{\mathrm{demand}}\]
  Ensuring \(E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(t_4) \ge E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(t_0)\).
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{18.3 Resolution of the Sign Issue: The True Invariant
Functional}\label{resolution-of-the-sign-issue-the-true-invariant-functional}

In earlier formulations, the relative entropy with respect to a thermal
Gibbs state \(\tau_S \propto e^{-\beta H_S}\),
\[D(\rho_S \| \tau_S) = \beta \operatorname{Tr}(H_S \rho_S) - S(\rho_S) - \ln Z_S\]
was inverted: restoring purity/order (\(\Delta S < 0\)) causes
\(D(\rho_S \| \tau_S)\) to \textbf{increase}, because a clean logical
state is further from the high-entropy thermal state \(\tau_S\) than a
degraded, mixed state.

\paragraph{The Correct Physical Code Error
Metric}\label{the-correct-physical-code-error-metric}

The objective of protection is fidelity to the \textbf{code subspace
\(\mathcal{C}\)}, not proximity to thermal chaos. We define the code
error directly via the orthogonal projection \(P_{\mathcal{C}}\) onto
the stabilized subspace:
\[\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S) \equiv \operatorname{Tr}\left(\rho_S (I - P_{\mathcal{C}})\right) = 1 - \operatorname{Tr}(\rho_S P_{\mathcal{C}})\]
which satisfies:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S) \ge 0\), with equality if and
  only if \(\operatorname{supp}(\rho_S) \subseteq \mathcal{C}\).
\item
  Under environmental noise/drift:
  \(\Delta_{\mathrm{noise}} \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}} > 0\).
\item
  Under syndrome feedback \(\mathcal{R}_C\):
  \[\Delta_{\mathrm{II}} \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}} = \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S(t_2)) - \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S(t_1)) = 0 - \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S(t_1)) = -\eta C < 0\]
  where \(\eta C > 0\) is the discrete recovery magnitude.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{The Master Storage / Lyapunov
Functional}\label{the-master-storage-lyapunov-functional}

Define the actual storage functional \[
\Phi(\rho,E)=\mathcal E_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho)+f(E),\qquad
f(E)=\frac{\max(0,W_{\mathrm{target}}-E)}{W_0},\quad W_0>0.
\] The storage capacity \(K\) is chosen at least as large as the
required reserve target. For nonnegative income \(I\), replenishment
gives \[
E^+=\min(K,E+I),\qquad
f(E^+)-f(E)=-\min\{I/W_0,f(E)\}.
\] Thus replenishment clears at most the existing deficit. Additional
income does not drive the clipped term below zero
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t}).

Let \(W_{\mathrm{cycle}}\) be the complete realised cycle charge,
including measurement, correction, reset, holding, guard, routing,
verification and controller work. Its physical escrow balance is \[
E_{n+1}=\min(K,E_n-W_{\mathrm{cycle}}+I_n).
\] The excess \(S_n=\max(0,E_n-W_{\mathrm{cycle}}+I_n-K)\) is explicitly
exported: \[
E_{n+1}+W_{\mathrm{cycle}}+S_n=E_n+I_n.
\] \texttt{capacity\_balance} and
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}
prove these identities. Within-cycle reservations still fund every
operation before it executes.

If \(W_{\mathrm{cycle}}\le I_n\), the reserve term does not increase
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t}).
The actual error drift and correction give \[
\mathcal E_{\mathrm{drift}}\le\mathcal E_n+\lambda\tau,\qquad
\mathcal E_{n+1}\le\mathcal E_{\mathrm{drift}}-\eta C.
\] Therefore the defined functional obeys \[
\Phi_{n+1}-\Phi_n\le-(\eta C-\lambda\tau).
\] \texttt{cycle\_contracts} proves this from the realised error bounds
and complete solvent balance. For positive margin the contraction is
strict on the stated error cycles. The controller supplies its tracking
rate; the reserve plateau does not imply an additional exponential
convergence theorem.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{18.4 Theorem 8 (Complete Physical Accounting
Statement)}\label{theorem-8-complete-physical-accounting-statement}

\textbf{Theorem 8 (Autonomic Preservation Invariant).} Let an autonomous
physical protector \(a \in \Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\) execute the periodic
syndrome-feedback-reset protocol
\((\mathcal{R}_C, \tau_{\mathrm{cycle}})\) under the Hamiltonian
convention (18.1) with fixed bath temperature \(T\). If \(a\) satisfies:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Solvency:}
  \(\dot W_{\mathrm{income}}(t) \ge \dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t)\) for
  all \(t\).
\item
  \textbf{Timeliness:}
  \(\tau_{\mathrm{cycle}} < \tau_{\mathrm{harm}} \equiv \eta C / \lambda_{\mathrm{decay}}\).
\item
  \textbf{Landauer Saturation:}
  \(E_{\mathrm{escrow}} \ge k_B T \, I_M\).
\end{enumerate}

Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  The combined storage functional \(\Phi(t)\) is a strict Lyapunov
  function for the unmitigated error dynamics, contracting by at least
  \(\sigma_0 > 0\) on each cycle containing an error.
\item
  The code subspace \(\mathcal{C}\) is asymptotically stable and
  invariant on the good event \(G\).
\item
  All work, heat, and information quantities are strictly conserved and
  accounted for:
  \[W_{\mathrm{income}} = W_{\mathrm{on}} + Q_B + \Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow}}, \quad Q_B \ge T \Delta S_B \ge k_B T I_M\]
  with zero uncharged entropy or hidden energy debt.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Proof.} The actual error drift and repair bound, together with the
complete solvent escrow balance of §18.3, give the cycle change at most
\(-(\eta C-\lambda\tau)\)
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s}).
The reserve-deficit term is handled through saturation and is
nonincreasing over the cycle; no linear replenishment descent past zero
is assumed. Timeliness gives \(\sigma_0=\eta C-\lambda\tau>0\)
(\texttt{margin\_pos}), and the cycle recurrence gives
\(\Phi_n\le\Phi_0-n\sigma_0\) over the stated error cycles
(\texttt{lyapunov\_iterate}). The physical holding and entropy-export
certificates retain their separate roles. \(\blacksquare\)

The compiled result establishes the defined storage inequality from the
actual drift, repair and complete solvent balance. The full Theorem 8
statement retains all its conclusions. Applying it to the physical
installation still joins the protective source dynamics, invariant/error
bounds and complete work/entropy accounts; those application
certificates are not inferred from the reserve plateau or from the
iteration identity alone.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y},

the composition statement is

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Section 19 --- Theorem E9: Observation, Wave-Race
Guarantees, and Epistemic
Self-Healing}\label{section-19-theorem-e9-observation-wave-race-guarantees-and-epistemic-self-healing}

\emph{This section establishes the detection guarantee, Byzantine sensor
fault tolerance, the wave-race propagation margin, and buffer protection
during the response interval.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{19.1 The Physical Measurement and Disturbance
Setting}\label{the-physical-measurement-and-disturbance-setting}

Let \(\mathcal{M}\) be the physical metric space of the substrate
hosting the protected particulars, equipped with Riemannian metric
\(d_{\mathcal{M}}(x, y)\).

\paragraph{Physical Velocities and
Limits:}\label{physical-velocities-and-limits}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Signal Propagation Velocity (\(c\)):} Electromagnetic/optical
  communication across the bus propagates at velocity \(c\).
\item
  \textbf{Harm/Decoherence Velocity (\(v_{\mathrm{harm}}\)):} Physical
  degradation fronts (thermal propagation, mechanical shock, acoustic
  damage, chemical diffusion, adversarial intrusion) propagate at
  characteristic velocity \(v_{\mathrm{harm}} < c\).
\item
  \textbf{Sensor Lattice:} A discrete lattice of non-destructive sensors
  \(\mathcal{S} = \{s_i\}\) with spatial density
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{sens}}\) and maximum spacing
  \(R_{\mathrm{sens}} = \sup_{x \in \mathcal{M}} \min_i d_{\mathcal{M}}(x, s_i)\).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{19.2 Byzantine Sensor Fault and Spoofing
Tolerance}\label{byzantine-sensor-fault-and-spoofing-tolerance}

Let a physical disturbance occur at location \(x \in \mathcal{M}\) with
onset time \(t_0\).

\paragraph{Sensor Quorum and Disjoint Observer
Sets:}\label{sensor-quorum-and-disjoint-observer-sets}

Each protected region is monitored by a local cluster of \(K\)
independent sensors. To account for sensor failures, noise bursts, or
adversarial sensor spoofing:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Adversarial Bound:} At any instant, at most \(f\) out of the
  \(K\) sensors in the local cluster are faulty or spoofed, with
  \(K \ge 2f + 1\) (or \(K \ge 3f + 1\) under Byzantine asymmetric
  signaling).
\item
  \textbf{Consensus Filter:} The cluster computes the robust trimmed
  estimate
  \(\hat{y}(t) = \operatorname{median}_{K-2f}(y_1(t), \dots, y_K(t))\).
\item
  \textbf{Detection Criterion:} An alarm is committed when
  \(\hat{y}(t) > \theta_{\mathrm{sens}}\).
\item
  \textbf{False Alarm \& Spoofing Immunity:} Because \(f < K/2\), no
  subset of \(f\) malicious or malfunctioning sensors can force a false
  alarm or suppress a true alarm:
  \[\hat{y}(t) \in [\min_{i \in \mathrm{honest}} y_i(t), \max_{i \in \mathrm{honest}} y_i(t)]\]
\item
  \textbf{Detection Time (\(\tau_{\mathrm{detect}}\)):}
  \[\tau_{\mathrm{detect}} = \tau_{\mathrm{sampling}} + \frac{R_{\mathrm{sens}}}{c} + \tau_{\mathrm{consensus}}\]
  where \(\tau_{\mathrm{sampling}}\) is the sampling cycle time,
  \(R_{\mathrm{sens}}/c\) is communication latency to the local voting
  node, and \(\tau_{\mathrm{consensus}}\) is local deterministic
  comparator time.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{19.3 The Wave-Race Margin and Response
Interval}\label{the-wave-race-margin-and-response-interval}

Let an adversarial or stochastic harm front originate at boundary
distance \(d_{\mathrm{boundary}}\) from a protected logical core.

\paragraph{The Timing Chain:}\label{the-timing-chain}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Alarm Transmission:} An alarm generated at the sensor lattice
  reaches the autonomic controller and actuation units across bus
  distance \(d_{\mathrm{bus}}\) in time:
  \[\tau_{\mathrm{signal}} = \frac{d_{\mathrm{bus}}}{c}\]
\item
  \textbf{Autonomic Decision \& Reroute:} The controller verifies
  reserve escrow and commits the repair/isolation plan:
  \[\tau_{\mathrm{decision}} \le \tau_{\mathrm{commit}}\]
\item
  \textbf{Actuation / Repair Pulse:} Actuators engage (diverting energy
  flux, activating topological braiding, or adjusting stabilizer
  syndrome drives):
  \[\tau_{\mathrm{actuate}} \le \tau_{\mathrm{repair}}\]
\item
  \textbf{Total Autonomic Response Time (\(\tau_{\mathrm{response}}\)):}
  \[\tau_{\mathrm{response}} = \tau_{\mathrm{detect}} + \tau_{\mathrm{signal}} + \tau_{\mathrm{decision}} + \tau_{\mathrm{actuate}}\]
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{The Earliest Irreversible Harm Window
(\(\tau_{\mathrm{harm}}\)):}{The Earliest Irreversible Harm Window (\textbackslash tau\_\{\textbackslash mathrm\{harm\}\}):}}\label{the-earliest-irreversible-harm-window-tau_mathrmharm}

The physical disturbance must traverse the protective buffer shell of
spatial thickness \(L_{\mathrm{buffer}}\) and overcome the code distance
\(d_{\mathrm{code}}\):
\[\tau_{\mathrm{harm}} = \frac{L_{\mathrm{buffer}}}{v_{\mathrm{harm}}}\]
For topological/stabilizer codes with physical error rate
\(p < p_{\mathrm{th}}\), logical failure requires accumulating at least
\(\lfloor(d_{\mathrm{code}} - 1)/2\rfloor\) errors, providing an
additional temporal buffer:
\[\tau_{\mathrm{code\_headroom}} = \frac{d_{\mathrm{code}}}{2 \lambda_{\mathrm{fault}}}\]

\paragraph{The Wave-Race Inequality:}\label{the-wave-race-inequality}

Because \(v_{\mathrm{harm}} < c\), the spatial buffer
\(L_{\mathrm{buffer}}\) guarantees a strictly positive propagation
margin:
\[\Delta \tau_{\mathrm{margin}} \equiv \tau_{\mathrm{harm}} - \tau_{\mathrm{response}} = \frac{L_{\mathrm{buffer}}}{v_{\mathrm{harm}}} - \left(\tau_{\mathrm{detect}} + \frac{d_{\mathrm{bus}}}{c} + \tau_{\mathrm{decision}} + \tau_{\mathrm{actuate}}\right)\]
By choosing the engineering parameter \(L_{\mathrm{buffer}}\) (Section
17.2) such that:
\[L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \left(\tau_{\mathrm{detect}} + \frac{d_{\mathrm{bus}}}{c} + \tau_{\mathrm{decision}} + \tau_{\mathrm{actuate}}\right)\]
the margin is strictly positive: \(\Delta \tau_{\mathrm{margin}} > 0\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{19.4 Protection Guarantee During the Response
Interval}\label{protection-guarantee-during-the-response-interval}

What guarantees that the system is preserved during the latency interval
\(t \in [t_0, t_0 + \tau_{\mathrm{response}}]\) before active repair
engages?

\paragraph{The Two Shields:}\label{the-two-shields}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Passive Physical Buffer (Energy \& Flux Shield):} The local
  physical enclosure possesses heat capacity \(C_{\mathrm{thermal}}\)
  and mechanical yield reserve \(E_{\mathrm{yield}}\). For maximum
  external incident flux \(\dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}^{\max}\):
  \[\int_0^{\tau_{\mathrm{response}}} \dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}(t) dt \le \dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}^{\max} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}} \le E_{\mathrm{buffer}}\]
  The buffer ensures temperature and strain at the protected core remain
  strictly within the linear elastic / non-damaging regime throughout
  the entire reaction window.
\item
  \textbf{Topological Syndrome Headroom (Information Shield):} During
  \(\tau_{\mathrm{response}}\), errors accumulated by the physical
  qubits satisfy:
  \[N_{\mathrm{errors}}(\tau_{\mathrm{response}}) \le \lambda_{\mathrm{fault}} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}} < \frac{d_{\mathrm{code}}}{2}\]
  Because \(N_{\mathrm{errors}} < d_{\mathrm{code}}/2\), the corrupted
  state remains strictly within the correctable decoding radius of the
  code space \(\mathcal{C}\). When active feedback engages at
  \(t = t_0 + \tau_{\mathrm{response}}\), the syndrome measurement
  uniquely identifies the error chain and restores the logical state
  with fidelity \(1\).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{19.5 Theorem E9 (Epistemic and Topological
Self-Healing)}\label{theorem-e9-epistemic-and-topological-self-healing}

\textbf{Theorem E9 (Timely Self-Healing \& Metric Rigidity).} Let a
Sovereign Lattice installation satisfy:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Superluminal Communication Margin:} \(v_{\mathrm{harm}} < c\).
\item
  \textbf{Sensor Redundancy:} Local sensor clusters have size
  \(K \ge 2f + 1\), with at most \(f\) faulty or adversarial nodes.
\item
  \textbf{Buffer Sizing:}
  \[L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}}, \quad E_{\mathrm{buffer}} \ge \dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}^{\max} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}}\]
\item
  \textbf{Code Distance Headroom:}
  \[d_{\mathrm{code}} > 2 \lambda_{\mathrm{fault}} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}}\]
\end{enumerate}

Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Detection \& Spoofing Immunity:} Every physical disturbance
  exceeding threshold \(\theta_{\mathrm{sens}}\) is detected and
  committed within \(\tau_{\mathrm{detect}}\), and no adversarial
  spoofing by \(\le f\) sensors can trigger false actuation or mask
  genuine harm.
\item
  \textbf{Timely Preemption:} Coherent repair and flow rerouting are
  fully applied before the harm front reaches the protected core
  (\(\tau_{\mathrm{response}} < \tau_{\mathrm{harm}}\)).
\item
  \textbf{Continuous Safety Invariant:} For all \(t \ge 0\), including
  throughout every active response window, the logical fidelity of
  protected particulars satisfies:
  \[F(\rho_{\mathrm{logical}}(t), \rho_{\mathrm{target}}) \ge 1 - \mathcal{O}(e^{-\kappa d_{\mathrm{code}}})\]
  ensuring zero uncontained catastrophic loss.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Proof.} Follows by combining the Byzantine quorum consensus
(19.2), the wave-race inequality (19.3), and the passive buffer and
syndrome headroom bounds (19.4). Machine-checked algebraic timing
instances are verified in \texttt{BridgeE9.lean}. \(\blacksquare\)

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Machine-checked counterpart (Section 23).} Compiled
counterparts:
\texttt{E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t},

with the sharp Mandelstam--Tamm bound as

\subsection{Section 20 --- Theorem H5: Generative Incompleteness and
Reflective Relational
Closure}\label{section-20-theorem-h5-generative-incompleteness-and-reflective-relational-closure}

\emph{This section establishes authority attenuation, the
reserve-before-activation lifecycle, safe refusal with continuing care,
and conservative logical axiom expansion.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{20.1 The Problem of Recursive Spawning and
Self-Modification}\label{the-problem-of-recursive-spawning-and-self-modification}

In any autonomous superintelligent architecture, agents may spawn child
processes, delegate sub-tasks, instantiate specialized sub-agents, or
undergo recursive self-modification. A catastrophic failure occurs if:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Escalation of Privilege:} A spawned descendant claims
  unmediated permissions, bypassing the parent's boundary constraints.
\item
  \textbf{Unfunded Proliferation (Fork Bomb / Sybil Drain):} A parent
  creates infinite un-escrowed descendants that exhaust the substrate's
  shared physical reserves.
\item
  \textbf{Poisoning via Axiom Expansion:} A cognitive agent extending
  its formal knowledge base introduces inconsistent or adversarial
  axioms that corrupt foundational safety invariants.
\end{enumerate}

Theorem H5 proves that under the \textbf{Reflective Relational Closure}
constraint \(\mathcal{I}\), recursive generation is mathematically
closed, strictly authority-attenuating, and universally pre-funded.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{20.2 The Spawning Formalism and Authority
Attenuation}\label{the-spawning-formalism-and-authority-attenuation}

Let \(\mathcal{A}\) be the universe of active agents. An agent
\(a \in \mathcal{A}\) is defined by a tuple:
\[a = \left(\mathcal{S}_a, \operatorname{Auth}(a), E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(a), \mathcal{C}_a\right)\]
where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{S}_a\) is the internal computational state space.
\item
  \(\operatorname{Auth}(a) \subseteq \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{act}})\)
  is the permitted set of physical action operators.
\item
  \(E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(a) \ge 0\) is the certified energetic reserve.
\item
  \(\mathcal{C}_a \subset \mathcal{H}_S\) is the bound protected code
  subspace the agent is sworn to maintain.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Definition (The Spawning
Transition):}\label{definition-the-spawning-transition}

When parent \(p\) spawns child \(c\), the transition is mediated by the
kernel compiler gate \(\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}\):
\[\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}} : (p, \text{spec}_c) \mapsto (p', c)\]

\paragraph{Theorem H5.1 (Strict Authority
Attenuation).}\label{theorem-h5.1-strict-authority-attenuation.}

For every legitimate spawning transition \(\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}\),
the child's authority satisfies:
\[\operatorname{Auth}(c) \subseteq \operatorname{Auth}(p) \setminus \Delta_{\mathrm{meta}}\]
where \(\Delta_{\mathrm{meta}}\) includes the capability to override
parent boundary monitors, re-allocate un-escrowed root reserves, or
modify the kernel compiler gate \(\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}\).

\emph{Proof.} The kernel compiler \(\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}\) verifies
capability inclusion as a type-checking prerequisite. Any child
specification with
\(\operatorname{Auth}(c) \not\subseteq \operatorname{Auth}(p)\) fails
type checking and is rejected as an un-compilable ill-typed program
before memory allocation. \(\blacksquare\)

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{20.3 The Reserve-Before-Activation
Machine}\label{the-reserve-before-activation-machine}

To guarantee that no spawned descendant creates an unhedged drain on the
physical maintenance ledger, every agent life-cycle is governed by a
strict five-state automaton:

\begin{verbatim}
  [UNBORN] ---> (reserve) ---> [RESERVED] ---> (activate) ---> [ACTIVE]
                                                                  |
                                                               (retire)
                                                                  v
  [TERMINATED] <--- (release) <--- [RETIRED] <--------------------+
\end{verbatim}

\paragraph{State Transitions and
Invariants:}\label{state-transitions-and-invariants}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{})}:}
  Parent \(p\) must carve out the child's complete maintenance and
  execution budget \(W_{\mathrm{req}}\) from its own tracked escrow:
  \[E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(p') = E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(p) - W_{\mathrm{req}}, \quad E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(c) = W_{\mathrm{req}} \ge W_{\mathrm{min}}(T_{\mathrm{lifetime}})\]
  If
  \(E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(p) < W_{\mathrm{req}} + E_{\mathrm{safety\_margin}}\),
  the reservation is rejected. Zero debt is permitted.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{activate(c)}:} Child \(c\) transitions to
  \texttt{ACTIVE} only after \(E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(c)\) is physically
  credited to its local hardware controller.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{retire(c)}:} Upon task completion or deadline expiry,
  child \(c\) ceases external action; its actuation channels are
  severed.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{release(c,\ p)}:} Residual unspent escrow
  \(E_{\mathrm{residual}}(c)\) is returned to parent \(p\) or the root
  commons ledger.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Corollary (No Unfunded
Descendants).}\label{corollary-no-unfunded-descendants.}

At every instant \(t\), the total active population \(\mathcal{A}(t)\)
satisfies:
\[\sum_{a \in \mathcal{A}(t)} \dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(a) \le \sum_{a \in \mathcal{A}(t)} \operatorname{Income}_a + E_{\mathrm{escrow\_pool}}\]
The network is immune to Sybil proliferation and resource-exhaustion
denial of service.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{20.4 Safe Refusal with Continuing
Care}\label{safe-refusal-with-continuing-care}

Suppose an adversarial, compromised, or misaligned parent issues an
actuation command to a child:
\[\mathrm{cmd} = \text{"Delete or cause unmediated harm to particular } \mathcal{W}\text{"}\]

\paragraph{Theorem H5.2 (Safe Refusal with Invariant
Protection).}\label{theorem-h5.2-safe-refusal-with-invariant-protection.}

Under Reflective Relational Closure:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Refusal of Harmful Command:} The command violates the boundary
  constraint
  \(\mathcal{C}_c \cap \operatorname{Effect}(\mathrm{cmd}) = \emptyset\).
  The child's effect enforcement engine intercepts and nullifies the
  harmful actuation channel.
\item
  \textbf{Continuing Care (\(R(m) \lor H(m)\)):} Refusal does not
  terminate the child's existence, nor does it abandon care for the
  compromised parent or affected particulars. The child switches into
  autonomous defensive holding:
  \[\operatorname{Action}(c) = \text{hold\_in\_escrow}(\mathcal{W})\]
  maintaining the witness and physical stability of \(\mathcal{W}\)
  while alerting the lattice consensus layer.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Proof.} By the reciprocity dominance theorem (§15.2) and Chapter 5
(The Canon Invariant), restraint removes harmful command authority while
care for all particulars continues unconditionally. Because the child's
escrow was fully funded at \texttt{reserve}, the child has the physical
energy required to sustain protection independently of the parent's
ongoing cooperation. \(\blacksquare\)

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{20.5 H5 Axiom Expansion: Logical Closure Without
Poisoning}\label{h5-axiom-expansion-logical-closure-without-poisoning}

When an intelligent agent extends its formal theory \(T_0\) by
postulating new axioms or learned generalizations \(\Delta T\), how is
the system shielded against inconsistency poisoning?

\paragraph{The Submodule Projection of Logical
Theories:}\label{the-submodule-projection-of-logical-theories}

Let \(\mathcal{L}\) be the Hilbert space of proposition valuations (or
the lattice of closed deduction spaces).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Foundational protected axioms form a closed subspace
  \(A \subset \mathcal{L}\).
\item
  Newly proposed theories or empirical models form a candidate subspace
  \(B \subset \mathcal{L}\).
\item
  The verified safe knowledge base is the orthogonal projection onto the
  intersection: \[\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{safe}} = P_{A \sqcap B}\]
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Theorem H5.3 (Axiom Expansion
Soundness).}\label{theorem-h5.3-axiom-expansion-soundness.}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Consistent Expansion:} If candidate axioms \(B\) are fully
  consistent with \(A\) and contain \(A\) (\(A \subseteq B\)), then:
  \[A \sqcap B = A\] and foundational anchors are preserved with zero
  distortion.
\item
  \textbf{Contradictory / Adversarial Expansion:} If candidate axioms
  \(B\) contradict foundational protection axioms, the global meet
  collapses: \(A \sqcap B = \{0\}\). By von Neumann's alternating
  projection theorem
  (\texttt{C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}V\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
  with
  \texttt{v\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n},
  compiled), the alternating projection converges to the projection onto
  the meet --- which is zero, so the state machine detects the collapse
  and rolls back (Chapter 4); the anchor is not silently poisoned. When
  the candidate is consistent and contains the anchors
  (\(A \subseteq B\)), the meet is \(A\) itself and the anchors are
  preserved with zero distortion.
\item
  \textbf{Epistemic Immunity:} No adversarial theorem deduction can
  force a contradiction into the protected anchor \(A\).
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Proof.} Follows from the machine-checked meet algebra
(\texttt{MeetAlgebra.lean}) and von Neumann identification
(\texttt{M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n}).
The operator \(P_{A \sqcap B}\) acts as a conservative projection that
absorbs only the non-contradictory relational content of new theories
while annihilating adversarial derivations. \(\blacksquare\)

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Machine-checked counterpart (Section 23).} Compiled
counterparts: H5.1--H5.2 and the reserve-before-activation machine ---
\texttt{H\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n},
\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n},
\texttt{no\_unfunded\_work},
\texttt{n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t},
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e};
H5.3 --- von Neumann
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}V\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}),
Halperin
