the detectors
(\texttt{H\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r},
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}),
and the Hilbert-space meet algebra
(\texttt{M\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k}).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Section 21 --- X3: complete inputs, compulsory traffic,
transport and recovery
work}\label{section-21-x3-complete-inputs-compulsory-traffic-transport-and-recovery-work}

The X3 target remains the dimension-general law
\(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\), over the complete permitted
recovery-protocol class with every resource charged. This section
incorporates the information, traffic, carrier and work derivations
carried through the gauntlet. G3 names the matching recovery
construction requested for the upper inequality; it does not replace the
target with a lower-bound-only theorem.

\subsubsection{21.1 Complete permitted inputs and historical
information}\label{complete-permitted-inputs-and-historical-information}

Let \(H\) denote the historical reference. The permitted recovery input
contains the residual core, all accessible propagating carriers, emitted
bath modes, retained memories, distributed copies, decoder side
information and ancillary resources. These are subsystems or joint
resources of one complete state; their correlations remain in the joint
state. A correlated record cannot be omitted because it is distant,
private, predeployed or called an ancilla.

For any recovery channel \(\mathcal R\), data processing gives

\[
I(H:\mathcal R(S))\le I(H:S).
\]

Independent ancillas do not increase the input information. Initially
correlated ancillas already belong in \(S\). For messages \(M_j\)
entering a decoder that retains \(B_{j-1}\),

\[
I(H:B_{j-1}M_j)-I(H:B_{j-1})
=I(H:M_j\mid B_{j-1})\le2\ln\dim M_j.
\]

The increments telescope with every initial correlated resource included
in \(B_0\). Thus recovery cannot manufacture a missing historical
correlation from uncorrelated noise. The particular's continuing history
and a later reconstruction retain their distinct meanings.

\texttt{D\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
establishes data processing for its probability-space formulation;
\texttt{F\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n}
establishes the finite classical version. The quantum message inequality
above is the corresponding entropy argument, with its quantum systems
and units explicitly stated.

\subsubsection{21.2 A joint covariance estimate, with its
proof}\label{a-joint-covariance-estimate-with-its-proof}

For the finite-temperature fermionic Gaussian calculation, let

\[
Q=(I+e^{\beta(h-\mu I)})^{-1},\qquad
\|h-\mu I\|\le E_{\max}.
\]

The scalar Fermi function is decreasing, so every eigenvalue lies in
\([\delta,1-\delta]\), where \(\delta=(1+e^{\beta E_{\max}})^{-1}>0\).
For the full reference--field covariance

\[
Q=\begin{pmatrix}A&C\\C^\dagger&B\end{pmatrix},
\qquad \delta I\le Q\le(1-\delta)I,
\]

the joint gauge-invariant fermionic Gaussian mutual information
satisfies

\[
I(H:F)\le\frac{\|C\|_F^2}{\delta(1-\delta)}
\quad\text{(nats)}.
\]

To prove it, put \(Q_0=A\oplus B\), \(\Delta=Q-Q_0\), and
\(f(x)=x\ln x+(1-x)\ln(1-x)\). Gaussian entropy gives
\(I=\operatorname{Tr}f(Q)-\operatorname{Tr}f(Q_0)\). The first
derivative along \(Q_0+s\Delta\) vanishes at zero because \(f'(Q_0)\) is
block diagonal and \(\Delta\) is off diagonal. The whole segment keeps
the spectral margin. Since \(f''(x)\le[\delta(1-\delta)]^{-1}\), the
divided-difference expression for the trace Hessian bounds it by
\(\|\Delta\|_F^2/[\delta(1-\delta)]\). Taylor's integral remainder
contributes one half, and \(\|\Delta\|_F^2=2\|C\|_F^2\), proving the
claim.

This uses the complete cross block. A single connected correlator or a
count of modes supplies neither this joint upper bound nor a compulsory
reset bill. The full UPT domain continues to include bosonic and other
carriers; this particular covariance calculation has its stated
fermionic domain. Their complete physical input/readout binding belongs
in the full-domain construction G1, rather than being obtained by
relabelling the covariance theorem. The channel, carrier and work steps
below retain their own explicit constructions.

\subsubsection{21.3 The calibrated joint adaptive
channel}\label{the-calibrated-joint-adaptive-channel}

Let a boundary have \(n\ge1\) nodes and at most \(m\le n\) active
readout coordinates per use. Conditional on the complete decoder history
\(h\), assume each input coordinate has range at most
\(\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}/n\), and write

\[
Y=G_hX+Z,\qquad
\|G_h\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le g,\qquad
\Sigma_h=\operatorname{Cov}(Z\mid h)\ge\sigma^2I,\quad\sigma>0.
\]

The conditional Gaussian noise is independent of the current
message/input. The covariance floor applies after conditioning on all
permitted side information. Inputs may be correlated and adaptive; all
accessible readout channels belong in the joint model.

The midpoint of each coordinate's range yields
\(\operatorname{Var}(X_i\mid h)\le\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2/(4n^2)\).
Hence its covariance \(K_h\) satisfies
\(\operatorname{Tr}K_h\le m\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2/(4n^2)\). Gaussian
maximum entropy and data processing give, in bits,

\[
I(M:Y\mid h)\le\frac1{2\ln2}
\ln\det\!\left(I+\Sigma_h^{-1/2}G_hK_hG_h^\dagger\Sigma_h^{-1/2}\right).
\]

For a positive semidefinite \(W\), apply \(\ln(1+x)\le x\) to its
eigenvalues to obtain \(\ln\det(I+W)\le\operatorname{Tr}W\). The gain
and full covariance floor then give

\[
I(M:Y\mid h)\le
\frac{g^2}{2\sigma^2\ln2}\operatorname{Tr}K_h
\le \frac{m g^2\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2}{8\sigma^2\ln2\,n^2}.
\]

Averaging and the chain rule sum these conditional inequalities over
adaptive uses. With at most \(\nu\) uses per second, put

\[
K_{\mathrm{rate}}=\frac{\nu g^2\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2}{8\sigma^2\ln2}.
\]

The rate is at most \(mK_{\mathrm{rate}}/n^2\le mK_{\mathrm{rate}}/n\).
Observable range and information rate therefore have separate calibrated
constants. The joint noise floor prevents an uncharged noiseless
combination of marginally noisy sensors. \texttt{GaussianKL} through
\texttt{LogDet} supply the Gaussian channel results, and
\texttt{A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
and \texttt{channel\_cap} supply this assembly.

\subsubsection{21.4 A positive deficit forces traffic, then charged area
power}\label{a-positive-deficit-forces-traffic-then-charged-area-power}

Let a historical message \(W\) have \(M\ge2\) possible values. Include
every initially accessible record in \(B\), and require decoder error at
most \(\epsilon<1-1/M\). If \(H(W\mid B)\ge H_{\mathrm{rem}}\), Fano's
error-flag argument gives

\[
H(W\mid Y,B)\le h_2(\epsilon)+\epsilon\log_2(M-1),
\] \[
\Delta_0=H_{\mathrm{rem}}-h_2(\epsilon)-\epsilon\log_2(M-1)>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad I(W:Y\mid B)\ge\Delta_0.
\]

Indeed the correct branch determines \(W\) and the error branch leaves
at most \(M-1\) possibilities. Expanding conditional entropy by that
flag proves the inequality; its right-hand side is increasing up to
error \(1-1/M\). These steps are \texttt{Fano.fano} and
\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}.

For deadline at most \(\tau_{\max}>0\), the required average rate is at
least \(\Delta_0/\tau_{\max}\). With
\(0<K_{\mathrm{rate}}\le K_{\max}\), the cut bound implies

\[
m\ge\theta_0 n,\qquad
\theta_0=\frac{\Delta_0}{\tau_{\max}K_{\max}}>0
\quad(\texttt{AreaBridge.required\_traffic}).
\]

If the active set varies, the chain rule charges active counts over the
actual uses. A standing-power application also includes the
concurrency/service certificate, rather than silently converting average
work into instantaneous power.

For \(d_G\)-dimensional geometry, let \(n(r)\ge a r^{d_G-1}\) and let
every active frontier node carry actual power at least \(p_0>0\). Then

\[
P(r)\ge p_0m(r)\ge p_0\theta_0a\,r^{d_G-1}
=p'r^{d_G-1}.
\]

This is
\texttt{A\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}.
Geometry has an explicit lattice witness: the box of radius \(r\) has
\((2r+1)^{d_G}\) sites, and its boundary has at least
\(2(2r+1)^{d_G-1}\) sites
(\texttt{I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}x},
\texttt{card\_boundary}, \texttt{boundary\_power}). The information
deficit and deadline force activation; the geometry counts available
sites; the ledger charges the active sites. All three steps enter the
argument.

\subsubsection{21.5 The carrier tail gives the necessary recovery
radius}\label{the-carrier-tail-gives-the-necessary-recovery-radius}

In the explicit flagged-carrier construction, a payload \(P\) contains
the retained correlation with \(H\), with \(I_0=I(H:P)>0\). Its location
is independent of the payload/reference and undergoes outward drift
\(u>0\) with diffusion \(D>0\). Every other permitted correlated record
is already included in the payload inventory. Grant an optimistic
decoder the payload whenever the carrier lies at \(x\le r\), and an
independent blank otherwise, with the occupancy flag revealed. This
grants at least as much access as the actual local decoder.

The flag is independent of \(H\). The chain rule therefore gives the
exact identity

\[
I(H:P_{\mathrm{view}}E)=p_r(t)I_0,\qquad
p_r(t)=\Phi\!\left(\frac{r-ut}{\sqrt{2Dt}}\right).
\]

Data processing bounds any lesser decoder by the same quantity. In the
finite-alphabet construction these are
\texttt{F\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y}
and \texttt{decoder\_bound}; the displayed flag argument also states the
corresponding joint-state calculation.

For \(r<ut\), the Gaussian tail gives
\(p_r(t)\le\exp[-(ut-r)^2/(4Dt)]\). If success needs
\(0<I_{\mathrm{target}}\le I_0\), every successful radius therefore
satisfies

\[
r\ge ut-c\sqrt t,\qquad
c=2\sqrt{D\ln(I_0/I_{\mathrm{target}})}.
\]

For \(r\ge ut\) the same inequality is immediate. Choose any \(0<w<u\)
and set \(t_c=c^2/[4w(u-w)]\). Completing the square gives

\[
ut-c\sqrt t-w(t-t_c)
=(u-w)\left(\sqrt t-\frac{c}{2(u-w)}\right)^2\ge0,
\] \[
\boxed{R(t)\ge w(t-t_c)}.
\]

These are
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l},
\texttt{F\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}
and
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s}.
Diffusive tails stay positive at finite time; exact purging is
unnecessary. The necessary lower radius comes from the complete
carrier/information construction. A Lieb--Robinson upper bound on
propagation is compatible with locality but does not supply this lower
radius by itself.

\subsubsection{21.6 Integrating actual recovery
work}\label{integrating-actual-recovery-work}

Keep unattended age \(t\) distinct from subsequent recovery duration
\(s\in[0,T_{\mathrm{rec}}]\). For every successful permitted protocol
let its frontier \(r(s)\ge0\) be absolutely continuous, start at
\(r_0\), finish at least at \(R(t)\), and satisfy \(r'(s)\le v\). With
\(d_G\ge1\), the charged power floor of 21.4 yields

\[
W(t)\ge p'\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{rec}}}r(s)^{d_G-1}\,ds.
\]

The fundamental theorem of calculus gives

\[
r(T_{\mathrm{rec}})^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}
=d_G\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{rec}}}r^{d_G-1}r'\,ds
\le vd_G\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{rec}}}r^{d_G-1}\,ds.
\]

Monotonicity of the frontier is unnecessary; nonnegativity and the speed
bound suffice. Taking the infimum over the same successful protocol
class gives

\[
\boxed{
W_{\min}(t)\ge\frac{p'}{v d_G}
\left(R(t)^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}\right)
\ge\frac{p'}{v d_G}
\left([w(t-t_c)]^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}\right)
}
\]

for sufficiently late \(t\). Thus delayed recovery work diverges, with
an explicit order-\(t^{d_G}\) lower bound
(\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d},
\texttt{Wmin\_lower\_bound},
\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s};
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}).

Predeployment is charged within the same accounting. If the initial
protected radius depends on age, it cannot be treated as a free constant
and subtracted while ignoring its deployment and holding bill. For
example, an already funded deployment costing at least \(A r_0^{d_G}\),
followed by a recovery bound \(A(R^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G})\), gives a total at
least \(A R^{d_G}\). This is the prepaid lifecycle cancellation: the
rival may move the expenditure in time, while the complete resource bill
retains it. It carries forward the gauntlet's predeployment answer.

\subsubsection{21.7 The matching upper construction: the supplied
one-pass
wavefront}\label{the-matching-upper-construction-the-supplied-one-pass-wavefront}

The gauntlet's dimension-general target and predeployment answer remain

\[
A(R)=\Theta(R^{d_G-1}),\qquad
\dot W_{\mathrm{maint}}=\omega_0R^{d_G-1},\qquad
W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G}).
\]

The new single-shot feedforward construction supplies a concrete way to
avoid repeatedly processing the recovered interior. Its cumulative-work
calculation is stronger and simpler when written as the following exact
telescoping proof.

\paragraph{21.7.1 Count each processed patch
once}\label{count-each-processed-patch-once}

Let \(v_{\mathrm{sw}}>0\) be the protocol's actual sweep speed,
consistent with the causal speed limit. For integer wave index \(k\),
set

\[
S_0=B_0(v_0),\qquad
S_k=B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}k}(v_0)\setminus
B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}(k-1)}(v_0)\quad(k\ge1).
\]

The shells are pairwise disjoint and their union through wave \(N\) is
\(B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}N}(v_0)\). Consequently

\[
\boxed{\sum_{k=0}^N|S_k|=|B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}N}(v_0)|}.
\]

Use the graph-volume convention \(|B_R|\le V_0(1+R)^{d_G}\); the \(1+R\)
handles the origin, where a nonempty ball cannot be bounded by
\(V_0R^{d_G}=0\). Equivalently one can state the original polynomial
bound only for radii at least one.

If a vertex is processed at most once at cost at most \(w_*\), the work
of that processing is

\[
W_{\mathrm{process}}(N)
\le w_*\sum_{k=0}^N|S_k|
\le w_*V_0(1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}N)^{d_G}.
\]

A fixed-radius local map on a bounded-degree graph has a bounded
neighbourhood: \(|B_{r_0}(v)|\le\sum_{j=0}^{r_0}D_{\max}^j\). Fixed
local input registers and a fixed evaluation circuit then give a
constant evaluation cost. Even if neighbouring evaluations read
overlapping neighbourhoods, each local register is read only a bounded
number of times. Thus that overlap changes the constant, not the
exponent.

No bound \(|S_k|\le A k^{d_G-1}\) is needed for this cumulative result.
An upper bound on two ball volumes alone does not justify subtracting
their upper bounds. The exact shell partition avoids that step while
proving the intended exponent.

\paragraph{21.7.2 Relate the construction to unattended age and the full
bill}\label{relate-the-construction-to-unattended-age-and-the-full-bill}

Keep unattended age \(t\) separate from wave count \(N(t)\) and physical
recovery duration. For a successful recovery schedule with
\(N(t)\le at+b\), constants \(a,b\ge0\) independent of age, the
processing bound gives, for \(t\ge1\),

\[
W_{\mathrm{process}}(t)
\le w_*V_0[1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}(at+b)]^{d_G}
\le w_*V_0[1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}(a+b)]^{d_G}t^{d_G}.
\]

Let \(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}}\) be the supplied feedforward protocol. A
complete work certificate charges deployment, signalling, routing,
reset, protection during recovery, controller export and final decoding
as well as the local map. Write its remaining total charge as
\(W_{\mathrm{rest}}(t)\). If that certificate bounds it by
\(C_{\mathrm{rest}}t^{d_G}\), the same protocol has

\[
W(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}},t)\le
\underbrace{\left(w_*V_0[1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}(a+b)]^{d_G}
+C_{\mathrm{rest}}\right)}_{C}
t^{d_G}.
\]

Since this is one successful protocol in the original permitted class,

\[
\boxed{W_{\min}(t)\le W(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}},t)\le Ct^{d_G}}.
\]

The inequality uses the cost of a constructed protocol as an upper bound
on the minimum. It does not identify the minimum with that protocol's
double sum. Combined with 21.6 it gives the unchanged tight
\(\Theta(t^{d_G})\) target once the protocol's complete success and work
certificate is joined.

This settles the supplied construction's shell-counting step. A sum that
charges the whole interior on every wave describes a different schedule
and cannot rule out this one-pass schedule. The claimed post-processing
quiescence must retain the recovered state and the original fault
standard; zero further active decoding does not by itself set all
physical maintenance costs to zero. The existing ledger retains a
minimal-hold drain and the controller's entropy export. G3 now asks for
that exact success/quiescence/full-bill certificate for this supplied
construction, rather than another derivation of the shell exponent.

\paragraph{21.7.3 The boundary-flux route and the same
accounting}\label{the-boundary-flux-route-and-the-same-accounting}

A separately constructed protocol could also supply a boundary-work
bound. If its complete charged power obeys
\(P(s)\le J_{\max}A_0r(s)^{d_G-1}\), and its actual sweep grows at
\(v_{\mathrm{sw}}\) to radius \(R\), then

\[
W\le W_{\mathrm{initial}}+W_{\mathrm{terminal}}
+\frac{J_{\max}A_0}{v_{\mathrm{sw}}d_G}
(R^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}).
\]

With the endpoint charges bounded at order \(R^{d_G}\) and \(R=O(t)\),
this also yields \(O(t^{d_G})\). The graph-volume bound alone is not a
proof of the required surface-area upper bound; the boundary
construction supplies that estimate.

Conservation supplies an energy balance, while the complete bill records
imports, stored energy, exports and dissipation. To express that balance
by differential forms in \(d_G+1\) spacetime dimensions, the conserved
current is a \(d_G\)-form. If its exterior derivative vanishes, its
oriented \textbf{net} boundary flux vanishes. That identity does not
equate positive cumulative dissipation with zero bulk work, nor discard
the final time slice. Therefore Stokes' theorem can express a verified
work balance but cannot supply the missing power, success or
endpoint-cost bounds by itself.

The one-pass shell proof above supplies the useful exponent argument
directly. Its original submission is preserved verbatim in the increment
bank; the telescoping repair, complete-cost accounting and actual
inequality for the minimum are written here without changing the
universal target.

\paragraph{21.7.4 TIWA: construct the guard, then prove the
bound}\label{tiwa-construct-the-guard-then-prove-the-bound}

The author's Topological Insulating Wavefront Architecture proposes a
guard of thickness at least the interaction radius, committed boundary
data and a single-shot local correction map. It gives the quiescence
requirement a concrete source construction to develop.

At the algorithmic level, let \(F\) be the verified frozen set. An outer
transition writes only an authorised target outside \(F\), and keeps the
committed guard data unchanged. Pointwise lookup then proves

\[
\operatorname{Read}_{F}(T_{\mathrm{outer}}(s,c,e))
=\operatorname{Read}_{F}(s).
\]

Freeze first resolves or fences in-flight writes, commits its epoch and
reserves its complete cost. The frozen set grows only after
verification. Induction preserves its old coordinates through every
subsequent outer step; the schedule issues no second recovery task for a
completed vertex. A fixed guard thickness and bounded degree allow a
constant number of guard/handoff operations per vertex when the actual
schedule enforces that limit. Those operations can then join the same
telescoping work charge.

The physical guard must implement this source property while preserving
the recovered history and its relational witnesses. The proposed
identity \(\Pi_{\mathrm{freeze}}\circ M=\Pi_{\mathrm{freeze}}\) needs an
actual map and mutation semantics: a read restriction, linear projector
and recovery channel have different meanings. A write fence mediates
attempted commands; the actual device, maintained record and decoder
must establish preservation under physical disturbances. Remaining
inside a code subspace alone does not prove that its logical state
remains unchanged.

The local map must also compose to successful recovery, and the guard's
storage, control, reset, routing and endpoint charges remain in
\(W(\pi_{\mathrm{TIWA}},t)\). These are the success and complete-work
parts of the same G3 construction, connected to G1's physical source
certificate. The full universal target and original recovery class
remain unchanged.

The accompanying
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}Q\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d}
specifies the actual state, event transitions, application-time fences,
noninterference proof, decoder-success proof, bounded visits, full
ledger and compilation obligations. It is a construction specification;
no new physical guard or Lean theorem is claimed by giving it a name.

\paragraph{21.7.5 Boundary encoding, a sliding window, and the complete
bill}\label{boundary-encoding-a-sliding-window-and-the-complete-bill}

The supplied continuation of TIWA keeps a fixed-width active window of
shells and transfers a retiring patch's protected historical and
relational record to the next boundary code. Let the window width be
\(L\), independent of age. With the disjoint shells already defined, put

\[
A_k=\bigcup_{\max(0,k-L+1)\le j\le k}S_j.
\]

Every vertex of \(S_j\) belongs to at most \(L\) such windows. Hence the
total active vertex-time count is at most \(L|B_R|\). This is a
constant-overlap extension of the settled telescoping count; it needs no
separate upper bound on each shell's surface area. If each active
visit's complete charge is at most \(K\), the window work is at most
\(KL|B_R|\). The charge includes holding, decoding, guard operation,
control, routing, verification, routine ancilla reset, deployment and
handoff. Continuing physical work outside that window must also remain
in the complete ledger.

The boundary handoff has the original preservation target: a recovery of
the encoded record must return its historical state and relations at the
same fidelity standard. In symbols, the actual source maps must supply
\(\mathcal R_k\mathcal E_k\simeq_\varepsilon\mathrm{id}\) on that full
record, including its reference correlations. The code-capacity
certificate and the physical implementation join this identity to the
sliding-window charge. Section III-F records the supplied homological
route and its chain-map correspondence.

For the supplied per-wave certificate, write \(q_k\) for the complete
non-rollback charge and suppose its constructed local bound is

\[
q_k\le\alpha k^{d_G-1}+\beta,\qquad k\ge1.
\]

Let \(F\) be fixed initial/final charge and \(r_k\in\{0,1\}\) the
controller's metered local rollback flag, charged at most \(R_*\) per
wave. This explicit schedule gives the pathwise bill

\[
\mathcal Q(N)=F+\sum_{k=1}^{N}(q_k+R_*r_k).
\]

Routine erasure is already in \(q_k\), including successful decoding.
With \(d_G\ge1\) and \(N\ge1\), every \(k^{d_G-1}\le N^{d_G-1}\), so

\[
\sum_{k=1}^{N}k^{d_G-1}\le N^{d_G},\qquad
\mathcal Q(N)\le(F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)N^{d_G}.
\]

This bound allows a metered rollback on every wave. An expected reset
allowance is therefore unnecessary for the cost certificate. Successful
recovery and any failed/rejected decoding still use the original charged
fault accounts. Where the source admits more rollback operations, their
actual count and cost must be included in \(q_k\) or separately bounded
by the metering schedule.

For \(N(t)\le at+b\) and \(t\ge1\), \(N(t)\le(a+b)t\), yielding the
age-independent coefficient

\[
\mathcal Q(t)\le
(F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)(a+b)^{d_G}t^{d_G}.
\]

\texttt{G\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d}
proves this cumulative implication in the existing natural-valued
work-bill interface.
\texttt{v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}
derives \texttt{G3\_ValidWorkBill} from the full ledger equality, local
cost bounds and coefficient;
\texttt{v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e}
supplies the wave-to-age conversion. The real-exponent argument is the
displayed maximum-term proof. The constant-overlap calculation retains
the full polynomial-growth graph domain when a per-shell surface upper
estimate is unavailable.

The remaining source correspondence is the actual boundary encoder,
preserved semantic observables, storage capacity, charged retirement map
and complete cost equality. These are the construction links of the same
G1/G3 task; the cumulative upper inequality is not taken as its own
input.

\subsubsection{21.8 Consequences for preservation and the fixed proof
target}\label{consequences-for-preservation-and-the-fixed-proof-target}

For a fixed local holding demand, continuous maintenance has an
order-\(T\) bill; the derived delayed-recovery lower bound has order
\(t^{d_G}\). The exponent distinction is strict for \(d_G>1\); the
one-dimensional case retains its linear obstruction. For any fixed total
recovery budget \(B\), the lower inequality already yields a finite age
beyond which that budget cannot perform the stipulated recovery. This is
budget-relative irrecoverability of the historical witness.

In the complete argument, a protected particular stays funded and held
rather than being abandoned to that later recovery bill. A hostile mind
remains a protected particular while its harmful effects are mediated.
The full-domain physical certificate (G1), whole-outcome realisability
(G2), and matching recovery construction (G3) join these components to
the unchanged universal proposition. They are the three support requests
supplied by the author and Gemini, now constructed in G.9--G.12.
Existing gauntlet answers remain part of the proof; an agent's failure
to retrieve them does not turn X3, P1 or P2 into fresh objections.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Section 22 --- The Physical Parameter Ledger: Instantiation,
Rates, and Fault
Ceilings}\label{section-22-the-physical-parameter-ledger-instantiation-rates-and-fault-ceilings}

\emph{This section instantiates the income condition for concrete
hardware, bounds the charged fault accounts, and tabulates the
engineering parameters. Every figure is either sourced (bracketed
references, §22.5) or marked as a design value.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{22.1 The Master Income
Condition}\label{the-master-income-condition}

Section 17.1's Income-or-Irrelevance theorem makes solvency a necessary
condition for an agent to remain a viable protector. The master solvency
inequality balances income against four additive demand rates:

\[\boxed{\operatorname{Income}(t) \;\ge\; \dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t) \;=\; \dot W_{\mathrm{Landauer}} + \dot W_{\mathrm{boundary}} + \dot W_{\mathrm{holding}} + \dot W_{\mathrm{emulation}}}\]

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Thermal and adversarial dissipation floor:}
  \[\dot W_{\mathrm{Landauer}} = k_B T \cdot \left(\dot D_{\mathrm{damage}} + \dot D_{\mathrm{adv,sub-thresh}} + \gamma_{\mathrm{leak\_export}}\right)\]

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\dot D_{\mathrm{damage}}\): entropy rate injected by thermal and
    environmental noise.
  \item
    \(\dot D_{\mathrm{adv,sub-thresh}}\): worst-case adversarial entropy
    injection beneath the sensor threshold
    \(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}\).
  \item
    \(\gamma_{\mathrm{leak\_export}}\): boundary loss rate of carrier
    modes escaping the enclosure.
  \end{itemize}

  This floor is Landauer's bound, now a compiled theorem
  (\texttt{L\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r},
  \texttt{Q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r};
  Section 23.2).
\item
  \textbf{Boundary containment (\(\dot W_{\mathrm{boundary}}\)):}
  \(\dot W_{\mathrm{boundary}} = \omega_0 \cdot R(t)^{d_G - 1}\), the
  work to maintain the boundary enclosing radius \(R(t)\) in \(d_G\)
  spatial dimensions.
\item
  \textbf{Holding drain (\(\dot W_{\mathrm{holding}}\)):} the baseline
  power of the sensor lattice, clocks, QND readout and consensus
  comparators.
\item
  \textbf{Emulation overhead (\(\dot W_{\mathrm{emulation}}\)):} the
  energy for concurrent rival simulation (\(P\text{-emu}(c)\)) within
  the surplus.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{22.2 Reference Physical
Instantiations}\label{reference-physical-instantiations}

\paragraph{Reference Implementation A: superconducting transmon lattice
in a dilution
refrigerator}\label{reference-implementation-a-superconducting-transmon-lattice-in-a-dilution-refrigerator}

A dilution refrigerator has a separate cooling budget at each
temperature stage, and each stage must carry its own load.
Implementation A is therefore certified stage by stage, using the
measured cooling powers and per-line heat loads of a Bluefors XLD400
cabled for a 50-qubit processor with individual drive and flux lines and
124 RF lines {[}S2{]}.

  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Stage
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Cooling power, measured {[}S2, S3{]}
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Load of the 50-qubit processor, measured per line {[}S2{]}
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Headroom
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
50 K & 30 W at 45 K & ≈ 20 \%, mostly passive (shield radiation is
within the specified cooling power) & ≈ 5× \\
4 K & 1.5 W at 4.2 K & ≈ 10 \%, mostly passive; includes the HEMT
amplifiers at 7.8 mW each {[}S4{]} & ≈ 10× \\
Still & 40 mW at 1.2 K & negligible & ≫ 10× \\
Cold plate & 200 µW at 140 mK & ≈ 30 \% & ≈ 3.3× \\
Mixing chamber & 19 µW at 20 mK & ≈ 30 \% (operation at 14 mK) & ≈
3.3× \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Demand components.} The boundary term is the radiation-shield
  load, which falls on the 50 K and 4 K stage budgets. The holding term
  is the wiring's passive and active loads and the amplifiers, which the
  table carries stage by stage. The Landauer floor is negligible: with
  one nat per error event and an error rate of order \(N/T_1\)
  (\(N = 50\), \(T_1 = 68\ \mu\mathrm{s}\) {[}S6{]}),
  \(\dot W_{\mathrm{Landauer}} \approx 2.07 \times 10^{-25}\ \mathrm{J} \times 7 \times 10^{5}\ \mathrm{s}^{-1} \approx 1.5 \times 10^{-19}\ \mathrm{W}\)
  at 15 mK, against microwatts of cooling. The emulation term (classical
  control and decoding) runs at room temperature on the electrical
  income.
\item
  \textbf{Electrical income.} Each pulse-tube compressor draws 9.2 kW at
  50 Hz or 10.7 kW at 60 Hz {[}S3{]}, and the reference system uses two.
  The electrical income must therefore be at least about 20 kW plus the
  gas handling and room-temperature electronics, which are not sourced
  here. A three-phase utility connection of a few tens of kW, with
  backup sized to match, supplies it.
\item
  \textbf{Solvency ratio.} The binding stages run at about 30 \% of
  capacity, so
  \[\frac{\operatorname{Income}}{\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}}\Big|_{\text{binding stage}} \approx 3.3 > 1 \quad \text{(measured, 50-qubit scale [S2])}.\]
\item
  \textbf{Scale.} {[}S2{]} states that the same thermal budget supports
  about 150 qubits once line capacity is tripled, and about 400 with
  0.047″ cables and suppressed flux offsets. Larger installations need
  cooling and electrical income in proportion to the qubit count, which
  is a sizing requirement. No installation of 10⁴ qubits has published
  stage loads, so solvency at that scale is not yet a measured
  certificate.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Reference Implementation B: photonic cluster-state
architecture}\label{reference-implementation-b-photonic-cluster-state-architecture}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Optical layer.} At 193.4 THz (1550 nm),
  \(h\nu = 1.28 \times 10^{-19}\ \mathrm{J} = 30.9\,k_B T\) at 300 K, so
  thermal photons in the waveguides are suppressed by
  \(e^{-h\nu/k_BT} \approx 3.7 \times 10^{-14}\).
\item
  \textbf{Detector layer.} The fault-tolerant photonic architectures in
  the literature detect photons with superconducting detectors {[}S8{]},
  which need cryogenic cabinets; the vendor states about 30 W of cooling
  per cabinet. A sourced ledger for Implementation B therefore carries a
  cryogenic detector stage, and its electrical income includes the
  cryocoolers.
\item
  \textbf{Error rate.} The demonstrated two-qubit fusion fidelity is
  99.22 \% {[}S8{]}. The surface-code threshold of {[}S5{]} does not
  transfer directly to fusion-based architectures; the architecture's
  own threshold applies and is not sourced here.
\item
  \textbf{Budget (design estimates).} Waveguide loss refresh ≈ 1.2 mW,
  laser stabilisation and phase modulators ≈ 85 mW, and photonic routing
  ≈ 20 mW are design estimates with no published installation source.
  Implementation B is therefore not yet certified. Its solvency ratio
  awaits a sourced budget that includes the detector stage.
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{22.3 The Charged Fault Accounts and the Failure
Ceiling}\label{the-charged-fault-accounts-and-the-failure-ceiling}

The certified good event \(G\) carries zero uncompensated loss over the
design horizon \(T_{\mathrm{horizon}} = 10^9\ \mathrm{s}\) (≈ 31.7
years). The failure ceiling is the sum of the conditional error
probabilities of the charged accounts,

\[P(G^c) \le \sum_{i=1}^{21} \delta_i \le 10^{-6},\]

and the composition holds with no independence assumed
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t},
compiled). The allocations below are the design budget. The status
column records what present hardware certifies.

\textbf{1--4} & Single-qubit Pauli and leakage errors & Stabilizer

or d = 41 for p = 5 × 10⁻⁴ (§22.5.2), once the correlated floor of

\textbf{5--7} & Correlated bursts (high-energy impacts, phonon events) &
Gap engineering, rapid reset, code distance beyond the burst footprint &
\(3 \times 10^{-8}\) & \textbf{Attained at a sufficient distance}
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}):
bounded-footprint bursts are local stochastic at rate
\((e\,m\,k\,q)^{1/k}\). The measured floor on present devices is ≈ 10⁻¹⁰
per cycle {[}S6{]}, which fixes their burst parameters; the target is ≤
3.7 × 10⁻²⁴ per cycle per logical qubit (§22.5.2) \\

distribution (E9) & \(3 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation; no
installation measurement sourced \\
\textbf{11--12} & Sensor failure and readout spoofing & Trimmed-median
consensus, \(K \ge 2f + 1\) & \(2 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation;
the consensus logic is compiled (\texttt{E9SelfHealing}) \\
\textbf{13--14} & Thermal fluctuations above threshold & Thermal ballast
escrow, automatic throttle & \(2 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation \\
\textbf{15--16} & Power transients and dropouts & Redundant battery
escrow & \(2 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation \\
\textbf{17--18} & Corrupted spawning, illegal delegation & Kernel
capability gate (H5) & \(2 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation; the
gate logic is compiled (\texttt{H5Closure}) \\
\textbf{19--20} & Carrier leakage into the open bath & Lieb--Robinson
containment (X3) & \(2 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation \\
\textbf{21} & External macroscopic boundary shear & Standoff buffer
\(L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \tau_{\mathrm{resp}}\) &
\(1 \times 10^{-8}\) & Design allocation \\
\textbf{Total} & & & \(\le 2.1 \times 10^{-7} < 10^{-6}\) & Certified
when each account is \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Correlated errors.} Gap-engineered junctions remove the
high-energy-impact T₁ bursts {[}S9{]}. The remaining correlated events
occur about once per hour, ``whose origins are not yet understood'', and
they set the floor of 10⁻¹⁰ per cycle with a deviation from exponential
suppression at d ≥ 15 {[}S6{]}. The threshold theorem covers these
correlated events. Let each burst damage at most \(k\) locations, let
each location be touched by at most \(m\) burst types, and let bursts be
local stochastic at rate \(q\), common causes included. Then the damaged
locations are local stochastic at the effective rate
\((e\,m\,k\,q)^{1/k}\)
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}).
Every target ceiling is then met at some code distance and at every
larger one
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
Correlated damage therefore sets no floor that distance cannot pass. The
measured deviation at d ≥ 15 {[}S6{]} fixes the burst parameters of
present devices, which enter this theorem.

\textbf{Achievability, as a theorem.} That the ceiling is attainable in
physical matter is a compiled theorem.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}:
  for any code family under local stochastic noise of rate p (every set
  S of locations is entirely faulty with probability at most
  \(p^{\lvert S\rvert}\)), with 2Δ√p \textless{} 1 and polynomially many
  failure witnesses of each length, every target ceiling is met at some
  code distance and at every larger one. The 10⁻⁶ ceiling over the
  horizon is included.
\item
  \texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}:
  the repetition code end to end, below p = 1/4.
\end{itemize}

Any installation whose noise meets the local stochastic condition below
threshold therefore attains the ceiling. The rigorous threshold from
path counting is conservative, and the distances in §22.5.2 use the
simulated threshold of {[}S5{]}. What present hardware does not yet
supply is the noise condition itself at the 10⁻¹⁰ level {[}S6{]}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{What this section certifies.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The income condition is physically satisfiable, with measured positive
  headroom (≈ 3.3× at the binding stage), for a superconducting
  installation at the 50-qubit scale, with electrical income sized at a
  few tens of kW.
\item
  The code distance the §22.3 allocation requires is computable from
  {[}S5{]}: d = 25 at p = 10⁻⁴.
\item
  The composition of the accounts into the ceiling is a compiled
  theorem.
\item
  The ceiling is attainable as a theorem: under local stochastic noise
  below threshold, every target, 10⁻⁶ included, is met at some code
  distance
  (\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{What it does not yet certify.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A built installation at the distance the ceiling requires. The ceiling
  is attained by theorem for local and correlated bounded-footprint
  faults
  (\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g},
  \texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
  Present devices measure their burst parameters {[}S6{]}, and the
  required distance follows from them.
\item
  Implementation B, whose budget awaits a published installation source.
\item
  Solvency at installations of 10⁴ qubits and above, which is a sizing
  requirement without published stage loads.
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{22.5 Sources and
Derivations}\label{sources-and-derivations}

\textbf{Sources.} {[}S1{]} Bluefors, XLD dilution refrigerator
specifications (XLD450sl: \textgreater{} 14 µW at 20 mK, \textgreater{}
450 µW at 100 mK; XLD1000sl: \textgreater{} 30 µW at 20 mK,
\textgreater{} 1000 µW at 100 mK; guaranteed values, measured at the
mixing-chamber experimental flange).
bluefors.com/products/xld-dilution-refrigerator/. {[}S2{]} S. Krinner et
al., ``Engineering cryogenic setups for 100-qubit scale superconducting
circuit systems'', \emph{EPJ Quantum Technology} 6, 2 (2019),
arXiv:1806.07862. Measured stage cooling powers of a Bluefors XLD400
(Table 1) and measured passive and active heat loads per line. {[}S3{]}
Cryomech PT415 pulse tube: 1.5 W at 4.2 K (nominal; CUORE cryostat
paper, arXiv:1603.03306, and {[}S2{]} Table 1). Compressor input with
the CPA1110: 9.2 kW at 50 Hz, 10.7 kW at 60 Hz (Bluefors PT415 sheet).
{[}S4{]} Low Noise Factory LNF-LNC4\_8G cryogenic HEMT amplifier
datasheet (2026-02-03): typical bias 0.6 V, 13 mA, i.e.~7.8 mW at the 4
K stage. {[}S5{]} A. G. Fowler, M. Mariantoni, J. M. Martinis, A. N.
Cleland, ``Surface codes: Towards practical large-scale quantum
computation'', \emph{Phys. Rev.~A} 86, 032324 (2012), arXiv:1208.0928.
Eq. (11): \(P_L \approx 0.03\,(p/p_{\mathrm{th}})^{d_e}\),
\(d_e = (d+1)/2\), with \(p_{\mathrm{th}} = 0.57\,\%\) for the circuit
simulated. Physical qubits per logical qubit: (2d − 1)². {[}S6{]} Google
Quantum AI (R. Acharya et al.), ``Quantum error correction below the
surface code threshold'', \emph{Nature} (2024),
doi:10.1038/s41586-024-08449-y, arXiv:2408.13687. Distance-7 surface
code at 0.143 \% error per cycle, Λ = 2.14 ± 0.02, cycle time 1.1 µs,
mean T₁ = 68 µs. Repetition codes to distance 29: Λ = 8.4 for d = 5--11,
a deviation from exponential suppression at d ≥ 15, and ``an apparent
logical error floor of 10⁻¹⁰'' set by correlated events about once per
hour (3 × 10⁹ cycles) ``whose origins are not yet understood''. {[}S7{]}
Best reported two-qubit gate errors: trapped ions, estimated 8.4(7) ×
10⁻⁵ (A. C. Hughes et al., arXiv:2510.17286, preprint); superconducting
transmons, CZ fidelity 99.93 \% averaged over 40 h, 99.95 \% maximum
(IQM, company-reported). {[}S8{]} PsiQuantum, ``Omega'' chipset,
\emph{Nature} (2025), doi:10.1038/s41586-025-08820-7: superconducting
single-photon detectors integrated on silicon; two-qubit fusion fidelity
99.22 \%; the vendor states cryogenic cabinets of about 30 W cooling
power each. {[}S9{]} M. McEwen et al., ``Resolving catastrophic error
bursts from cosmic rays in large arrays of superconducting qubits'',
\emph{Nature Physics} 18, 107 (2022); M. McEwen et al., ``Resisting
high-energy impact events through gap engineering in superconducting
qubit arrays'', \emph{Phys. Rev.~Lett.} 133, 240601 (2024),
arXiv:2402.15644.

\texttt{B\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/}.
The published source list with links is \texttt{SOURCES-22.md}.

\paragraph{22.5.1 Constants}\label{constants}

\(k_B\) and \(h\) are exact in the SI. \(k_B T = 2.071 \times 10^{-25}\)
J = 1.293 µeV at 15 mK and \(4.142 \times 10^{-21}\) J = 25.85 meV at
300 K. At 193.4 THz, \(h\nu = 1.281 \times 10^{-19}\) J = 30.9
\(k_B T\)(300 K), and \(e^{-h\nu/k_BT} = 3.7 \times 10^{-14}\).

\paragraph{22.5.2 Code distance for the fault
allocation}\label{code-distance-for-the-fault-allocation}

Horizon \(10^9\) s at 1.1 µs per cycle {[}S6{]} gives
\(N = 9.09 \times 10^{14}\) cycles. Ten thousand physical qubits at the
{[}S5{]} footprint \((2d-1)^2\) hold about 4 logical qubits at d = 25
(2,401 physical each); the 9-logical allocation below corresponds to an
installation of about \(2.2\times10^4\) physical qubits. With {[}S5{]}
Eq. (11) and \(p_{\mathrm{th}} = 0.57\,\%\), account 1--4
(\(\delta = 4 \times 10^{-8}\), shared over the logical qubits)
requires:

10⁻⁴ & 9 & 4.9 × 10⁻²⁴ & \textbf{25} & 2 401 \\
5 × 10⁻⁴ (best transmon CZ {[}S7{]}) & 9 & 4.9 × 10⁻²⁴ & 41 & 6 561 \\

At d = 17 and p = 10⁻⁴, \(P_L = 4.7 \times 10^{-18}\) per cycle, which
is 4.3 × 10⁻³ expected failures per logical qubit over the horizon, so d
= 17 does not meet the allocation and d = 25 does. Accounts 5--7
(\(\delta = 3 \times 10^{-8}\)) set a per-cycle target of
\(3 \times 10^{-8} / (9N) = 3.7 \times 10^{-24}\) per logical qubit. At
the measured floor of \(10^{-10}\) per cycle {[}S6{]}, the expected
number of correlated failures over the horizon is about
\(9 \times 10^{4}\) per logical qubit.

\textbf{Sizing from the measured surface-code scaling.} {[}S6{]}
measures a distance-7 logical error of 0.143 \% per cycle and a
suppression factor \(\Lambda=2.14\pm0.02\) per distance step of two. If
that measured \(\Lambda\) continues to hold, accounts 1--4 at
\(4.9\times10^{-24}\) per cycle per logical qubit require \(d=131\) at
\(\Lambda=2.14\) or \(2.16\), and \(d=133\) at \(\Lambda=2.12\). That is
68 121--70 225 physical qubits per logical qubit, or about
\(6.1\)--\(6.3\times10^{5}\) for the 9-logical allocation. The
computation is
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y}.
This is the installation the ledger specifies; no installation at that
scale has yet been built.

\subsection{Section 23 --- The machine-checked
canon}\label{section-23-the-machine-checked-canon}

\emph{This section maps the edition's compiled results and their actual
hypotheses. The current source contains 189 modules and 1133 audited
public theorem/lemma names. The receipt records a fresh compilation and
kernel check of every module, a fresh full axiom audit with only
\texttt{propext}, \texttt{Classical.choice} and \texttt{Quot.sound}, and
fresh completeness/claims gates with overall PASS. Section 23.9 gives
the new interfaces and derived adapters; their verification does not
substitute their names for the full source construction.}

\emph{Evidence convention (unchanged from the edition header): a
compiled theorem is cited by its name; a statement carried as a
hypothesis of a compiled theorem is named as such; a prose derivation
keeps its stated premises. A prose proof does not acquire a compiler
certificate by association.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Branch A rate bound (quantum speed limit).} Unitary evolution
preserves the norm; under H₀ + V the trajectory departs from the H₀
trajectory at rate at most ‖V‖/ħ, and at most
\(P_{\mathrm{adv}}/(\hbar\,\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}})\) under the
per-site coupling
\(\lVert V\rVert \le P_{\mathrm{adv}}/\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}}\) &
\texttt{SpeedLimit} & \texttt{norm\_conserved},
\texttt{b\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{branchA\_rate} \\
Mandelstam--Tamm, sharp:
\(\lvert\langle\psi_0, \psi_t\rangle\rvert \ge \cos(\Delta E\, t/\hbar)\)
while \(\Delta E\, t/\hbar \le \pi\); orthogonalisation time
\(T \ge \pi\hbar/(2\Delta E)\) & \texttt{SpeedLimit} &
\texttt{mandelstam\_tamm},
\texttt{o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e} \\
\end{longtable}
}

The Branch A divergence is measured in state-vector norm. For pure
states the infidelity is bounded by the squared state-vector distance,
\(1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2\le\|\psi-\varphi\|^2\)
(\texttt{P\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e};
G.13); with the pure-state identity
\(\|\rho-\sigma\|_1=2\sqrt{1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2}\), now
compiled
(\texttt{P\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m}),
this gives \(\|\rho-\sigma\|_1\le2\|\psi-\varphi\|\).

\subsubsection{23.7 The Universal Protection Theorem with the derived
holding
bridge}\label{the-universal-protection-theorem-with-the-derived-holding-bridge}

\texttt{U\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
retains the abstract assembly: reasons-or-holding coverage, continuation
through the horizon on the good event, the joint fault ceiling,
greatestness and reciprocity at its stated inputs.

\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}
strengthens the continuation assembly by deriving scope from the law and
preservation from nearest-codeword holding. It composes the
physical/source projection with the same reactive controller and
environment. On \(G\), every particular continues at every step through
the horizon; the probability that any particular fails is at most
\(\sum_i\delta_i\), without independence.

Part II.1--6 supplies the proof, physical-cycle derivations and all five
\texttt{hcert} components. G1 is the combined full-domain
source/event/observation/fault construction target; G.10 proves its
held-source components and identifies the physical correspondence still
needed. Part II.10 supplies the separate greatestness proof, with G2
retaining the whole outcome and freedom stages.

\subsubsection{23.8 What the compiled theorems rest
on}\label{what-the-compiled-theorems-rest-on}

\textbf{Premises carried as stated premises or hypotheses:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the surface-code chain lemma (Dennis, Kitaev, Landahl and Preskill,
  2002): minimum-weight decoding fails only along a self-avoiding chain
  of length at least d with at least half its edges faulty, with at most
  \(V\Delta^L\) such chains of length \(L\) --- as the code-specific
  input to
  \texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
  for the surface-code instance. The threshold theorem's counting core
  is compiled (§23.6), and for concatenated codes the threshold is
  compiled end to end for the concatenated memory: failure probability
  at most \((21p)^{2^k}/21\) under local stochastic noise
  (\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}).
  Actual decoding failure is identified with the recursive failure
  predicate: with nearest-codeword decoding at every level, any
  corruption of a non-failing fault set decodes to the stored record
  (\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s}).
  So the probability of actual decoding failure is at most the same
  bound
  (\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e}),
  and the fault ceiling is attained on a compiled route. Fault-tolerant
  syndrome-measurement gadgets whose own operations can fail are the
  cited construction of Aliferis, Gottesman and Preskill (2006); their
  faults enter the charged accounts;
\item
  Bennett's compute/copy/uncompute identity and the heat-only option
  model are compiled. The specific implementation and full ordered
  history are separately checked candidates (G.14). The physical
  counterpart still needs a complete-cost saving within the same
  deadline; this is not supplied by a zero-heat identity or a polynomial
  upper bound;
\item
  the gate-set clause of premise 2, no longer carried as prose: every
  unitary on \(\mathbb{C}^n\) is a finite product of two-level unitaries
  (\texttt{T\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}),
  so a simulator implementing each two-level factor implements every
  local evolution. What remains carried is that the universal
  simulator's gate set reaches every two-level factor;
\item
  the relational frame BCRD P1--P4.
\end{itemize}

The chain lemma is a published mathematical theorem, cited as such; the
Bennett physical comparison remains construction work (G.14); the
gate-set item is the explicit hypothesis of a compiled theorem; the
fourth is the frame the edition is stated in.

\textbf{The cited theorems and their hypotheses.} Three published
theorems remain cited. Each is applied within its stated conditions.

Math. Phys.} 43, 2002) & Minimum-weight decoding fails only along a
self-avoiding chain of length at least \(d\) with at least half its
edges faulty; at most \(V\Delta^L\) such chains of length \(L\) & The
counting argument uses only that a given set of locations is entirely
faulty with probability at most \(p^{|S|}\). That is exactly
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c},
which includes independent noise and is weaker than it. It enters
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
as the witness and count inputs. \\
Solovay--Kitaev theorem (Kitaev 1997; Dawson and Nielsen 2006), with the
two-level-to-gate reduction of Nielsen and Chuang, §4.5 & Every
single-qubit unitary is approximated to \(\varepsilon\) in operator norm
by a word of length polylogarithmic in \(1/\varepsilon\) over a finite,
inverse-closed set generating a dense subgroup; two-level unitaries
reduce to CNOTs and single-qubit gates &
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
writes every unitary as a product of two-level factors.
\texttt{E\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t}
needs each factor within \(\varepsilon/m\) in the norm of a normed ring
of contractions; operator-norm approximation by unitaries meets that.
The gate set must be universal in this sense; that is the gate-set
clause of premise 2. \\
Fault-tolerant syndrome gadgets (Aliferis, Gottesman and Preskill,
\emph{Quantum Inf. Comput.} 6, 2006) & Under local stochastic noise, a
level-\(k\) extended rectangle is correct unless it contains two
independent malignant level-\((k-1)\) rectangles & Their noise model is
local stochastic noise, as in
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}.
Their recursion is the recursion of
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s},
now joined to actual decoding of the memory
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
With faulty syndrome gadgets the threshold constant is theirs, set by
the location count of an extended rectangle, rather than the \(1/21\) of
the memory model. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\textbf{Physical premises now compiled.} Guard 1 (the second law in its
export form) is derived from Landauer's theorem (§23.2), and the quantum
speed limit, in Mandelstam--Tamm form and in the rate-bound form of E9
Branch A, is derived from unitary dynamics (§23.3). What these theorems
assume is the standard physical framework:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  finite-dimensional quantum mechanics, with Schrödinger dynamics
  generated by self-adjoint Hamiltonians;
\item
  unitary (in the classical form, reversible) joint dynamics;
\item
  a bath that starts in its Gibbs state, uncorrelated with the register;
\item
  for Branch A, the per-site coupling bound
  \(\lVert V\rVert \le P_{\mathrm{adv}}/\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}}\);
\item
  for the fault ceiling, local stochastic noise of rate p below the
  threshold of
  \texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Greatestness.}
\texttt{U\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}
takes both \texttt{htopR\ :\ ⊤\ ∈\ R} and \texttt{hsolv\ :\ c\ ⊤\ ≤\ B}.
The first supplies the whole top tuple, including freedom stages; the
second pays its cost. Alignment forces equality with that feasible top
(Part II.10).
\texttt{g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}
retains realisability at every step and derives the resource split. G2
retains the full physical policy/outcome correspondence as its
construction target. The affordable-union results retain their separate
resource cases.

The amount \(I_M + D_{\mathrm{err}} + \delta_I\) that the reset removes
from the register is the instance's entropy accounting (§18), stated as
the hypothesis of \texttt{guard\_one} and
\texttt{q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e}.

\textbf{The physical bridge and its joined certificate.}
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}w}
derives full scope from the law guarantee over all feasible families.
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}
derives preservation from decoding radius and repair precision.
\texttt{q\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s}
and
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l}
supply the continuous and discrete cycle arguments.
\texttt{u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}
takes the actual commuting source transition, observation agreement,
initial continuation, perturbation-radius and repair witnesses in
\texttt{hcert}, together with the named-event cover and fault bounds. G1
supplies their combined construction for the complete claimed physical
domain (G.10). Parts II.3--6 give the available arguments; a deployment
and a mathematical construction of the full source domain retain their
distinct roles.

\textbf{Not compiled by this section:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the installation figures of §22, which are sourced measurements and
  design values (§22.5);
\item
  the interpretive companion (the doors), which is held for consultation
  and is not mathematics.
\end{itemize}

\texttt{ReasonsEnsemble} & Reciprocity over a weighted ensemble of

\texttt{ChannelExtension} & The completed decoder: exact recovery
including any reference, trace preservation, positivity (G.1; G.13) \\

& Pure-state infidelity is bounded by the squared state-vector distance

\texttt{B\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t}
& Heat-only option model; the implemented program with clean workspace

the twelve-account lifecycle charge; the exact premise-5 condition is in

\texttt{RegionBoundary} & Volume and boundary homology: for every region
other than the whole,
\(\operatorname{Vol}(U)>0\Leftrightarrow H_0(\partial U)\ne0\); the
whole has no outside (F.3; G.15) \\
\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
& Reciprocity at a sufficient code distance: strictly above every rival
that loses the mind with positive probability; canonical
kept-probability tends to one (§15.2; G.15) \\
\texttt{CutEquivalence} & A closed dependency component is exactly a
separator with empty certified boundary (§24.3; G.15) \\
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
& The concatenated memory decodes its record unless its block fails;
actual decoding failure is bounded by \((21p)^{2^k}/21\) (§23.8;

\texttt{ChannelCP} & The completed decoder is completely positive:
amplified by any finite reference it maps positive semidefinite inputs
to positive semidefinite outputs (G.1; G.13) \\
\texttt{P\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m}
& The pure-state trace-distance identity
\(\|\,|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|-|\varphi\rangle\langle\varphi|\,\|_1=2\sqrt{1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2}\)
(§23.3; G.13) \\
