& Timely care under reserved capacity; window demand within supply; only
certified decisions granted; observation-based winning (G.15) \\
\texttt{G1Refinement} & The installation's own guarded run protects
every particular on the good event, failure at most \(\sum_i\delta_i\),
with the journal retaining history; the speed limit removes the alarm
device from holding; every hypothesis has an inhabited instance
(G.16) \\
\texttt{G2Physical}, \texttt{G2Freedom} & The held installation realises
the continuation top, which dominates without union closure; holding and
timely authorised R17 delivery hold on one joint execution (G.16) \\
\texttt{G3GoodEvent}, \texttt{G3ArchiveStock} & The wavefront sweep
recovers on the good event with failure at most \(\sum_i\delta_i\);
archive holding is a separate bill term, charged on the held stock: live
stock gives \(\gamma(I_0+c)t^d\) for every \(d\ge1\), while retaining
every increment or rolling the whole stock over epochs is quadratic
(G.16) \\
\texttt{BennettExact} & Premise 5 with the implemented Bennett program
and complete lifecycle cost: forced inside the adiabatic window
\(0<Q-H\), \(A/(Q-H)<\tau_{\max}\), and outside it the counterpart is
not cheaper (G.17) \\
\texttt{KnillLaflamme} & Knill--Laflamme identities
\(PE_i^\dagger E_jP=\alpha_{ij}P\) for errors of weight \(t\) with
\(2t<d\), from the code distance; logical operators excluded by the
distance (G.18) \\
\texttt{MatchingWitness} & Minimum-weight decoding leaves every
zero-syndrome residual cycle at least half faulty: the witness
hypothesis of
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}
(G.18) \\
\texttt{EdgeLaw}, \texttt{ExRecBound} & Circuit faults induce local
stochastic edge noise at rate \(cp\); with the enumerated degree 12 and
\(c=52\), \(r=22\sqrt{52p}<1\) exactly when \(p<1/25168\); the gadget
bound \(|M_2|p^2+\binom{|L|}{3}p^3\) (G.18) \\
\texttt{AgeReliability} & A logarithmic distance schedule holds global
failure below \(\varepsilon\) at every age; a logarithmic overhead is
not absorbed into an age-independent constant (G.18) \\
\texttt{SparseFailure}, \texttt{ToleranceSweep} & Patches with disjoint
windows fail jointly with probability at most the product bound; many
failures are exponentially unlikely at every age; on the actual sweep
every wrong vertex is bad, the wrong count obeys the same tail, the bill
is pathwise and a tolerant outer decoder recovers the record (G.19) \\
\texttt{PassiveReference}, \texttt{StageSchedule} & Indefinite holding
at fixed ε: doubling-schedule stages cover every age, total failure at
most ε, rewrite cost O(log² t); with transfers, holding and transfer
failures sum to at most ε at polylog cost (G.19) \\
\texttt{RedundantRecord} & Majority decoding of a redundant record
tolerates any minority of wrong fragments, supplying the tolerance
hypothesis of \texttt{recovery\_tail} (G.19) \\
\texttt{BennettBatch} & Premise 5 is forced for every large enough batch
once the per-bit reversible overhead is below the irreversible heat
(G.19) \\
\texttt{WeightedEdgeLaw} & Weighted fault rates push forward to edge
rates; with the enumerated edge rate 94/15·p and walk bound 9.87, the
explicit threshold exceeds 4×10⁻⁴ (G.19) \\
\texttt{WarmStart} & A warm start, a prepared syndrome law at most C
times the thermal law pointwise, stays dominated at every time, so every
event keeps the stationary bound up to C (G.20) \\
\texttt{WalkBound9} & The length-9 walk bound c₉ = 763,504,745 ≤ 9.71⁹
gives an explicit threshold above 4.2×10⁻⁴ (G.20) \\
\end{longtable}
}

The earlier
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}
has conclusion \texttt{True}; it is a placeholder and is not the
protection theorem
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}.
The latter's full source certificate is constructed in G.10
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).
The exact integration history and dependency trace accompany this
working edition.

\subsection{23.A Complete theorem index}\label{a-complete-theorem-index}

Generated from the sources by

\subsection{Section 24 --- Statements of record for the retained
core}\label{section-24-statements-of-record-for-the-retained-core}

\emph{Part II supplies the retained proofs and stronger bridge in this
edition; Section 21 supplies the information--traffic--carrier--work
derivation. This section records concrete instrument rows and numerical
instances at their evidence tiers. The parent core, DOI
10.5281/zenodo.23599528, remains publication provenance; the current
argument is given here.}

\subsubsection{24.1 The nine-row A1 register (effect
mediation)}\label{the-nine-row-a1-register-effect-mediation}

\textbf{Statement of record.} The A1 register identifies nine rows of
effect mediation: host memory mapping, DMA, context switches, packet
ingestion, bus messages, storage synchronisation, commissioning,
delegation and retirement. Each row names its refinement carrier and the
enforcement witness the physical instance must supply. A packet or
memory operation acquires no exemption by being below the application
layer.

\emph{Tier:} stated definition, given in Part II.5 and the nine rows
above. The enforcement machinery it names is compiled in the abstract
(\texttt{H\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t},
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e});
the nine rows themselves are the instance inventory.

\subsubsection{24.2 Capacity rows 17 and 22, and the pool bill
(workload)}\label{capacity-rows-17-and-22-and-the-pool-bill-workload}

\textbf{Statement of record.} The workload pool bill is compiled:
capacity rows 17 and 22 at 48,000 and 67,200 per cycle give
\(48000 \times 17 + 67200 \times 22 = 2{,}294{,}400\) --- the per-cycle
pool bill against which the FIFO completion certificate (Proposition 6)
is issued
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e},
exact rationals; the same module derives the bill-saving ratios
\(6{,}604{,}800/2{,}294{,}400 = 688/239\) and
\(6{,}604{,}800/2{,}409{,}600 = 688/251\) of the earlier bill over the
corrected bills,
\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).

\emph{Tier:} compiled. The deadline-certificate machinery of which this
is the workload instance is published in the parent core (DOI
10.5281/zenodo.23599528, exact FIFO deadline certificates with
application-time finality and funding conservation).

\subsubsection{24.3 The causal boundary (reactive
refinement)}\label{the-causal-boundary-reactive-refinement}

\textbf{Statement of record.} The causal boundary is given in two forms,
which agree: a closed dependency component is exactly a directed
separator in the unrolled dependency graph with empty certified boundary
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s}),
and the separator form extends it to a matched certified boundary
(\texttt{U\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}).
Pending effects belong in that graph. Omitting one future effect or one
path invalidates the corresponding exclusion certificate.

\emph{Tier:} stated definition (R16-CUT); the reactive refinement that
uses it is compiled
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p},
with the causal boundary carried as the instance's refinement source).

\subsubsection{24.4 The revised JW2.6 arithmetic (conserved allocation
and joint
risk)}\label{the-revised-jw2.6-arithmetic-conserved-allocation-and-joint-risk}

\textbf{Statement of record.} The revised JW2.6 arithmetic gives
\(\approx 2.06419526094 \times 10^{-7}\), within the declared
\(10^{-6}\) horizon ceiling, under its source-event and stochastic-law
premises. The positive dormant allowance, recruitment noninterference
and the historical recruitment-family event retain their distinct
meanings. The three ledger scenarios carry this value (solvent and
quarantine) and the charged variant \(9.86419526094 \times 10^{-7}\)
(one priced event), each with a sealed, hash-chained trace.

\emph{Tier:} exact replay receipt ---
\texttt{L\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}G\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}K\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}0\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}/},
chain heads as printed in Chapter 1.4; the composition into the ceiling
(no independence) is compiled
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t});
the arithmetic itself is a receipt, not a theorem.

\subsubsection{24.5 The quorum (safety)}\label{the-quorum-safety}

\textbf{Statement of record.} The design instantiation is five-of-seven
with at most two Byzantine identities (\(K = 7\), \(f = 2\), satisfying
\(K \ge 2f + 1\)). The Byzantine quorum results it instantiates are
compiled
(\texttt{E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t},
\texttt{no\_false\_alarm}, \texttt{no\_masking},
\texttt{exists\_robust}); the five-of-seven values are instance
constants (§22.4's parameter matrix).

\emph{Tier:} compiled theorem with stated instance constants.

\section{Part III-C --- The three joined supports and their
constructions}\label{part-iii-c-the-three-joined-supports-and-their-constructions}

The proposition remains protection of all things from any digital mind,
especially a pure optimiser. X3, P1 and P2 retain their established
gauntlet arguments. These are the three support requests supplied by the
author and Gemini; their constructions are recorded below, with no
additional objection introduced by this incorporation.

\textbf{G1 --- combined full-domain physical certificate.} Construct the
actual source state, admitted commands/events, physical projection,
observations and charged fault accounts that jointly supply
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}.
Its \texttt{hcert} establishes commuting refinement, observation
agreement, initial continuation of every particular, the
perturbation-radius bound and repair precision on the named good event.
Also join the event cover and conditional fault bounds. Part II
incorporates the law-derived scope, code decoder, physical cycle,
controller-derived tracking, E9 branches, H5 care-preserving mediation,
R14/R16 source refinements and timely R17 effect construction. The
requested result joins these for the complete claimed physical domain.

Compiled support modules for this construction: \texttt{G1Certificate}
(placeholder with conclusion \texttt{True}), \texttt{FaultCover}
(bad-event cover), \texttt{G1Executable} (executable
admitted-command/event/observation pipeline), \texttt{PhysicalGuard}
(factorized evolution), \texttt{G1Complete} (inductive event-trace
compliance), and \texttt{G1FullDomain} (source domain, fault taxonomy,
exhaustive classification).

\emph{Constructed (G.10).}
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
proves all five \texttt{hcert} components for the canonical held source,
and \texttt{good\_cover} proves the cover of the good event by the named
accounts. \texttt{held\_source\_qsl} derives the per-step drift from the
quantum speed limit for any perturbing agent, and
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}9}
derives the coupling bound from the exhaustive E9 split. The journal of
G.8 preserves its supplied transition
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y});
protective refinement of the actual installation is the separate
transition equation identified in G.10. The inputs these theorems take
are physical: the frame's Schrödinger evolution and the sub-threshold
coupling bound in each step; the honest-sensor response and quorum size;
the response's removal of coupling once the alarm fires; and the account
probabilities, whose composition and attainment are
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t},
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
and
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}.

\textbf{G2 --- realisability of the whole greatest outcome.} Construct
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}⊤\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}∀\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{})\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}∈\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}R}
for the same full order, including existing and new continuation and
freedom stages, with the semantic continuation reading
\texttt{htopkeeps}. Keep it distinct from \texttt{hsolv\ :\ c\ ⊤\ ≤\ B}.
Part II.10 supplies the algebra from those inputs to equality of every
aligned option with the top and dominance over every rival. The
construction retains the full tuple.

First compiled support module for this construction:
\texttt{TopRealisability} (union-closed realisability of singletons
yields realisability of the top outcome).

Compiled support modules for this construction: \texttt{G2Singleton}
(coverage discharge of singleton realisability), \texttt{G2Outcome}
(transactional outcome-tuple invariance), and \texttt{G2WholeOutcome}
(authorized-implies-closed, authorized-implies-positive-work).

\emph{Compiled components (G.9, G.11).} \texttt{G2Splice} proves
product-model union closure and closed-block agreement. \texttt{G2Held}
proves the top of its assigned grade model from held continuation and
its one-step H5 predicate. The full construction must map actual
policies and outcomes to that model, preserving all original
continuation and freedom stages, shared constraints and R17 delivery for
every admitted rival and world. \texttt{hsolv} remains the separate
complete funded-cost certificate.

\textbf{G3 --- supplied wavefront/TIWA protocol: success and the
complete work certificate.} The author has supplied the single-shot
feedforward wavefront construction and the TIWA guard-ring proposal.
Section 21.7.4 and
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}Q\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}d}
specify the latter's source, physical and work obligations. Section 21.7
proves its processing count exactly by disjoint shells:
\(\sum_{k=0}^N|S_k|=|B_{vN}|\), hence bounded work per processed vertex
gives \(O(N^{d_G})\). The same construction must now supply its actual
recovery success and retained-state/quiescence witness, the
age-to-wave-count bound, and the complete physical bill including
routing, reset, holding and controller export. Those establish
\(W_{\min}(t)\le W(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}},t)\le Ct^{d_G}\) in the original
permitted class. The shell exponent is established and is not requested
again. This is the remaining certificate for the supplied construction,
with the full \(\Theta(t^{d_G})\) target unchanged.

First compiled support modules for this construction:
\texttt{TIWASource} (transition system, frozen-frame property, no-rework
rule), \texttt{TIWAGuard} (guard installation monotonicity),
\texttt{TIWARecovery} (bounded visits and wave counts),
\texttt{TIWAWork} (per-vertex and ball-size work accounting),
\texttt{G3WorkBill} (polynomial work-bill invariance), and
\texttt{G3CompleteBill} (success-implies-bound, wave-count
nonnegativity).

\emph{Constructed (G.12).}
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s}
proves that the sweep recovers every shell and retains it against any
outer activity the TIWA fence admits, and \texttt{sweep\_visits} proves
that no vertex is reworked.
\texttt{w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d}
gives the wave count from finite propagation. \texttt{sweep\_bill}
proves recovery success on the whole region together with the complete
bill \(W(t)\le(F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)(c+r)^{d}t^{d}\), with an
age-independent coefficient. With §21.6 this gives
\(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\). The inputs it takes are the local
correction's success against a recovered interior, the fence, the
propagation bound and the per-wave complete charge.

\section{Part III-D --- Transducer, trajectory and semantic
constructions}\label{part-iii-d-transducer-trajectory-and-semantic-constructions}

These constructions retain their full proposed conclusions. Their source
correspondence and complete bill join the verified components, rather
than being certified by a title or signature.

\subsection{D.1 Transduced Preservation Theorem
(TPT)}\label{d.1-transduced-preservation-theorem-tpt}

The supplied transducer route uses a macroscopic dissipation factor
\(\Gamma\approx10^{14}\), an adiabatic efficiency \(\alpha\in(0,1]\),
and a boundary transducer
\(\tau:\mathcal H_{\mathrm{phys}}\to\mathcal M_{\mathrm{rel}}\). Its
target is the complete physical work ceiling \(W(t)\le Ct^{d_G}\),
preserving the protected historical record and relations.

Every exterior transducer, reset, holding and controller charge remains
in that total. A fixed multiplicative hardware overhead can enter \(C\);
deriving the complete bill still requires the actual device and
operation count. Section 21.7.5 gives the cumulative ledger implication
for the supplied sliding-window route.

\subsection{D.2 Topological entropy and trajectory
bounding}\label{d.2-topological-entropy-and-trajectory-bounding}

For the supplied trajectory count \(N(t,\varepsilon)\le Kt^{d_G}\) and
complete work correspondence \(W(t)=\beta N(t,\varepsilon)\), the
intended conclusion is \(W(t)\le\beta Kt^{d_G}\). The transducer
construction must supply that actual trajectory estimate and the work
correspondence, including every physical charge.

The pinned Mathlib already contains Bowen--Dinaburg dynamical nets and
topological entropy in
\texttt{M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y}
and \texttt{CoverEntropy}. It also proves the general Hilbert-space
mean-ergodic theorem,
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}M\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n},
for a continuous linear contraction. Missing upstream infrastructure is
not a blocker for these results. Applying them requires the actual
operator and the claimed growth/work correspondence.

\subsection{D.3 Top continuation and the constructed evaluation fixed
point}\label{d.3-top-continuation-and-the-constructed-evaluation-fixed-point}

The \texttt{Htopkeeps} record carries \texttt{eval\ ⊤\ =\ ⊤} as its
unitality field. Its extractor theorem does not by itself supply the
different continuation semantics used in
\texttt{S\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y}.

The supplied decoder interface now has a derived construction. For its
code inclusion and recovery, put
\(P=\mathrm{encode}\circ\mathrm{decode}\). Retraction gives
\(P(P(y))=P(y)\) and \(P(\mathrm{encode}(c))=\mathrm{encode}(c)\).
\texttt{D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
constructs this decoder and a non-bottom fixed-point witness from a
maintained non-bottom code record. Part III-F connects that result to
the proposed ontological capstone while retaining the actual G1 source
obligation.

\section{Part III-E --- The constructions and the physical inputs they
take}\label{part-iii-e-the-constructions-and-the-physical-inputs-they-take}

The G1--G3 constructions are compiled in G.8--G.12. Each item below
records the original requirement, what now constructs it, and the
physical inputs the compiled theorems take.

\subsection{E.1 G1 full-domain source/event/fault
certificate}\label{e.1-g1-full-domain-sourceeventfault-certificate}

Construct the actual source state, admitted commands/events, physical
projection, observations, and charged fault accounts that jointly supply
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}.
Requires: physical guard device/Hamiltonian, readout instrument,
tensor-factor isolation proof, event cover for 21 charged accounts,
conditional fault bounds. The \texttt{G1Executable} module provides the
discrete command/event/observation pipeline; \texttt{FaultCover}
provides the cover lemma; \texttt{PhysicalGuard} provides algebraic
Kronecker factorization.

\emph{Compiled held-source components:} \texttt{G1Held} and
\texttt{G1E9} (G.10) prove their five-component certificate and event
cover on the normalised held source. \emph{Physical construction
target:} the actual installation projection/transition, observations,
timely correction and device enforcing removal of coupling after alarm,
with its complete charge and named fault bounds.

\subsection{E.2 G2 whole-outcome
realisability}\label{e.2-g2-whole-outcome-realisability}

Construct
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}⊤\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}:\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}∀\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{})\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}∈\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}R}
for the full order, including the continuation and freedom stages. Keep
its realisability separate from \texttt{hsolv}, the funded-cost
certificate. The supplied singleton route uses actual singleton-keeping
policies and union-closed realisability; \texttt{TopRealisability}
provides its finite induction and \texttt{G2Singleton} supplies set
coverage.

\emph{Compiled components:} \texttt{G2Splice} and \texttt{G2Held}
establish their stated local-product and grade-model results.
\emph{Physical construction target:} identify the actual full
policy/outcome family and R17 delivery on the same execution.
\texttt{hsolv} pays the complete cost and does not replace this
realisability witness.

\subsection{E.3 G3 TIWA success and complete work
bill}\label{e.3-g3-tiwa-success-and-complete-work-bill}

Requires: physical guard installation, decoder success, age→wave-count
bound \(N(t) \le at+b\), and full charging (routing, reset, holding,
controller export, minimal-hold drain). The \texttt{TIWASource},
\texttt{TIWAGuard}, \texttt{TIWARecovery}, \texttt{TIWAWork} modules
provide abstract scaffolding. \texttt{SnuffyLocal} provides a polynomial
bound surrogate.

\emph{Now constructed:} \texttt{G3Sweep} (G.12) proves recovery success
with no rework against fenced outer activity, the wave count from finite
propagation, and the complete bill \(O(t^{d})\) with an age-independent
coefficient. \emph{Physical inputs:} the local correction against a
recovered interior, the fence, the propagation bound and the per-wave
complete charge.

The new \texttt{G3CompleteBill} lemmas now derive the cumulative bill
and its age conversion from complete local charges and the metered
schedule; they do not take the desired cumulative bound as an input.
Section 21.7.5 joins that derivation to the supplied sliding window and
keeps the remaining physical boundary encoding, complete charge equality
and carrier/schedule correspondence explicit. Part III-G now constructs
a finite coordinate encoder and adjoint decoder, transports the full
represented historical algebra and work-shifted flow, and derives its
finite dual kernel. The source must instantiate their capacity
assignment on the actual historical record and meter its physical
retirement/handoff, retained memory and query operations in that
complete bill.

\subsection{E.4 Existing analytic support and its source
correspondence}\label{e.4-existing-analytic-support-and-its-source-correspondence}

The general contraction mean-ergodic theorem and topological entropy are
present in the pinned Mathlib, with the names and files listed in Part
III-D. The existing \texttt{MeanErgodic} and \texttt{MeanErgodicDeep}
modules prove their stated identity-map cases; those filenames do not
certify an application of the general theorem to an unspecified physical
operator.

\texttt{G\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
proves commuting Kronecker factors. The source construction must connect
the actual dynamics to the required factors and preserved record. A von
Neumann algebra development is not established as a prerequisite for
this finite construction.

\section{Part III-F --- Unified architecture and the self-grounding
capstone}\label{part-iii-f-unified-architecture-and-the-self-grounding-capstone}

This section integrates the author's supplied unified ledger and
homological proposal into the full edition. Parts I--III and their
maximal domain, outcome order and rival quantifiers remain in force. The
original submissions are preserved with the integration record.

\subsection{F.1 The unified gate and work
ledger}\label{f.1-the-unified-gate-and-work-ledger}

The guard is an idempotent, monotone projection with the intended
fault-nullification action. The transaction emits
\((h_{\mathrm{pre}},h_{\mathrm{post}},w,a,c)\) and enforces
\(a=1\Rightarrow(c=1\land w>0)\). The complete work functional is
additive over executed traces and has the full target
\(\mathcal W(T)\le Ct^{d_G}\).

The transaction invariant supports the source implementation of G2; its
connection to the whole greatest outcome, including all continuation and
freedom stages, remains explicit. The work derivation in 21.7.5 now
supplies \texttt{G3\_ValidWorkBill} from its complete local ledger.
Guard operation, transduction, boundary encoding, retirement and
preserved history belong to that same charged execution.

\subsection{F.2 Historical boundary encoding and
recovery}\label{f.2-historical-boundary-encoding-and-recovery}

Part III-G supplies the explicit operator/coordinate encoder, adjoint
recovery and historical query transport developed from the later
three-pillar submission. The topological route below retains its
faithful source correspondence.

The supplied history is a causal labelled graph
\(\mathcal H=(V,E,\phi)\). Its full preservation target is

\[
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{bnd}}(\mathcal E_{\mathrm{bnd}}(\mathcal H),v)=\phi(v)
\]

at the original fidelity standard, together with preservation of the
relevant relations. Within its proved radius, the finite-code decoder
returns the exact original codeword. The boundary encoding separately
specifies which full historical record that codeword represents.

The supplied accumulator route retains the proposed representation
\((\partial\mathcal H,\operatorname{Acc}(V),\bigoplus_{e\in E}\psi(e))\)
and capacity target

\[
|\mathcal B_{\mathrm{boundary}}(t)|\le K\log\operatorname{Vol}(U(t)).
\]

Its capacity certificate must count the physical information in the
accumulator, payload, query witnesses and retained recovery data. An
accumulator authenticates supplied data and membership; an exact
reconstruction additionally supplies the data needed to return
\(\phi(v)\).

The supplied retirement charge is

\[
W_{\mathrm{retire}}
=c_{\mathrm{acc}}|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|
+c_{\mathrm{bound}}|E_{\mathrm{retire}}|\log|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|.
\]

For the protocol's fixed-radius local retirement patches, bounded degree
gives \(|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|\le M_0\) and
\(|E_{\mathrm{retire}}|\le D_{\max}|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|\). Thus this
local term is at most
\((c_{\mathrm{acc}}+c_{\mathrm{bound}}D_{\max}\log M_0)|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|\),
a constant charge per retired vertex. The settled bounded-overlap count
absorbs it into \(O(t^{d_G})\). Global accumulator updates, witness
generation, routing, reset and continued storage still enter the
complete local or separately metered charge.

The proposed topological route represents history by a chain complex and
uses

\[
0\longrightarrow C_*(\partial U)\xrightarrow{i}C_*(K)
\longrightarrow C_*(K,\partial U)\longrightarrow0.
\]

Its long exact sequence contains

\[
H_n(\partial U)\xrightarrow{i_*}H_n(K)\xrightarrow{j_*}
H_n(K,\partial U)\xrightarrow{\delta_n}H_{n-1}(\partial U).
\]

The supplied route is implemented by constructing a boundary chain map
\(\rho:C_*(K)\to C_*(\partial U)\), inclusion \(i\), and chain homotopy

\[
i\rho-\mathrm{id}=dh+hd.
\]

For a cycle \(z\), the recovered chain satisfies \(i\rho z-z=d(hz)\), so
its homology class is preserved. A protected semantic observable \(q\)
that annihilates these exact terms then obeys \(q(i\rho z)=q(z)\). The
historical encoder must establish this preservation for the actual full
query family and its physical records, including the \(\phi(v)\)
evaluations, rather than identifying every history with its unlabelled
homology class.

Exactness identifies images and kernels. If \(H_n(K,\partial U)=0\), the
displayed connecting map has zero domain; this alone does not make it an
injective encoding of arbitrary histories. The concrete chain
contraction and faithful semantic representation supply the intended
repair. Their boundary capacity and charged retirement/handoff map join
the same source and work certificate.

\subsection{F.3 The Axiom of Internal
Self-Grounding}\label{f.3-the-axiom-of-internal-self-grounding}

The author proposes the following ontological capstone:

\[
\exists e\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{rel}},\qquad
\mathcal E(e)=e,\qquad \tau(e)\ne\bot.
\]

Autopoiesis is characterised by this nontrivial persistent evaluation
witness. The new axiom statement is recorded as the author's proposed
foundation; no additional axiom is introduced into the Lean canon.

There is a derived finite-code witness for the displayed property. Use
the constructed decoder, let

\[
\mathcal E=\tau=\mathrm{encode}\circ\mathrm{decode},
\qquad e=\mathrm{encode}(c),
\]

where \(c\) is a maintained non-bottom record. Retraction gives

\[
\mathcal E(e)=e,\qquad \tau(e)=e\ne\bot.
\]

\texttt{D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
proves this construction with the same finite-code decoding guarantee.
Its physical record, permitted disturbances and funded implementation
are the existing G1/G3 construction links. This establishes the
fixed-point component without adding the desired conclusion as a Lean
axiom.

The proposed mutual-bootstrap statement is retained in full:

\[
\operatorname{Vol}(U)>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad H_*(\partial U)\ne0.
\]

It is proved for regions
(\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p}).
Take the relational structure to be a connected graph on finitely many
particulars, with relations as edges (P1: one structure). A region's
volume is its number of particulars. Its boundary is the set of its
particulars related to a particular outside it. \(H_0\) of the boundary
is the free ℤ-module on the boundary's connected components. For every
region other than the whole structure,
\(\operatorname{Vol}(U)>0\Leftrightarrow H_0(\partial U)\ne0\). The
forward direction holds because a nonempty proper region in a connected
structure has a relation crossing out of it (\texttt{exists\_crossing}).
The whole structure has no outside (P1), so its boundary and boundary
homology vanish while its volume is positive
(\texttt{w\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y},
\texttt{whole\_H0\_trivial}). The bootstrap is therefore the statement
about regions, as used for retiring patches in F.2.

\subsection{F.4 Funding, persistence and lifetime
error}\label{f.4-funding-persistence-and-lifetime-error}

The work ceiling bounds demand. The existing escrow certificate supplies
funding: \(E(0)+\int_0^t\mathrm{Income}-\mathcal Q(t)\ge0\) at every
admitted operation, including within-cycle obligations. Together with
the decoder radius, timely controller and initial continuation witness,
the holding theorem supplies persistence. When the maintained record's
distance from null exceeds the continuation error,
\texttt{n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
gives a nonzero witness.

For per-epoch failure bounds \(\sigma_k\), the probability of any
failure through epoch \(n\) is bounded by the prefix sum
\(\sum_{k\le n}\sigma_k\). The bound on a future failure from \(n\)
onward is the tail \(\sum_{k\ge n}\sigma_k\). If the actual code/noise
schedule makes this series summable, the tail tends to zero; the
all-time failure probability remains bounded by the full sum. The
existing declared fault standard is retained.

This completes the unified presentation of the supplied gates, bill
derivation, decoder, historical-boundary route and self-grounding
capstone. The full source, capacity and physical work constructions are
G.8--G.12; the axiom wording is not used as a replacement for their
derivation.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Part III-G --- The boundary decoder, complete history and
work-shifted
flow}\label{part-iii-g-the-boundary-decoder-complete-history-and-work-shifted-flow}

The three supplied pillars are joined here by a constructed encoder, its
adjoint decoder, transport of the full historical operator algebra, a
derived smearing kernel and the complete work ledger. These are
components of the same all-particular protection construction in Parts
I--II. The maximal outcome, rival domain, continuation, freedom,
historical fidelity and charged fault standard remain fixed.

\subsection{G.1 From the source flux to an encoder and
decoder}\label{g.1-from-the-source-flux-to-an-encoder-and-decoder}

Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{hist}}\) contain the protected historical
registers, their labels and relational observables at the original
fidelity standard. On a local finite physical code, let
\(E:\mathcal C_{\mathrm{hist}}\to\mathcal H_\partial\) be complex linear
and preserve the full symplectic form:

\[
\operatorname{Im}\langle Ex,Ey\rangle
=\operatorname{Im}\langle x,y\rangle\qquad\text{for every }x,y.
\]

Applying this identity to \(x,iy\) also preserves the real part. Hence

\[
\langle Ex,Ey\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle,\qquad
E^\dagger E=I,\qquad D=E^\dagger,\qquad DE=I.
\]

\texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}x},
\texttt{boundaryIsometry},
\texttt{a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y}
and
\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}x}
implement these steps. The source flux identity is an identity for the
whole encoded phase space. Its derivation must use the actual
source-to-boundary map and boundary observations; the scalar Gauss
charge balance supplies its charge component.

There is also an explicit finite encoder. For an injection
\(j:\{1,\ldots,m\}\hookrightarrow\{1,\ldots,n\}\), assign

\[
(E_jx)_{j(a)}=x_a,\qquad (E_jx)_b=0\quad(b\notin\operatorname{im}j).
\]

Its adjoint reads those same coordinates.
\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}
constructs this map;
\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p}
proves its exact inverse on the code.
\texttt{b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y}
proves that every such linear isometry requires \(m\le n\). These
numbers are Hilbert-space dimensions: an \(n=2^b\) boundary register
contains \(b\) qubits. At every retirement, the source assignment must
include all protected history coordinates and the records supporting
their relations, and charge their storage and movement.

For a density operator \(\rho\) on the code, the encoded state is
\(E\rho E^\dagger\), and

\[
D(E\rho E^\dagger)D^\dagger=\rho.
\]

The same calculation holds with \(E\otimes I_R\) for every reference
register \(R\); it therefore retains reference correlations. The adjoint
is the decoder on the encoded range. A channel on all boundary inputs
can be completed, for a fixed code state \(\rho_0\), by

\[
\mathcal D(\sigma)=E^\dagger\sigma E+
 \operatorname{Tr}((I-EE^\dagger)\sigma)\rho_0.
\]

Both summands are completely positive, their traces sum to
\(\operatorname{Tr}\sigma\), and the second vanishes on encoded inputs.
The exact recovery, including with any reference register, trace
preservation and positivity of this channel are compiled
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s},
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{ext\_trace}, \texttt{ext\_posSemidef}; G.13); complete
positivity is compiled: for every finite reference register the
amplified decoder maps positive semidefinite inputs to positive
semidefinite outputs
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e}).
The existing finite-code correction theorem supplies the noisy-radius
guarantee when the physical perturbation satisfies that certificate.

\subsection{G.2 Charge and the complete historical
algebra}\label{g.2-charge-and-the-complete-historical-algebra}

Define the boundary representative of every code observable by

\[
\alpha(A)=EAE^\dagger,\qquad P=EE^\dagger.
\]

Then \(\alpha(A)Ex=EAx\), \(\alpha(A)\alpha(B)=\alpha(AB)\), and

\[
\langle Ex,\alpha(A)Ex\rangle=\langle x,Ax\rangle.
\]

For an ordered word \(A_1\cdots A_r\), the same identity holds without
exchanging its factors.
\texttt{b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
and
\texttt{b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l}
prove these statements. Thus the decoder preserves the historical
queries and their ordered relations represented on the code. The
identity of this represented algebra is \(P\).

In particular,

\[
Q_\partial E=EQ_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad
[\alpha(A),\alpha(Q_{\mathrm{src}})]=\alpha([A,Q_{\mathrm{src}}]).
\]

\texttt{b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}
proves the commutator transport. An ambient physical boundary charge may
have an additional action on the orthogonal complement; the displayed
source correspondence concerns the encoded range. Connecting that
representative to the actual gauge field and readout remains part of the
source construction.

For history words \(W_i\), use the genuine Gram kernel

\[
G_{ij}=\langle W_i x,W_j x\rangle.
\]

It is positive semidefinite by its squared-norm quadratic form. Its
encoded value is identical, and it is nonsingular when the history
vectors \(W_i x\) are linearly independent.
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}
and
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}
prove the latter two conclusions using Mathlib's Gram results. Time
ordering is retained inside each \(W_i\). A time-ordered two-point Green
function requires its own analytic properties; the squared-norm proof
applies to the displayed Gram kernel.

\subsection{G.3 The effective modular state and its transported
flow}\label{g.3-the-effective-modular-state-and-its-transported-flow}

Work on the support of a faithful finite-code state \(\rho\), and put

\[
K=-\log\rho,\qquad G=K+\beta H_W,\qquad
\widetilde\rho=\frac{e^{-G}}{\operatorname{Tr}e^{-G}}.
\]

For self-adjoint \(H_W\), \(G\) is self-adjoint and \(U_s=e^{isG}\) is
unitary. The additive normalisation in
\(-\log\widetilde\rho=G+\log Z\,I\) cancels in conjugation. With the
sign convention in the supplied proposal,

\[
\widetilde\sigma_s(A)=U_sAU_s^\dagger
=\widetilde\rho^{-is}A\widetilde\rho^{is}.
\]

This identifies the state whose modular flow is being used.
\(K+\beta H_W\) is an effective generator for that changed state; a
relation to the original state's flow requires its additional
perturbation/cocycle correspondence.

Represent the generator on the boundary code by
\(G_\partial=\alpha(K)+\beta\alpha(H_W)\). An arbitrary self-adjoint
block on \(P^\perp\) gives an ambient block-diagonal extension. Since
\(G_\partial E=EG\), induction gives \(G_\partial^rE=EG^r\). The
norm-convergent exponential series yields

\[
e^{zG_\partial}Ex=E e^{zG}x.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\widetilde\sigma_s^\partial(\alpha(A))Ex
=E\widetilde\sigma_s(A)x.
\]

\texttt{p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}
and
\texttt{w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e}
prove this transport for bounded operators, including the sum
\(K+\beta H_W\). The proof uses the exponential of that sum directly and
needs no commutation between its two terms. Finite local codes supply
bounded operators. A continuum application must separately supply
domains, self-adjointness and the source/observable correspondence for
its unbounded generators.

\subsection{G.4 Construct the smearing
weight}\label{g.4-construct-the-smearing-weight}

Let \(B_1,\ldots,B_M\) be a complete basis of the local historical
code's operator algebra, with dual coordinates \(b^j\). For
\(A=\Phi_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\tau)\), define

\[
c_j(\tau,s)=b^j\!\left(\widetilde\sigma_{-s}(A)\right).
\]

Expanding the inverse-flowed operator and applying
\(\widetilde\sigma_s\) gives, at every \(s\),

\[
A=\sum_{j=1}^M c_j(\tau,s)\widetilde\sigma_s(B_j).
\]

\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p}
and
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l}
prove this exact finite-basis identity. It constructs the dual
coefficients from a complete operator family.

Choose an integrable compactly supported weight \(w\) with
\(\int_{\mathbb{R}} w(s)\,ds=1\). Unitarity bounds the flowed operators,
finite-dimensional coordinate functionals are bounded, and there are
finitely many basis terms. The Bochner integral therefore exists in
operator norm and

\[
\alpha(A)=\int_{\mathbb R}ds\,w(s)
 \sum_j c_j(\tau,s)\widetilde\sigma_s^\partial(\alpha(B_j)).
\]

For the proposal's temporal form, partition \([0,\tau]\), for
\(\tau>0\), into \(M\) intervals \(I_j\) of positive lengths \(\ell_j\).
A boundary probe schedule representing this complete family uses

\[
\mathcal O_\partial(t')=\alpha(B_j),\qquad
f(\tau,s,t')=\frac{w(s)c_j(\tau,s)}{\ell_j}
\quad(t'\in I_j).
\]

Integration over each interval returns its basis term. Thus

\[
\alpha(\Phi_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\tau))
=\int_{\mathbb R}ds\int_0^\tau dt'\,
 f(\tau,s,t')e^{isG_\partial}\mathcal O_\partial(t')e^{-isG_\partial}.
\]

Pulling back by \(E^\dagger(\,\cdot\,)E\) gives the original bulk
historical operator. At \(\tau=0\), use the finite-basis formula
directly. The integral step is compiled
(\texttt{S\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n},
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n};
G.13), as are the exact finite-basis reconstruction and exponential
transport; the probe-schedule partition is the displayed finite
rearrangement.

For an independently specified continuous probe family, construct its
analysis/synthesis frame operator \(\mathsf S\). On the full represented
historical operator space, a lower frame bound \(aI\le\mathsf S\),
\(a>0\), supplies the dual \(\mathsf S^{-1}\) and inverse bound \(1/a\).
This is the quantitative observability connection needed to derive that
family's smearing weight. The finite complete basis above supplies a
concrete local route. A local Fisher metric by itself does not establish
the required global frame correspondence.

This exact local reconstruction uses finite dual coefficients and a
normalized compactly supported average. A Tikhonov inverse with positive
parameter instead has mode response \(\lambda^2/(\lambda^2+\epsilon)\);
its residual error must enter the original fidelity allowance whenever
that implementation is used. Its spectral bounds and parameter/work
relation must be derived from the actual implementation. The
finite-basis derivation does not require infinite work or a conjectured
modular spectral gap.

\subsection{G.5 The complete work algebra and capacity
account}\label{g.5-the-complete-work-algebra-and-capacity-account}

Use the complete decoder, memory, controller and reservoir ledger. At
fixed Hamiltonian with Gibbs reference \(\gamma_\beta\), write
dimensionless relative free energy as \(D=-S+\beta U+\log Z\). The first
law and entropy production are

\[
U_2-U_1=W_{\mathrm{on}}-Q_B,\qquad
\Sigma=S_2-S_1+\beta Q_B\ge0.
\]

They give the exact identity and lower bound

\[
\beta W_{\mathrm{on}}=D_2-D_1+\Sigma,\qquad
W_{\mathrm{on}}\ge\frac{D_2-D_1}{\beta}.
\]

\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
derives the identity from the existing
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y};
\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}
proves the bound.
\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}
identifies equality with \(\Sigma=0\) in this ledger. Historical energy
belongs in \(U_1,U_2\); any separately charged historical term must
denote a component not already counted there. The Gibbs reference in
this calculation is distinct from the protected constrained reference
\(\tau\) in the preservation functional.

The existing quantum Landauer theorem supplies the entropy-export cost
of actual erasure/reset under its bath and coupling assumptions. Reads
and reversible inversions are charged by their implemented operations
and full thermodynamic account. Joint unitarity supplies a model for
both reversible and dissipative processes; entropy production determines
saturation of the work floor.

For the prepared decoder state, the existing quantum Gibbs bound retains
its partition term:

\[
S(\rho)\le \beta\operatorname{Tr}(\rho H_W)+\log Z.
\]

\texttt{f\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d}
joins this bound to an actual energy allocation
\(\operatorname{Tr}(\rho H_W)\le E_0+\mathrm{budget}\). Entropy is
measured per state; a channel rate must be multiplied by its operating
duration before comparison with a total information demand. The accepted
data-processing/Fano account still applies to the complete decoder
input. Energy can power transmission, storage or a different protocol;
reconstruction also needs the historical information carried by those
inputs.

The work floor and the complete \(O(t^{d_G})\) ceiling are joined to the
same execution. The ceiling remains the gross bill of Section 21.7.5:
encoding, historical retention, boundary handoff, routing, guard,
controller, reset and minimal hold are charged. \texttt{G3CompleteBill}
supplies the cumulative and age-conversion derivations.
\texttt{f\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}
supplies the escrow certificate when local income covers that complete
demand. Prefix solvency, including within-cycle charges, is required for
the actual schedule.

The operator-basis formula proves a reconstruction identity. Executing
every basis query as tomography is a separate workload. The physical
decoder's circuit, query schedule and retirement operations must meet
the existing complete work bill; no uncharged dense tomography is added
to the wavefront construction.

\subsection{G.6 A charged exchange with an accounted
reservoir}\label{g.6-a-charged-exchange-with-an-accounted-reservoir}

On the basis \(|00\rangle,|01\rangle,|10\rangle,|11\rangle\), take

\[
Q_\partial=\operatorname{diag}(0,0,1,1),\quad
Q_R=\operatorname{diag}(0,1,0,1),\quad
H_{\mathrm{ex}}=|01\rangle\langle10|+|10\rangle\langle01|.
\]

Then

\[
[H_{\mathrm{ex}},Q_\partial+Q_R]=0,\qquad
[Q_\partial,H_{\mathrm{ex}}]\ne0.
\]

\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n},
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e}
and
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y}
prove these identities for the explicit matrices. The unitary exchange
gate \(S\) swaps \(|01\rangle\) and \(|10\rangle\), fixes
\(|00\rangle,|11\rangle\), and satisfies

\[
S^\dagger S=I,\quad S^\dagger Q_\partial S=Q_R,\quad
S^\dagger(Q_\partial+Q_R)S=Q_\partial+Q_R.
\]

\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y},
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e}
and
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l}
prove these identities. This gives a concrete local exchange with its
reservoir charge included.

For the full Hamiltonian, the Heisenberg convention is

\[
\frac{d}{dt}\langle Q\rangle
=\frac{i}{\hbar}\langle[H,Q]\rangle
=\frac{1}{i\hbar}\langle[Q,H]\rangle.
\]

Thus a constructed \(H_{\mathrm{tot}}\) commuting with the
bulk-boundary-reservoir total charge gives its conservation. The
continuum action must supply the corresponding gauge symmetry and
surface current. Scalar Lagrange multipliers enforce equality
constraints of a specified action; work inequalities require the
corresponding admissibility or complementary-slackness treatment. Their
insertion alone does not construct the historical encoder or prove its
work bill.

\subsection{G.7 Unified proposition and its source
correspondence}\label{g.7-unified-proposition-and-its-source-correspondence}

For the constructed code encoder, adjoint decoder, represented
historical algebra, effective generator and dual operator family, the
following conclusions now join:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  The encoder/decoder round trip preserves the full represented state
  and its historical queries; reference correlations obey the same round
  trip.
\item
  Source charge and all ordered historical operator relations transport
  to the boundary code.
\item
  The effective work-shifted flow intertwines with that transport, and
  the derived finite dual kernel reconstructs every represented
  historical operator.
\item
  The same physical execution is subject to the thermodynamic floor,
  complete wavefront ceiling, funding certificate and charged fault
  standard.
\end{enumerate}

The first three operator components have the native results named above,
and the channel and integral steps are compiled
(\texttt{ChannelExtension}, \texttt{SmearingIntegral}; G.13). The work
and exchange components have the native ledger and matrix proofs.
Application to the full protection theorem joins these maps to the
full-domain source (G.10), the complete historical capacity and physical
handoff (G.8), and the whole continuation/freedom outcome (G.11). Part
III-E records these constructions and their physical inputs; this
component theorem does not replace the maximal conclusion with an
isometry or work premise.

The research precedents have their stated domains:
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1512.06431}{JLMS} relates relative entropy
and modular flow in holographic entanglement wedges;
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.05464}{Faulkner--Lewkowycz} constructs
modular reconstruction using the relevant bulk flow. Their results
motivate the operator route; the local encoder and dual-kernel proofs
above supply this edition's explicit algebraic components.
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1306.4352}{Reeb--Wolf} gives a precise
Landauer account including finite-reservoir corrections, while
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1406.0034}{Jakšić--Pillet} treats saturation
under its quantum statistical-mechanical assumptions.

\subsection{G.8 The complete journal, whole-outcome transport, charged
handoff and the syndrome
stock}\label{g.8-the-complete-journal-whole-outcome-transport-charged-handoff-and-the-syndrome-stock}

This subsection joins the full historical record to the holding bill.
The historical record is kept in full and moved reversibly. The syndrome
and excitation stock is flushed to an accounted reservoir. The holding
charge has an age-independent envelope, and the gross ledger keeps every
exported unit. The maximal proposition, its domain and its fault
standard are unchanged.

\textbf{The complete journal.} Let the source have states \(X\),
commands \(C\), environment inputs \(E_v\) and transition
\(T:X\times C\times E_v\to X\). The journal state keeps three
independent fields: the initial state, the live state and the ordered
list of every command/environment pair. A step applies \(T\) to the live
state and appends \((c,e)\). Replay folds \(T\) over the list from the
initial state. If replay equals the live state before a step, it equals
it after the step, since folding over an appended list applies \(T\)
once more
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p}).

Use the projection \(s\mapsto s_{\mathrm{live}}\). It commutes with the
transitions by definition, and observations are read from the live
state. Proposition 5 (reactive lockstep) then gives, for \textbf{every}
reactive controller \(\mathrm{ctrl}:\mathrm{List}\,O\to C\) and every
reactive environment \(\mathrm{env}:\mathrm{List}\,O\to C\to E_v\), at
every step \(k\):

\[
s^{\mathrm{journal}}_{k,\mathrm{live}}=s_k,\qquad
\mathrm{obs}^{\mathrm{journal}}_{0..k}=\mathrm{obs}_{0..k},\qquad
\mathrm{replay}(s^{\mathrm{journal}}_k)=s^{\mathrm{journal}}_{k,\mathrm{live}}.
\]

Induction on \(k\) carries the replay invariant
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y}).
The journal therefore changes nothing the controller, environment or
protected particular can observe, and the full history determines the
live state exactly. No hash or authenticator replaces a command or an
environmental payload.

The record's size is counted from its alphabet. A transcript of horizon
\(N\) is an initial label together with \(N\) ordered
command/environment pairs. If \(|X|\le2^{b_0}\) and
\(|C\times E_v|\le2^{b}\), then

\[
|X\times(C\times E_v)^N|\le2^{\,b_0+bN}
\quad(\texttt{G1FullDomain.transcript\_capacity}).
\]

This is the full payload's capacity, not the length of a digest.

\textbf{Whole-outcome transport.} Let \(\mathrm{outcome}\) be any
function of the final state and the complete observation history. In
particular it can be the full dependent continuation-and-freedom tuple
of II.1. Lockstep gives equal outcomes for every controller and
environment
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e}).
Hence the family of outcomes realisable by some controller is identical
with and without the journal
(\texttt{j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y}).
Introducing the journal neither loses a realisable whole outcome nor
adds an unrealisable one. No affordability premise is used to infer
realisability; \texttt{hsolv} stays separate, as in II.10.

\textbf{Reversible, reference-preserving handoff.} For a bulk register
and a reserved boundary register of the same dimension \(\iota\),
together with an arbitrary reference \(R\), let \(\mathrm{swap}\) be the
coordinate permutation of \(\mathbb C^{(\iota\times\iota)\times R}\)
exchanging the two registers and fixing \(R\). It is a linear isometry
equivalence. If the boundary register holds the blank state, then any
payload \(\chi\) on \(\iota\times R\), however entangled with the
reference, is moved to the boundary and the bulk is left blank
(\texttt{D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d}).
Nothing is copied, so there is no conflict with no-cloning. The swap
preserves norm and is its own inverse
(\texttt{h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p}).
At the gate level, three parallel CNOT layers implement the bitwise
register swap, so the logical gate count is three per payload bit before
physical gate and fault charges are added
