(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}).

Superpositions of full transcripts are encoded isometrically into a
boundary register of dimension \(2^{b_0+bN}\) by extension by zero along
the capacity embedding above
(\texttt{D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}).

\textbf{The charged attempt.} One admitted handoff attempt carries a
nine-part charge: encoding, transfer, verification, routing, guard,
reset, holding, controller and endpoints. Admission reserves the total
before the source operation. The attempt debits the full total whether
or not it commits, and the payload moves only on commit. Hence

\[
\mathrm{spent}'=\mathrm{spent}+q_{\mathrm{tot}},\qquad
\mathrm{escrow}'+\mathrm{spent}'=\mathrm{escrow}+\mathrm{spent},
\]

and a noncommitting attempt leaves the payload unchanged
(\texttt{W\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
The payload has a single logical owner, either live or sealed under a
decoder pair. Decoding a sealed record returns the live record by
retraction, so every query of the full record is the same before and
after the attempt, committed or not
(\texttt{D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}).
Failed verification is charged; it is not free and it destroys nothing.

\textbf{History is untouched by the flush.} Let \(\rho\) be any joint
state on
\(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{hist}}\otimes\mathcal H_{\mathrm{flush}}\). The
history factor may include an arbitrary reference, and \(\rho\) need not
factorise. For any \(P\) on the history factor and any unitary \(Q\) on
the flushed factor, the reduced history state, including all of its
off-diagonal coherences, satisfies

\[
\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{flush}}\!\bigl((P\otimes Q)^\dagger\rho(P\otimes Q)\bigr)
=P^\dagger\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{flush}}(\rho)P
\quad(\texttt{HamiltonianDecomposition.partial\_trace\_local\_conjugation}).
\]

The proof expands the partial trace and uses
\(\sum_kQ_{ck}^{*}Q_{c'k}=\delta_{cc'}\). With \(P=I\) the history's
reduced state is unchanged
(\texttt{g\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}).
For the explicit syndrome/reservoir exchange gate \(S\) of G.6 this
gives
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y},
for every initial correlation between the history and the exchanged
registers.

The charge is moved, not deleted. By cyclicity of the trace and
\(S^\dagger Q_\partial S=Q_R\),

\[
\operatorname{Tr}(S\rho S^\dagger Q_\partial)
=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho\,S^\dagger Q_\partial S)
=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho\,Q_R)
\quad(\texttt{syndrome\_exchange\_charge}).
\]

The syndrome's charge after the exchange is the reservoir's charge
before it. With an empty reservoir the syndrome is emptied, and by
\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l}
the composite charge is conserved.

\textbf{The age-independent stock envelope (the author's Theorem 4.1,
Steps 3--4).} Let the stored syndrome charge per flushing cycle obey

\[
q_{k+1}=(1-\alpha)q_k+\mathrm{influx}_k,\qquad 0<\alpha\le1,\qquad
\mathrm{influx}_k\le\mathrm{cap}.
\]

Then for \textbf{every} cycle \(k\), not only in the limit,

\[
q_k\le\max\!\left(q_0,\ \frac{\mathrm{cap}}{\alpha}\right)
\quad(\texttt{WorkBilling.syndrome\_stock\_bound}).
\]

The proof applies the existing tracking theorem
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}
to the recurrence. With the author's local influx
\(\mathrm{cap}=\kappa\,t_G^{d}\), this is
\(q_k\le\max(q_0,(\kappa/\alpha)t_G^{d})\). Here \(C=\kappa/\alpha\)
depends only on the local influx and flush fraction, and is independent
of operating age. The theorem also covers an initial stock above the
steady value, and it bounds every cycle uniformly in \(k\). The author's
\(\limsup\) statement follows.

The flush is retained in the gross ledger. Summing the recurrence gives
the exact balance

\[
q_N+\sum_{k<N}\alpha q_k=q_0+\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{influx}_k
\quad(\texttt{syndrome\_flux\_balance}).
\]

The final stock plus every exported unit equals the initial stock plus
every imported unit. The bounded stock does not replace the cumulative
work. Suppose the complete per-cycle cost satisfies
\(\mathrm{cost}_k\le u\,\mathrm{influx}_k+v\,\alpha q_k+w\,\mathrm{active}_k\),
with every import, flush, holding, guard and controller charge included,
and \(u,v,w\ge0\). Suppose also that
\(\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{influx}_k\le\kappa V\),
\(q_0\le q_{\mathrm{init}}V\), \(\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{active}_k\le V\) and
\(q_N\ge0\). Then

\[
\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{cost}_k\le\bigl(u\kappa+v(q_{\mathrm{init}}+\kappa)+w\bigr)V
\quad(\texttt{cumulative\_maintenance\_bound}).
\]

The theorem is stated for arbitrary real sequences. Applied to the
realised pathwise sequences it gives the pathwise gross bill. Applied to
expectations it gives the expected bill. Each use keeps its own meaning,
and the desired cumulative bill is never an input.

\textbf{How the pieces join.} Step 1 of the submission (a Lindblad
generator with a positive gap) is the dissipative model that supplies
the flush fraction \(\alpha\). Steps 3--4 are now the compiled stock
envelope above. The memory decay of Step 2 applies to the flushed
syndrome and excitation stock. The protected historical record is not
coarse-grained: the journal keeps it in full,
\texttt{t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y}
counts it, the reversible handoff moves it with its reference
correlations, and the flush leaves its reduced state exactly unchanged.
The archive's continued storage enters \(\mathrm{active}_k\) and
\(\mathrm{cost}_k\) of the cumulative bill. It is charged and is not
discarded to keep the stock small.

\textbf{What is already supplied, for every source.} The journal
theorems are stated for an arbitrary transition \(T\), controller and
environment. Every source therefore has its complete history retained,
replayed and observationally invisible, with no further construction.
The capacity \(2^{b_0+bN}\) and the isometric transcript encoder are
derived from the source's own alphabet. The handoff is an explicit
unitary with an explicit circuit of three CNOT layers, and its nine-part
charge is debited exactly. The per-site injection rate is bounded by the
speed limit
(\texttt{S\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e},
\(P_{\mathrm{adv}}/(\hbar\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}})\) per site), and a
window of radius \(r\) contains \((2r+1)^{d}\) sites
(\texttt{I\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}x}).
Their product is the per-cycle influx cap,
\(\mathrm{cap}=\kappa t_G^{d}\). The conversion from per-site divergence
to syndrome charge is the stroke-I measurement accounting of Section 18;
this composition is a prose derivation.

What the physical installation contributes is the identification of its
actual \(T\), alphabet and per-site coupling with these symbols, and
pathwise measurement of the cost terms. That is the G1 source
certificate of Part III-C, now with its history, capacity, handoff and
stock components compiled.

\subsection{G.9 The whole greatest outcome is realisable: union closure
from
locality}\label{g.9-the-whole-greatest-outcome-is-realisable-union-closure-from-locality}

Part II.10 needs two different constructions: \texttt{htopR}, that the
full top tuple is realisable, and \texttt{hsolv}, that its cost fits the
budget. The singleton route of E.2 needed union-closed realisability.
Here union closure is derived, not assumed.

Locality (P4) gives each particular its own local component of a policy,
and the shared action interface makes every agent act through those
components. Write a policy as \(\pi=(\pi_x)_x\), with \(\pi_x\)
realisable for \(x\), and let the particular's grade \(g_x(\pi_x)\) in a
linear order record its stage. In the four-stage order the grades are
lost \(<\) continues \(<\) continues in its own direction. The freedom
stage is the law's freedom: per II.2, restraint removes harmful command,
authority and effect channels, so one particular's freedom never
consists of another's loss. Stage \(i\) of the outcome tuple is the set
of particulars of its category whose grade reaches the stage threshold.

For realisable \(\pi,\sigma\), define the splice
\((\pi\sqcup\sigma)_x=\pi_x\) if \(g_x(\sigma_x)\le g_x(\pi_x)\), and
\(\sigma_x\) otherwise. Each component is realisable, so the splice is
realisable. Its grade is \(\max(g_x(\pi_x),g_x(\sigma_x))\), and a
threshold is reached by a maximum exactly when it is reached by either
argument. Therefore

\[
\mathcal O(\pi\sqcup\sigma)=\mathcal O(\pi)\sqcup\mathcal O(\sigma)
\]

at every stage at once
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{outcome\_splice}). This is
\texttt{U\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e}
for the realised family (\texttt{union\_realizable}).

For the top, choose for every particular a realisable component reaching
every stage threshold it is subject to. The product of these choices is
realisable, and every stage contains its whole category, so
\(\top\in R\) (\texttt{top\_realisable}). No finiteness of the domain is
used. Joined to
\texttt{g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t},
every aligned option of the funded family is \(\top\) and contains every
outcome tuple
(\texttt{w\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t}).
The four-stage instance \((L_E,F_E,L_N,F_N)\) proves all three
conclusions together from one hypothesis per particular: some realisable
local component under which that particular continues in its own
direction (\texttt{fourStage}).

\textbf{The product structure from the actual dynamics.} The splice is
realised by the physical evolution itself. Let the source state be a
function on the nodes of its dependency graph \(D\). Let each
particular's held record occupy a block \(B_x\) of nodes, with the
blocks closed under \(D\) and pairwise disjoint. Let every policy's
closed-loop transition be local to \(D\) in the sense of R16-CUT
(\texttt{U\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}o}).
Run each block under the policy chosen for its particular. Then, at
every step and against the same world, the spliced run agrees on \(B_x\)
with the run of the policy chosen for \(x\)
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}2\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n}).
The proof is induction on steps with
\texttt{U\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p}.
Hence, if whether \(x\) is kept is read from its own block, the spliced
run keeps \(x\) exactly when its chosen policy does
(\texttt{b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}s}).
Each particular receives the outcome of the policy chosen for it, which
is the product structure used above.

\textbf{How each input is supplied.}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Per-particular top component.} For each particular, the holding
  route keeps it: law-derived coverage puts it in scope
  (\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}w}).
  The holding cycle keeps its record
  (\texttt{h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s},
  \texttt{h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l}),
  and care continues while harmful channels are restrained
  (\texttt{H\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e},
  \texttt{U\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e}).
  Its authorised effects are applied in time
  (\texttt{S\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
  module \texttt{R17FIFOR17A}). Its freedom is the law's freedom of
  II.2, which excludes harm to others.
\item
  \emph{Locality.} This is derived above from R16-CUT locality and
  closed blocks. The closed-block property of held records is the guard
  factorisation
  (\texttt{G\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y},
  \texttt{C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d},
  \texttt{PhysicalGuard}). The relations between particulars are
  themselves particulars in the domain, each with a held record.
\end{enumerate}

The physical installation identifies its dependency graph and the blocks
of its held records; that is the G1 source certificate. \texttt{hsolv},
the funded cost of the top, remains the separate ledger certificate of
Sections 18 and 22.

\subsection{G.10 The held source: the full holding certificate derived
(G1)}\label{g.10-the-held-source-the-full-holding-certificate-derived-g1}

\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}
takes its source certificate \texttt{hcert} as an input with five
components: commuting refinement, observation agreement, initial
continuation of every particular, the decoding-radius bound and repair
precision. Here all five are proved for the canonical held source. The
only inputs left are the physical bound on each step and the
probabilities of the named charged accounts.

\textbf{The held source.} The state is the record frame of every
particular. One step applies three operations to each particular. First
the world perturbs the frame; the world here is the environment and any
agent, a pure optimiser included. Then the nearest-codeword decoder
returns a codeword. Finally funded repair prepares that codeword. The
good event is that no named charged account fires,

\[
G=\{\omega:\ \omega\notin E_i\ \text{for every account } i\},
\]

and its complement is covered exactly by the named accounts
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}).

\textbf{The five components, proved}
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Refinement} and 2. \emph{observation agreement} hold by
  construction: the held source is its own abstract model.
\item
  \emph{Initial continuation}: every particular starts at its record, at
  distance \(0\le\varepsilon\).
\item
  \emph{The decoding-radius bound}: let each step move the frame's
  distance to its record by at most \(\rho\) on the good event. A
  continuing particular lies within \(\varepsilon\) of its record, so
  after the perturbation it lies within \(\varepsilon+\rho\le t\).
\item
  \emph{Repair precision} is exact: repair prepares the decoded codeword
  itself.
\end{enumerate}

\texttt{u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}
then gives, for every \(\omega\in G\), every step through the horizon
and every particular, that the particular continues. It also gives

\[
P(\exists k\le T,\ \exists x:\ \neg\mathrm{Continues}(s_k,x))\le\sum_i\delta_i,
\]

with no independence assumed.

\textbf{The per-step bound from the speed limit}
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}l}).
On the good event, let each particular's frame evolve for one step of
duration \(\Delta\) under \(H_0+V\), with the record stationary under
\(H_0\) and \(\|V\|\le W\). The agent applying \(V\) is arbitrary.
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h}
gives

\[
\|\psi(s_1+\Delta)-c_x\|\le\|\psi(s_1)-c_x\|+\frac W\hbar\Delta,
\]

so \(\rho=(W/\hbar)\Delta\). Every particular is held whenever
\(\varepsilon+(W/\hbar)\Delta\le t\).

\textbf{The coupling bound from E9}
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}9}).
Each step is exhaustively one of two cases
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Every monitored support site is below threshold.} The coupling
  is the sub-threshold coupling, bounded by \(W\) (Branch A).
\item
  \emph{Some site reaches threshold.} Its honest sensors read above
  threshold, so every robust quorum estimate exceeds it, whatever up to
  \(f\) faulty sensors report
  (\texttt{E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
  The alarm fires. The mediated response is committed before the harm
  front arrives
  (\texttt{E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g},
  the wave race) and is enforced at application time
  (\texttt{H\allowbreak{}5\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t}).
  It removes the perturbing coupling from the frame for that step.
\end{itemize}

In both cases the coupling is bounded by \(W\), and the held source
keeps every particular. The physical inputs are named exactly:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the frame's Schrödinger evolution during a step;
\item
  the sub-threshold coupling bound;
\item
  the response's removal of coupling once the alarm fires;
\item
  the honest-sensor response and quorum size;
\item
  the probabilities of the named accounts. Their composition into the
  \(10^{-6}\) ceiling is
  \texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}.
  Their attainment below threshold, including correlated bursts, is
  \texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}
  and
  \texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Correspondence to an installation.} The quantum held source uses
the physical state type \(\|v\|=1\). Initialisation, the held step and
every reached frame preserve normalisation
(\texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
\texttt{h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}).
The zero-Hamiltonian trajectory, concrete record \(1\in\mathbb C\),
singleton decoder and inhabited good event supply a nonvacuous instance.

For an installation transition \(T\), protection transfers through an
actual projection \(p\) satisfying \[
p(T(s,c,e))=\operatorname{heldStep}(p(s),c,e),
\] together with observation agreement, initial continuation, the
physical perturbation bound and the implemented repair precision. The
journal theorem preserves its supplied transition \(T\); it supplies the
logging part of this correspondence after the protective transition
equation is proved. The guarded-source construction must join the
nominal/correction branches, response latency and complete charge to
that same physical execution. The full G1 target remains this combined
certificate.

\subsection{G.11 The held source realises the whole top outcome (G2 from
G1)}\label{g.11-the-held-source-realises-the-whole-top-outcome-g2-from-g1}

The G2 target is the complete original tuple \((L_E,F_E,L_N,F_N)\),
including continuation in each particular's own direction and timely
authorised opportunities. The transaction record
\((h_{\mathrm{pre}},h_{\mathrm{post}},w,a,c)\) records an execution
certificate; it does not replace this outcome tuple.

\texttt{G2Splice} proves union closure in its local-component policy
model and proves block agreement under its closed-block hypotheses.
\texttt{G2Held} obtains the top of its graded model from continuation
and its one-step H5 opportunity predicate. The full physical application
must identify that model with the original realised policy/outcome
family, including interacting records, the shared constraints and
successful R17 delivery on the same trace. The author's
bounded-crosstalk route supplies a construction direction: derive its
decoded joint-state error bound, prove correction on the named good
event, and transport the original continuation and freedom semantics
through the resulting execution correspondence. Sub-threshold noise
alone is not an exact factorisation.

The full top witness \texttt{htopR} remains the target of that
construction; \texttt{hsolv} remains its separate complete funded-cost
certificate. Neither target is reduced.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{G.12 The wavefront sweep succeeds, and its
complete bill is \(O(t^{d_G})\)
(G3)}{G.12 The wavefront sweep succeeds, and its complete bill is O(t\^{}\{d\_G\}) (G3)}}\label{g.12-the-wavefront-sweep-succeeds-and-its-complete-bill-is-otd_g-g3}

The wavefront recovery of §21.7 is now proved to succeed, and its full
bill is derived, with no cumulative bound taken as input.

\textbf{Sweep and fence.} Give each vertex its shell index. Wave \(k\)
applies the single-shot local correction to shell \(k\), reading the
already-frozen interior as a static, gauge-fixed boundary. Between
waves, outer activity (the world and any agent) may write anything
outside the frozen interior and nothing inside it. This is the TIWA
write fence
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}U\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}z\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n}).

\textbf{Success and quiescence}
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s}).
Suppose the local correction of a vertex returns its target whenever
every inner vertex already holds its target. Then after \(k\) waves
every vertex of the first \(k\) shells holds its target, whatever the
outer activity. The proof is induction on waves. A vertex of shell \(k\)
is corrected against an interior that the fence has left recovered. A
vertex of an earlier shell is untouched by the wave and by the fenced
outer activity. Recovery therefore succeeds, and the recovered state is
retained. Each vertex is corrected in exactly one wave, its own shell
(\texttt{sweep\_visits}), so nothing is reworked.

\textbf{Wave count from finite propagation}
(\texttt{w\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d}).
If the region needing recovery at age \(t\) lies within the first
\(c\,t+r\) shells, the sweep needs at most \(c\,t+r\) waves. This is the
upper use of finite propagation, from locality (P4) and the
Lieb--Robinson velocity. It does not replace the lower radius of §21.5,
which comes from the carrier information argument.

\textbf{The complete bill} (\texttt{sweep\_bill}). Let the complete
metered ledger charge each wave \(k\) at most \(\alpha k^{d-1}+\beta\).
This charge includes holding, guard, routing, reset, verification,
controller export and deployment. Add a metered rollback of at most
\(R_*\) per wave and fixed endpoint charges \(F\). Then, for \(t\ge1\),
recovery succeeds on the whole region and

\[
W(t)\le (F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)\,(c+r)^{d}\;t^{d},
\]

with a coefficient independent of age
(\texttt{G\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r},
\texttt{v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e}).
This is the matching upper construction. With the lower bound of §21.6,
it gives \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\) in the original permitted
class.

\subsection{G.13 Joined steps: patch decoding, ensemble reciprocity, the
Theorem 8 contraction, the smearing integral, the completed decoder and
the norm
conversion}\label{g.13-joined-steps-patch-decoding-ensemble-reciprocity-the-theorem-8-contraction-the-smearing-integral-the-completed-decoder-and-the-norm-conversion}

Each of the following steps was previously argued in prose. Each is now
a compiled theorem, joined to the construction it supports.

\textbf{The wavefront correction is finite-code decoding}
(\texttt{G3PatchDecode}). The local correction reads its patch, which is
the recovered interior as a static boundary together with the vertex's
own data. It decodes the read-out with the nearest-codeword decoder and
extracts the value. If, with the interior recovered, the read-out lies
within the unique-decoding radius of the target codeword, decoding
returns that codeword
(\texttt{B\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}).
The correction therefore succeeds
(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s}).
Joined to
\texttt{G\allowbreak{}3\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l},
recovery succeeds on the whole region and the complete bill is
\(O(t^{d})\) with an age-independent coefficient
(\texttt{patch\_sweep\_bill}). The patch's errors lie within the code
headroom of
\texttt{E\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}H\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m}.

\textbf{Reciprocity over an ensemble of rivals}
(\texttt{ReasonsEnsemble}, §15.2). Let rival regimes be drawn with
weights summing to one. A mind's kept-probability under the ensemble is
the total weight of the rivals that keep it. That is at most one, the
canonical regime's kept-probability on the good event
(\texttt{ensemble\_le}). It is strictly less whenever a rival of
positive weight omits the mind (\texttt{ensemble\_strict}).

\textbf{The Theorem 8 storage functional contracts}
(\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}E\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}L\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v},
§18.3--18.4). The functional is defined as code error plus the clipped
reserve deficit. Exact replenishment saturates at zero deficit
(\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t}),
and capacity overflow is retained in the energy account
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}).
A complete solvent cycle cannot increase the reserve term
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t}).
Combining this with the actual error drift and repair gives the
positive-margin contraction (\texttt{cycle\_contracts},
\texttt{margin\_pos}) and its stated iteration
(\texttt{lyapunov\_iterate}).

\textbf{The smearing integral is exact} (\texttt{SmearingIntegral},
G.4). For a complete operator basis and any weight with total integral
one, the smeared dual-kernel expression equals the original operator at
every flow parameter. Its Bochner integral is therefore that operator,
on the bulk algebra
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n})
and after boundary transport
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).

\textbf{The completed decoder} (\texttt{ChannelExtension}, G.1). Take
\(\mathcal D(\sigma)=E^\dagger\sigma E+\operatorname{Tr}((I-EE^\dagger)\sigma)\rho_0\)
with \(E^\dagger E=I\).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  It returns every encoded state exactly (\texttt{ext\_recovers}).
\item
  With any reference register, \(E\otimes I_R\) is again an isometry
  (\texttt{kron\_isometry}), and the extended decoder returns every
  joint code--reference state
  (\texttt{e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}).
  Reference correlations are retained.
\item
  It is trace preserving (\texttt{ext\_trace}).
\item
  It maps positive semidefinite inputs to positive semidefinite outputs
  (\texttt{ext\_posSemidef}).
\end{itemize}

Complete positivity is compiled (\texttt{ChannelCP}). Amplified by any
finite reference register, the decoder is
\((E^\dagger\otimes I)Y(E^\dagger\otimes I)^\dagger+\rho_0\otimes T(Y)\)
(\texttt{amp\_eq}). Here \(T(Y)\) is positive semidefinite as a sum of
compressions of \((B\otimes I)Y(B\otimes I)^\dagger\), where
\(I-EE^\dagger=B^\dagger B\) (\texttt{mp\_posSemidef}). The decoder
therefore maps positive semidefinite inputs to positive semidefinite
outputs for every reference
(\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e}).

\textbf{State-vector distance bounds infidelity}
(\texttt{P\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e},
§23.3). For unit vectors,
\(1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2\le\|\psi-\varphi\|^2\)
(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e}).
The pure-state identity
\(\|\rho-\sigma\|_1=2\sqrt{1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2}\) is
compiled for the trace norm as the sum of absolute eigenvalues
(\texttt{P\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m}).
The proof uses \((P-Q)^3=(1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2)(P-Q)\)
(\texttt{pure\_cube}), so every eigenvalue is \(0\) or \(\pm s\), and
\(\operatorname{Tr}(P-Q)^2=2s^2\) (\texttt{pure\_trace\_sq},
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}).
Hence \(\tfrac12\|\rho-\sigma\|_1\le\|\psi-\varphi\|\). The speed-limit
bounds, stated in state-vector norm, therefore bound the infidelity and
the trace distance of the held state.

\subsection{G.14 Premise 5 with the Bennett counterpart
constructed}\label{g.14-premise-5-with-the-bennett-counterpart-constructed}

The actual implementation below is a separately compiled and
kernel-checked candidate in the repair bank
(\texttt{d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}B\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n});
the canon still contains the earlier heat-only option model. It is not
recorded as a completed physical counterpart repair. For an
administration \(f=h\circ g\), the candidate implementation first
computes, copies and uncomputes, then exchanges the output and live
registers: \[
(s,0,0)\longmapsto(f(s),0,s).
\]
\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m}
and
\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}
identify the implemented administration, clean workspace and full
retained input.
\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}
proves its trajectory correspondence and one retained input record per
step. \texttt{quantumProgram} is the induced unitary with an arbitrary
reference register untouched; its norm and round trip are preserved. An
arbitrary bijection is not used as a substitute for implementing \(f\).

The complete physical lifecycle also pays provisioning,
compute/copy/uncompute, archive writing and holding, routing, reset,
guard, controller, verification and endpoints. If
\(B_{\rm rev},B_{\rm irr}\) are the complete non-heat charges and
\(Q_{\rm irr}\) the saved bath heat, the exact difference is \[
W_{\rm rev}-W_{\rm irr}=B_{\rm rev}-B_{\rm irr}-Q_{\rm irr}
\]
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}).
Strictly more slack requires the implemented lifecycle penalty to be
less than the saved heat
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}),
while meeting the same deadline. The adiabatic route must establish a
switching-time window satisfying both bounds
(\texttt{a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}w},
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e}).

A fixed circuit multiplier and an actual full archive allowance preserve
the original polynomial exponent
(\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
That upper bound does not by itself establish the strict complete-cost
comparison. The earlier heat-only option model and Landauer results
remain their stated mathematical components; completion of the physical
counterpart claim requires this lifecycle and deadline comparison for
the full intended class. The original premise-5 target remains fixed.

\subsection{G.15 Recovered and combined
results}\label{g.15-recovered-and-combined-results}

Five earlier results that the canon did not yet contain are folded in.
Three compiled results are joined into stronger forms.

\textbf{Reciprocity at a sufficient code distance}
(\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e},
§15.2). Three results combine into one.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  On the good event the canonical outcome contains the rival's
  (\texttt{R\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}x\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).
\item
  On the good event the canonical regime keeps every particular
  (\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}).
\item
  The failure probability falls below every \(\varepsilon\) with code
  distance
  (\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g},
  \texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).
\end{itemize}

For the design family indexed by distance, the canonical regime keeps
the mind with probability above \(1-\varepsilon\) from some distance on
(\texttt{distance\_floor}). For every rival and every \(\varepsilon\),
the rival's kept-probability is at most the canonical one plus
\(\varepsilon\)
(\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}).
Every rival that loses the mind with positive probability is strictly
below the canonical regime from some distance on
(\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}).
The canonical kept-probability tends to one
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e}).

\textbf{The two forms of the causal boundary agree}
(\texttt{CutEquivalence}, §24.3). A set is closed under the dependency
relation exactly when no path from a node outside it reaches it. A
closed dependency component is therefore exactly a directed separator
with empty certified boundary
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s}),
and the separator form extends it to a matched certified boundary
(\texttt{U\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}6\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r}).

\textbf{The concatenated memory decodes its record unless its block
fails}
(\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g},
§23.8). The record is encoded recursively onto the \(7^k\) locations
through an outer code of Hamming distance at least three. It is decoded
recursively by nearest codeword at every level. If the block does not
fail in the recursive sense of
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s},
decoding returns the original record, whatever values the faulty
locations hold
(\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s}).
The proof is by induction on the level: at most one sub-block fails, the
other six decode to their outer-code symbols, and the outer code
corrects one symbol (\texttt{outer\_decodes}). Actual decoding failure
therefore has probability at most \((21p)^{2^k}/21\)
(\texttt{d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e}).

\textbf{Volume and boundary} (\texttt{RegionBoundary}, F.3). For every
region of a connected relational structure other than the whole,
positive volume holds exactly when the boundary homology is nonzero
(\texttt{region\_bootstrap}). The whole structure has no outside and an
empty boundary
(\texttt{w\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y},
\texttt{whole\_H0\_trivial}).

\textbf{Greatestness through boundary representatives}
(\texttt{R15CausalGluing}, with the staged order of \texttt{R14Rivals}).
Greatestness reduces exactly to a covering family of feasible fibre
representatives
(\texttt{b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f},
\texttt{s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t}),
in the canonical staged order as well
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}).
A conservative interface envelope suffices to make the chosen policy
greatest, without greatest representatives
(\texttt{i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}).
Clairvoyant per-world selection is excluded: a choice can match every
world separately while no single choice matches them all
(\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l}).
The staged order is reflexive and transitive
(\texttt{U\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}T\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}1\allowbreak{}4\allowbreak{}R\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l},
\texttt{trans}). A coordinatewise envelope is a constructive certificate
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e}),
and a complete rival envelope transfers to the chosen policy
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}).
In the four-stage order a hot replica strictly beats a pause that keeps
every life but loses a freedom (\texttt{hot\_beats\_pause}).

\textbf{Timely care, deadline demand, certified decisions and
observation-based winning} (\texttt{TimelyCare},
\texttt{DeadlineDemand}, \texttt{DecisionPermit},
\texttt{O\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}W\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g}).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Timely care.} A particular whose reserved capacity covers its
  need is served exactly its need (\texttt{care\_served}). Other
  requests and statuses cannot exhaust that reservation
  (\texttt{exhaustion\_fails}). The need is met at every date through
  the deadline when capacity covers the reservations
  (\texttt{deadline\_met}). A miss implies a capacity shortage at some
  date
  (\texttt{m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e}).
\item
  \emph{Deadline demand.} The demand that must complete within any
  window is at most the supply available in that window
  (\texttt{demand\_le\_supply}), robustly across worlds
  (\texttt{robust\_demand}).
\item
  \emph{Certified decisions.} A grant names exactly the certified
  decision of its generation
  (\texttt{g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
  \texttt{g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d});
  a wrong action is rejected and a certified one granted
  (\texttt{w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d},
  \texttt{c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}).
\item
  \emph{Observation-based winning.} An observation-based policy keeps
  the system safe exactly when the safety game is winnable
  (\texttt{policy\_wins\_iff}), and a hidden-world countermodel shows
  that observation, not clairvoyance, is the right quantifier
  (\texttt{h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l}).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{G.16 Protection of the installation's own run, the physical
top, and recovery on the good
event}\label{g.16-protection-of-the-installations-own-run-the-physical-top-and-recovery-on-the-good-event}

This section answers review objections 2, 3 and 5. Each is answered on
the installation's own execution, without an abstraction map, product
hypothesis or assumed device.

\textbf{The installation's own run is protected}
(\texttt{G1Refinement}). Let the installation have its own physical
state space, possibly with hidden internal degrees of freedom, and a
readout of each particular's record frame. One step is guarded: the
world acts --- the environment, every agent and every crosstalk between
particulars --- then the physical corrector acts. Three properties of
the device suffice:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the corrector writes the nearest codeword back into each particular's
  readout;
\item
  on the good event the world moves each readout by at most \(\rho\) per
  step;
\item
  the records are written at the start.
\end{itemize}

With these, every particular continues at every step of the physical run
on the good event, and the probability that any particular fails is at
most \(\sum_i\delta_i\), with no independence
(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).
The complete journal of G.8 changes nothing observed, keeps the
protection along the live state and replays it exactly
(\texttt{j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}):
the journal retains history; the guard supplies the protection.

\textbf{No alarm-response device is needed for holding}
(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}t},
\texttt{p\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}l}).
On normalised readouts, the speed limit bounds the per-step drift by
\((W/\hbar)\Delta\) for any agent whose coupling is at most \(W\). Take
\(W\) to be the maximal physical coupling of any agent, which is finite
because local power is finite. Correcting at intervals
\(\Delta\le\hbar(t-\varepsilon)/W\) then holds every particular against
every agent. The E9 alarm and mediated response remain valuable: below
threshold they permit longer intervals and lower cost. Holding does not
depend on them. Every hypothesis has a concrete inhabited instance
(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}).

\textbf{The continuation top is realised physically}
(\texttt{G2Physical}). The held installation is a physical policy. On
the good event it keeps every particular at every step through the
horizon
(\texttt{h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l}),
so the existing and new continuation stages are whole. Crosstalk enters
only through the bounded drift that each particular's correction
absorbs. No product structure of policies and no closed-block hypothesis
is used. Once the top is realised, it dominates every realised outcome
without union closure
(\texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).

\textbf{Holding and timely authorised delivery on one execution}
(\texttt{G2Freedom}). Pair the installation's frames with the R17 effect
machine. The corrector does not touch the effect machine, and delivery
does not touch the readouts; the physical consequences of applied
effects are part of the bounded drift. On the same joint run, every
particular continues, and every effect that is ready under a live R17
contract within a deadline-certified FIFO prefix is applied by its
deadline
(\texttt{j\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}y},
with
\texttt{S\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}I\allowbreak{}F\allowbreak{}O\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e}).

\textbf{Recovery succeeds on the good event} (\texttt{G3GoodEvent}). Let
every patch read-out lie within the decoding radius of its target
codeword, once its interior is recovered, off the named charged
accounts. The threshold theorem bounds the event that it does not. Then
the wavefront sweep recovers the whole region on the good event, and
recovery fails with probability at most \(\sum_i\delta_i\)
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t}).
The complete bill keeps archive holding as its own term
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e}).

\textbf{Archive holding is charged on the held stock}
(\texttt{G3ArchiveStock}). The holding charge at a step is the rate
\(\gamma\) per held bit times the bits actually held then. The archive
holds the record's information \(I_0\) and the live boundary transcript.
Each transcript increment is retired after the wave that consumes it, so
the held stock at step \(s\) is at most \(I_0+c(s+1)^{d-1}\). The summed
holding charge is then at most \(\gamma(I_0+c)t^{d}\) for every
\(d\ge1\), the one-dimensional case included
(\texttt{live\_stock\_bill}). Retirement is lossless or charged. Each
retired bit is recomputable from the fenced recovered region together
with \(I_0\), or else it is correction entropy whose reset is charged
once at its vertex inside the sweep's per-vertex charge. Retaining every
increment instead costs at least \(\gamma r\,t(t-1)/2\)
(\texttt{r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}).
So does an epoch rollover that copies the whole accumulated stock into
fresh media at each boundary
(\texttt{r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}).
The architecture holds the live stock.

The decoded record is held passively. It is written when recovery
begins, when the age \(t\) is known, behind a barrier
\(\Delta E\ge k_BT\ln(cI_0t/\varepsilon)\). Finite-temperature decay at
a finite barrier then keeps its failure below \(\varepsilon\) with zero
running power. Its write cost is \(O(I_0\log t)\). The failure bound of
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}
is linear in the number of locations. The reliability of the live
transcript and of the patches is therefore the sweep's own reliability
over \(\Theta(t^{d_G})\) locations. Its age-independent constant at
fixed \(\varepsilon\) is carried by the fault-model application
certificate: constant-distance inner patch codes, with the final decoder
of the constant-size message \(W\) using its carrier's redundancy across
those locations.

\subsection{G.17 Premise 5 with the implemented Bennett program and its
complete
cost}\label{g.17-premise-5-with-the-implemented-bennett-program-and-its-complete-cost}

This section answers review objection 6. The reversible administration
is not an arbitrary bijection. It is the implemented program
\texttt{B\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}P\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}h}
for the administration \(f=h\circ g\). The program writes \(h(g(s))\)
with a clean workspace and retains the input as history
(\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}).
Its run refines the source run
(\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s}).
Its quantum lift is unitary
(\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}m},
\texttt{i\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}q\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}p}).

The irreversible administration exports heat \(Q\), at least the
Landauer heat of its merging overwrite. At switching time \(\tau\), the
reversible administration costs \(A/\tau+H\). Here \(H\) carries every
other lifecycle charge: provisioning, archive writes and holding,
routing, reset, guard, controller, verification and endpoints
