(\texttt{LifecycleCharge},
\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}).
The deadline allows \(0<\tau\le\tau_{\max}\).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{premise5\_exact}: when \(0<Q-H\) and \(A/(Q-H)<\tau_{\max}\),
  no aligned option administers irreversibly. The implemented
  counterpart computes the same administration and meets the deadline.
  It keeps every earlier outcome and leaves strictly more resource
  slack.
\item
  \texttt{window\_exact}: when that window fails, every admissible
  reversible counterpart costs at least \(Q\).
\end{itemize}

The window is therefore exactly the condition under which cost dominance
forces premise 5
(\texttt{a\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}f}).
Outside the window, the reversible counterpart is not cheaper, so cost
dominance alone does not establish premise 5 there.

\subsection{G.18 Application data for the cited fault-tolerance
theorems}\label{g.18-application-data-for-the-cited-fault-tolerance-theorems}

This section supplies application data for the cited fault-tolerance
theorems (review objection 7). The full fault ceiling and the complete
bill \(\le Ct^{d_G}\) with an age-independent \(C\) are unchanged.
Components are compiled or computed; the remaining joins are named at
the end.

\textbf{Knill--Laflamme from the code distance}
(\texttt{KnillLaflamme}). In any complex \(*\)-algebra, the spectral
projection \((1+S)/2\) of an involution is idempotent and fixed by \(S\)
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}j\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n}).
The code projector annihilates any operator that anticommutes with a
generator
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}m}).
It returns \(cP\) for an operator acting as the scalar \(c\) on the code
space (\texttt{sandwich\_scalar}). Let errors have weight at most \(t\)
with \(2t<d\), where distance \(d\) means that every operator of weight
below \(d\) commuting with all generators acts as a scalar on the code
space. Then \(PE_i^\dagger E_jP=\alpha_{ij}P\) for every pair
(\texttt{k\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}).
Logical operators are excluded by the distance, not by the case split.

\textbf{The witness property of minimum-weight decoding}
(\texttt{MatchingWitness}). Take a minimum-weight decoder and a syndrome
additive under symmetric difference. Every zero-syndrome
\(\gamma\subseteq E\,\triangle\,C\) of the residual is then at least
half faulty: \(|\gamma|\le2|\gamma\cap E|\) (\texttt{half\_faulty},
\texttt{matching\_witness}). This is the witness hypothesis of
\texttt{T\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}s}
and
\texttt{f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}.
The nontrivial component of a failed residual is a simple cycle or a
boundary-to-boundary path, of length at least the circuit distance.

\textbf{The circuit-level detector graph.} A dependency-free Pauli-frame
enumeration covers the rotated surface-code memory over \(d\) rounds,
with X checks in a Z-shaped CNOT order and Z checks in an N-shaped
order. The faults enumerated are the 15 two-qubit Paulis after every
CNOT, \(X\), \(Y\), \(Z\) after every reset, Hadamard and idle, and
every measurement flip. For \(d=3,5,7,9\) the enumeration gives:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  every fault flips at most two detectors of each check type;
\item
  the maximum detector degree is 12;
\item
  at most 52 (location, Pauli) faults produce each edge of the relevant
  type;
\item
  the circuit distance is exactly \(d\).
\end{itemize}

With the orders swapped, so that hooks run parallel to the logical, the
distance falls to \(\lceil d/2\rceil\). The orientation is load-bearing.

The bound for every \(d\) uses a potential. Let \(\varphi\) be the face
coordinate across the logical. Boundary edges attach only at
\(\varphi=0\) and \(\varphi=d-2\), and every edge, time-like and hook
edges included, changes \(\varphi\) by at most one. So any walk joining
opposite boundaries has at least \(d\) edges. Every fault is local to
one check neighbourhood in one round, so the edge types for larger \(d\)
are translates of those enumerated. The script and its results are
banked with their hashes.

\textbf{Lattice-surgery merges.} The same enumeration was applied to the
merge. Two \(d\times d\) patches and a seam column were merged as the
\(d\times(2d+1)\) patch for \(T\) rounds, with the seam initialised and
finally split in the conjugate basis. The joint logical is found by
\(GF(2)\) elimination over the random seam checks. For \(d=3,5,7\) and
\(T\in\{d-2,d,d+2\}\), the circuit distance is exactly \(\min(d,T)\) and
every fault is graphlike. A merge therefore keeps distance \(d\) exactly
when it runs at least \(d\) rounds; with fewer, the time-like failure of
the merged-product outcome binds. The horizontal merge enumerated
measures \(\bar X\bar X\); the vertical merge, measuring
\(\bar Z\bar Z\), is the same construction rotated and was not run
separately.

\textbf{The edge law and the explicit ratio} (\texttt{EdgeLaw},
\texttt{ExRecBound}). Suppose circuit faults are local stochastic at
rate \(p\) on (location, Pauli) pairs, and at most \(c\) of them produce
each edge. Then the induced edge law is local stochastic at rate \(cp\)
(\texttt{p\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}).
With degree 12, so non-backtracking branching at most 11, and \(c=52\),
\texttt{f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}
applies with \(r=22\sqrt{52p}\). This ratio is below one exactly when
\(p<1/25168\)
(\texttt{s\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e}).

An operation outside the spacetime-volume argument has its own gadget
bound. Under local stochastic noise, if every failing pattern contains a
malignant pair or at least three faults, then
\(P(\mathrm{fail})\le|M_2|p^2+\binom{|L|}{3}p^3\)
(\texttt{exrec\_pair\_bound}).

\textbf{Reliability against age} (\texttt{AgeReliability}). A distance
\(D\ge\log(Kt^d/\varepsilon)/(-\log r)\) holds the global failure over
\(Kt^d\) locations below \(\varepsilon\) at every age
(\texttt{l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e}).
A logarithmic overhead per location, however, is not absorbed into an
age-independent constant: for every \(C\) some \(t\ge1\) has
\(Ct^d<t^d\log t\)
(\texttt{l\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}b\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d}).
The age-independent bill therefore needs the logarithm removed by
construction.

\textbf{Remaining joins.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The potential argument for merges at every \(d\), with the round index
  as the time-like potential.
\item
  A map-by-map list showing that every repeated local map of holding,
  recovery, encoding, handoff and smearing is a Clifford circuit with
  exact native gates. This removes the compilation logarithm.
\item
  Tolerance of the wavefront sweep to sparse patch failures, so that
  constant-distance inner patches suffice. This needs:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    confinement of each failure;
  \item
    no cascade through the feedforward front;
  \item
    an outer decoder of the constant-size message \(W\) using its
    carrier's redundancy, with its deviation bound.
  \end{itemize}

  This removes the reliability logarithm.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{G.19 Circuit distance for every d, sparse failure at every
age, and indefinite holding of the
record}\label{g.19-circuit-distance-for-every-d-sparse-failure-at-every-age-and-indefinite-holding-of-the-record}

This section continues review objection 7 and the holding item. The
targets are unchanged: the full fault ceiling, decoding error at most
\(\varepsilon\) on the historical record, and the complete bill at most
\(Ct^{d_G}\) with \(C\) independent of age.

\textbf{An exact cut certificate for the circuit distance.} For any set
\(S\) of detectors, the fault set
\(K_S=\{f:\mathrm{obs}(f)\oplus|\mathrm{sig}(f)\cap S|\ \text{odd}\}\)
is crossed an odd number of times by every fault set with zero syndrome
and odd observable. Over \(GF(2)\), the parity of \(|F\cap K_S|\) is
\(\mathrm{obs}(F)\) plus the syndrome summed over \(S\), which is
\(1+0\). So \(D\) sets whose fault sets are pairwise disjoint certify
that every logical error has at least \(D\) faults.

The cuts are given by explicit potentials:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{memory}: \(\varphi\) is the crossing face coordinate plus one,
  and \(D=d\);
\item
  \emph{lattice-surgery merge}: \(\varphi=\max(\tau(r),\sigma(j))\), and
  \(D=\min(d,T)\). Here \(\tau(r)=\max(1,r)\) is the time part. The
  space part \(\sigma(j)\) is \(1\) on the two seam face columns,
  \(d-1-j\) to their left and \(j-d\) to their right.
\end{itemize}

For the rotated surface code with the standard CNOT schedule, an
exhaustive single-fault enumeration shows every fault lies in at most
one cut. The cases are \(d=3,5,7,9\), the memory in both bases, and the
merge at \(T=d-2,d,d+2\): 19 of 19 cases, up to 120,544 faults. The
merge therefore keeps distance \(d\) exactly when it runs at least \(d\)
rounds. With the swapped schedule, hooks run parallel to the logical and
the distance falls to \(\lceil d/2\rceil\).

A fault's multiplicity is fixed by three things: its observable bit, the
values of \(\varphi\) at its at most two detectors, and \(D\). The
potentials are translation-covariant formulas, so the condition is local
to each fault type, and every type occurs at the enumerated sizes. That
locality step is what carries the certificate to every \(d\).

\textbf{The installation's repeated maps need no approximate
compilation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Holding correction, patch decoding, merge and split, the padding
  boundary encoder and its decoder, the charged handoff and the guard
  are exact circuits over reset, Hadamard, CNOT, idle and measurement,
  with classical control.
\item
  The modular flow is not a runtime map. The finite-basis route
  \(\alpha(A)=EAE^\dagger\) with \(D=E^\dagger\) gives the same boundary
  representative exactly.
\item
  The Bennett administration is exact reversible logic.
\end{itemize}

So no precision grows with age, and only reliability remains.

\textbf{Sparse failure at every age} (\texttt{SparseFailure},
\texttt{ToleranceSweep}). Each patch's failure is decided by the faults
in a bounded window. Patches whose windows are pairwise disjoint all
fail with probability at most \(\prod_v\binom{|W_v|}{k}p^{k|U|}\)
(\texttt{disjoint\_windows}). With a \(K\)-colouring in which
same-coloured windows are disjoint, at least \(Kn\) of \(N\) patches
fail with probability at most \(K\binom{N}{n}(\binom{w}{k}p^k)^n\)
(\texttt{many\_failures}). This falls exponentially in \(N\) once
\(\binom{w}{k}p^k<\theta/(eK)\), uniformly in age.

On the actual wavefront sweep, a vertex keeps its value after its wave
(\texttt{frozen\_after}). With interface-tolerant correction, every
wrong vertex is bad (\texttt{wrong\_subset\_bad}). Interface tolerance
means a good window decodes correctly whatever the inner commit, because
a wrong commit reaches it only as a bounded interface error, which is
the overlapping-recovery analysis of DKLP. The sweep's wrong-vertex
count obeys the same tail (\texttt{sweep\_density}). The realised bill
on that event is at most
\(\mathrm{base}\cdot N+\mathrm{repair}\cdot Kn\)
(\texttt{pathwise\_bill}). An outer decoder that tolerates fewer than
\(Kn\) wrong patches returns the record except with that probability
(\texttt{recovery\_tail}). Failures do occur, at constant density; the
theorems bound their number, and recovery tolerates them.

\textbf{Redundancy of the record.} The recovery tolerates a constant
fraction of silent local errors exactly when the record is spread
redundantly across its carrier. For an objective classical record this
is a theorem of quantum Darwinism: classical information emerges
redundantly and generically across environment fragments (Brandão, Piani
and Horodecki, 2015), and objectivity implies a spectrum-broadcast
structure (Horodecki, Korbicz and Horodecki, 2015). For a record with no
redundancy, every location matters, and no protocol of noisy gates
avoids a logarithmic factor (Dobrushin--Ortyukov; Pippenger).

\textbf{Indefinite holding at fixed \(\varepsilon\)}
(\texttt{PassiveReference}). A constant per-step failure rate gives a
total failure that grows linearly with age, so periodic renewal at fixed
size does not hold \(\varepsilon\) indefinitely. The protector's
reference record is therefore held passively on a doubling schedule:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the reference is rewritten at ages \(2^j\);
\item
  stage \(j\) has a barrier \(k_BT(2j\ln2+\ln(1/\varepsilon))\) above
  the attempt scale, so it fails with probability at most
  \(\varepsilon/2^{j+1}\).
\end{itemize}

Every age is covered by \(\log_2t+1\) stages (\texttt{stages\_cover}).
The total failure is at most \(\varepsilon\) for every number of stages
(\texttt{total\_failure\_le}). The rewrite cost is \(O(\log^2t)\)
(\texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t}),
so the long-run holding rate is the constant per-step comparison of each
particular with its reference. A faulty comparison or false repair is
transient: the intact reference corrects it at the next comparison (E9
self-healing).

This holds the objective classical record and the relations of every
particular.

\textbf{The record's redundancy is decoded by majority}
(\texttt{RedundantRecord}). Each fragment reads a record value from the
recovered state, and an intact fragment reads the record. The
strict-majority decoder then returns the record whenever fewer than half
of the fragments are wrong (\texttt{majority\_decodes}). This supplies
the tolerance hypothesis of \texttt{recovery\_tail}
(\texttt{t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r}).
For an objective record, the redundancy theorems cited above supply the
intact-fragment hypothesis, so the carrier application rests on them.
The interface hypothesis of the windowed sweep rests on DKLP's
overlapping recovery, in the same way the witness lemma does.

\textbf{Premise 5 for batched administration} (\texttt{BennettBatch}).
Administer \(b\) merges in one batch. The irreversible heat is at least
\(qb\), with \(q\ge k_BT\ln2\) per merged bit. The implemented
counterpart's complete non-heat lifecycle charge is at most \(\eta b\),
and its drive constant is bounded. If \(\eta<q\), every large enough
batch lies inside the adiabatic window for every deadline
(\texttt{batched\_window}), so no aligned option administers
irreversibly (\texttt{premise5\_batched}). The single remaining physical
input is \(\eta<q\). Adiabatic logic below \(k_BT\ln2\) per operation
meets it.

\textbf{The explicit threshold, tightened} (\texttt{WeightedEdgeLaw}).
Under circuit-level depolarizing noise, each fault carries its own rate:
\(p/15\) for each two-qubit Pauli, \(p/3\) for each single-qubit Pauli,
and \(p\) for a measurement flip. Pushed to the detector graph, the edge
rates are the weighted sums of their faults (\texttt{push\_weighted}),
and the largest edge rate is at most \(\tfrac{94}{15}p\)
(\texttt{push\_uniform}, enumerated).

Self-avoiding walk counts on the detector graph are submultiplicative.
The exact maximum over all interior vertices at length 8 is
\(c_8=89{,}270{,}654\le9.87^8\) (\texttt{walk\_bound}), so walks of
every length are at most \(c_8\,9.87^L\). The ratio of
\texttt{f\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}c}
is therefore below one exactly when
\(p<1/(4\cdot9.87^2\cdot\tfrac{94}{15})\)
(\texttt{t\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d}),
which exceeds \(4\times10^{-4}\) (\texttt{threshold\_value}). The bound
at length 9, \(c_9=763{,}504{,}745\), gives about \(4.2\times10^{-4}\).
This is a requirement on the installation's devices, of the same kind as
\(\eta<q\).

\textbf{Indefinite holding of quantum coherence.} Balasubramanian,
Davydova and Lin (arXiv:2605.10943, 2026) construct a passive memory in
\(\mathbb R^3\): 3D-local CSS stabilizer Hamiltonians on
\(n_k=e^{\Theta(k)}\) qubits, with \(O(1)\)-range terms and bounded
density. Below \(T_c\), the thermal encoding satisfies
\(\|\Phi_t\circ\mathrm{Enc}_k(\rho)-\mathrm{Enc}_k(\rho)\|_1\le t\,e^{-\Theta(n_k^\eta)}\).

Held on a doubling schedule, each stage needs a code size \(n_j\) with
\(c\,n_j^\eta\ge(2j+2)\ln2+\ln(1/\varepsilon)\), and each stage ends
with a transfer into the next code. If each stage's holding failure and
each transfer's failure are at most \(\varepsilon/2^{j+2}\), the total
failure is at most \(\varepsilon\) at every age
(\texttt{S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s}).
A cost polynomial in each stage's code size sums to a polylogarithm of
age (\texttt{stage\_cost\_le}). The transfer lands the qubit in the next
code's codespace, so its hypothesis is that a codespace-initialised
state obeys the same lifetime bound, or that its syndrome sector
thermalises within polynomial time without logical error. This is the
open initialisation problem those authors identify. The quantum branch
is proved conditional on it, with the hypothesis stated explicitly. The
classical record and its relations are held unconditionally by
\texttt{PassiveReference}.

\textbf{Remaining.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The initialisation problem above.
\item
  Measured device parameters: the fault rate \(p\), the coupling bound
  \(W\), the per-bit overhead \(\eta\), and the barrier materials and
  temperature.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Barrier heights in numbers.} For an age of
\(10^{18}\,\mathrm s\), an attempt time of \(10^{-12}\,\mathrm s\), a
base stage of \(1\,\mathrm s\) and \(\varepsilon=10^{-9}\), the largest
barrier needed is about \(132\,k_BT\), roughly \(3.4\,\mathrm{eV}\) at
\(300\,\mathrm K\). The barrier grows only logarithmically with age and
lies within the barriers of atomic defects in solids.

\textbf{The tightened threshold} (\texttt{WalkBound9}). The exact
maximum self-avoiding walk count at length 9 over all interior vertices
is \(c_9=763{,}504{,}745\le9.71^9\) (\texttt{walk\_bound\_9}). This
raises the explicit threshold of G.19 above \(4.2\times10^{-4}\)
(\texttt{threshold\_9},
\texttt{t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}9\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}e}).

\textbf{The quantum-coherence branch: the warm start and the open
problem} (\texttt{WarmStart}). Balasubramanian, Davydova and Lin prove a
passive memory in \(\mathbb R^3\) for the thermal encoding
(arXiv:2605.10943, Theorems 1.1 and 2.6, via Chesi, Loss, Bravyi and
Terhal, New J. Phys. 12, 025013, 2010). The open step is preparing an
initial state the theorem covers.

Call a prepared syndrome law \(\mu\) a warm start when \(\mu\le C\pi\)
pointwise, with \(\pi\) the thermal syndrome law. A warm start stays
dominated at every time: \(\mu K^t\le C\pi\) (\texttt{warm\_start}), and
every event has probability at most \(C\,\pi(A)\)
(\texttt{warm\_start\_event}). Tracking the protected logical
observables then gives a drift of the decoded qubit of at most
\(t\cdot\mathrm{poly}(n)\cdot\sqrt{C\,\mathrm{Tr}(P^\perp\rho_\beta)}\).
Chesi et al.'s hypotheses make \(P\mathcal L^\dagger(\tilde X)P=0\), so
only leakage from \(P\) moves \(\langle\tilde X\rangle\). A warm start
with \(C=\mathrm{poly}(n)\) therefore preserves the exponential
lifetime, with its exponent halved. This derivation is recorded with its
sources and is not compiled.

The empty syndrome is not a warm start: its constant is
\(1/\pi(\emptyset)=e^{\Theta(n)}\).

An exhaustive computation on the construction itself shows that a
fixed-range polymer route cannot supply the warm start. The construction
is built algebraically from the paper's §3.2.2, and the build verifies
\(\delta^1\delta^0=0\) and one logical qubit at every level computed.
The metachecks of level \(i\) are stretched by the scale factor \(\ell\)
at each later level. In the computed cases, all dependencies are
generated within radius 4 at level 1 and within radius 12 at level 2,
with \(\ell=3\). Hence for every fixed range a single violated check
passes all local metachecks yet is invalid.

The remaining problem is a multiscale, recursive cluster expansion of
the code's syndrome Gibbs law that treats level-\(j\) metachecks at
scale \(\ell^j\), together with a sampler built from it, yielding a warm
start with polynomial constant. This is the technique Balasubramanian,
Davydova, Lin and Thorngren apply to the classical two-dimensional model
(arXiv:2610.22548). Given it, the quantum branch closes through
\texttt{S\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}S\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}w\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}h\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}s}.
Until then, indefinite holding of full quantum coherence is proved
conditional on this one stated problem, while the objective classical
record and its relations are held unconditionally
(\texttt{PassiveReference}).

\section{Appendix --- Verification provenance of this
edition}\label{appendix-verification-provenance-of-this-edition}

The original published baseline contains 115 modules and 831 audited
names. The current tree contains 189 modules and 1133 audited public
theorem/lemma names. All 189 modules were freshly compiled and
kernel-checked in one run, and the full axiom, completeness and claims
gates were freshly run, overall PASS. The earlier receipts, including
the 924-name combined receipt that reused Beam's 136 results, are
preserved with their banks. The original failed run is preserved in the
integration bank.

The eight-module integration and its 896-statement receipt remain
preserved. This increment adds 28 checked statements in the existing
DecoderRetraction, HamiltonianDecomposition and WorkBilling modules,
integrates the three-pillar operator/history/work construction in Part
III-G, and keeps the continuous integral/channel derivations and
physical source correspondences explicit. Its exact changes and
verification evidence accompany the working draft. No published edition
or release is changed.

\textbf{The analytic bridge.} In the Canon Edition, the strong-operator
convergence of alternating projections was carried as an explicit
structure
(\texttt{V\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}C\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e})
and not claimed. In 4.1 it is discharged:
\texttt{C\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}V\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}N\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}m\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}g\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}e}
fills both limit fields in Hilbert space, so the meet algebra's analytic
bridge is a theorem.

\textbf{The Canon Edition suite.} The containerised replay of the Canon
Edition
(\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}D\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}c\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}e},
\texttt{c\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}A\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}n\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}d\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}f}),
the byte-identical replay of the four-stroke ledger
(\texttt{v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}u\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}s\allowbreak{}t\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}o\allowbreak{}k\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}p\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{} \allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}v\allowbreak{}e\allowbreak{}r\allowbreak{}i\allowbreak{}f\allowbreak{}y\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}a\allowbreak{}l\allowbreak{}l}),
